# gobby-skills: First-Class Script-ful Skill Packages (impeccable parity+)

**Plan ID:** gobby-skills-scriptful-skills

## Overview

`kind: framing`

Make the gobby-skills system work fully with script-ful skill packages like impeccable — skills that ship runnable script trees, need pinned CLI tooling, and declare runtime requirements. User rescope (2026-08-05): "the plan now is not to worry about the phase 2 website changes: it's to update the gobby-skills system to work fully with a skill system like impeccable."

Three verified system gaps drive the work: (1) bundled-skill drift refresh compares SKILL.md-derived fields only, so scripts-only edits sync as `skipped` and `skill_files` keeps stale bytes forever; (2) `get_skill` inlines an unbounded file manifest (~7–9K tokens at ~190 files, 64-char content hashes included); (3) `get_skill_files` has no path/prefix filtering. On top of the fixes, this plan vendors the full impeccable skill at upstream skill release **4.0.4** (the release channel written by `impeccable install`, currently ahead of git HEAD `a075d89b`), installs the pinned `impeccable` npm CLI as a Gobby-managed tool, and adds a `materialize_skill_scripts` MCP tool so DB-backed scripts execute from a content-addressed cache with full engine parity (parser deps + puppeteer + Chrome).

Verified upstream facts (2026-08-05): npm `impeccable@3.5.0`, bin `impeccable` → `cli/bin/cli.js`, deps css-select/css-tree/domutils/htmlparser2/fflate/marked, optional puppeteer ^25.1.0, engines node >=22.18 (local v26.5.0 OK). Generated 4.0.4 output: 39 reference files (35 top-level plus four under `degraded/`, verified against the staged release inventory) and a 107-file, 2.8MB scripts tree (largest file 492KB — under the 1MB/file skill cap; total ~3.3MB under the 10MB skill cap). Scripts are cwd-relative for project resolution (verified out-of-project), so a materialized-cache execution model works.

## Constraints

`kind: framing`

Decision Record (user-confirmed 2026-08-05):

1. Rescope to gobby-skills system support for script-ful skills; Phase-2 web styling deferred (epic #19148 dormant, its plan artifact untouched).
2. Full planning depth (artifact, validation, enhancement gate, adversarial review gate, optional build handoff).
3. Vendor the entire released scripts tree at skill release **4.0.4** (release channel via pinned CLI install staging, never git HEAD), plus a Gobby-added scripts-level `package.json`.
4. All 39 upstream reference files (35 top-level plus the four `degraded/` files) adapted or vendored in this plan.
5. Live mode is agent-driven via materialized scripts; no daemon-level port/session management.
6. puppeteer included; Chrome installs to `~/.gobby/cache/puppeteer` via the locked installation's own puppeteer executable (`node_modules/.bin/puppeteer browsers install chrome` — never plain npx, whose registry auto-install would escape the locked graph); parser deps install at materialize time; degraded line-based mode stays the offline fallback.
7. Managed CLI: npm `impeccable@3.5.0` on the SRT installer pattern, `~/.gobby/bin/impeccable` launcher, deps diagnostics; deliberately outside `MANAGED_BIN_VERSION_PINS` (that map drives GitHub-release updates, wrong for an npm-sourced tool).
8. `metadata.gobby.runtime` declaration in skill frontmatter; fail-loud parse of a malformed `cli` block; zero DB schema changes (metadata is opaque JSON, and `existing.metadata != parsed.metadata` already marks bundled rows stale on version bumps).
9. System fixes: sync file-hash drift detection; `get_skill` manifest trim; `path_prefix` filtering; `ctx.run_db` for large reads and `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` for subprocesses in the materialize tool.
10. Uninstall cleanup behind an opt-in `gobby uninstall --tools` flag.
11. Named adaptation defaults: init routes to teach mode; document generates/updates `.impeccable.md`; hooks.md documents available hook scripts while Gobby rules own edit-time enforcement; upstream `install`/`update` never manages the vendored skill.
12. A new epic roots this work (not under #19148). Task reconciliation is an explicit pre-expansion step: existing task #19679 (claim released 2026-08-06) is retitled and moved under the new epic to own and close **only** the in-flight uncommitted impeccable content diff (refreshed references, craft-floor/operate, SKILL.md sections, NOTICE.md rewrite, `.gitignore` entry, 32-file scripts subset), committed before the expansion manifest applies. The expansion-generated 2.1 leaf owns the remaining full-tree 4.0.4 vendoring and builds on that commit, so every deliverable maps to exactly one executable task identity.

Execution constraints:

- Focused pytest only, always `GOBBY_TEST_PROTECT=1`; never the full suite. Repo type gate is `uv run mypy src/`; ruff clean on touched files.
- The `bundled-content-manifest-freshness` pre-commit hook requires every commit that stages `src/gobby/install/shared/**` to regenerate `src/gobby/install/bundled_content_manifest.json` in that same commit. This binds the #19679 reconciliation commit and sections 2.1, 3.1, 3.2, and 3.3 (each carries the manifest in Targets); 4.1 verifies final parity.
- 1,000-line ceiling on hand-maintained production files. `src/gobby/cli/install.py` sits at 955 lines (recounted 2026-08-07), leaving a net budget of 44 lines — all impeccable installer/uninstaller logic lives in `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py`; `install.py` gains only flag plumbing and short calls, and if the result would reach 1,000 lines the file is decomposed in the same task rather than deferred.
- Homebrew-distribution installs skip npm-based tools today (playwright/clawhub precedent); impeccable follows the same policy — documented, not solved here.
- Dev-mode sync (daemon startup and `gobby sync` in dev checkouts) bypasses bundled-content integrity gating, so iteration on the skill tree is unblocked; non-dev `gobby sync` from a dirty checkout blocks content-type `skills` until 2.1 commits (transient, expected).
- Accepted known gaps: `restore_skill` has no source-side hook, so restoring a soft-deleted skill does not re-trigger a CLI install (`gobby install` repairs); binary assets (`.svg`, fonts) under `scripts/` are silently dropped by the loader's binary-extension filter (impeccable's tree is all `.mjs`/`.js`/`.json` — not hit); no file-count limit and per-file 1MB hard-fail semantics stay as-is.
- Out-of-scope follow-ups to mint as tasks in the new epic after expansion, not in this plan: content-scanner performance/false-positive hardening for hub-distributed script-ful skills (bundled impeccable is unaffected — the bundled path never scans); `.svg`-under-scripts loader allowlist if ever needed; `SkillContentView.tsx` file-nav grouping for 100+-file skills; daemon-integrated live-mode management (only if agent-driven live proves valuable); a separately named global cleanup operation for the shared `~/.gobby/cache/skill-scripts/` and `~/.gobby/cache/puppeteer/` trees (`gobby uninstall --tools` removes only impeccable-owned entries — see 2.3).
- Manifest guidance for the approving adversary: deliverables 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 2.2, and 2.3 are category `code` with `implementation_domain: backend` and `tdd: true`; 2.1 is `config`; 3.1 is category `code` with `implementation_domain: backend` and `tdd: false` (maintenance tooling pinned by a deterministic fixture test plus the reproduction run); 3.2 and 3.3 are `docs`; 4.1 is `test`.

## P1: gobby-skills system hardening

`kind: framing`

**Goal**: Close the three verified system gaps so a 107-file script-ful skill syncs correctly, serves a bounded manifest, and supports prefix-scoped file reads.

### 1.1 Fix bundled-skill drift detection and add runtime-metadata validation [category: code]

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `src/gobby/skills/sync.py::_handle_existing_gobby_skill`
- `src/gobby/skills/sync.py::_sync_single_skill`
- `src/gobby/skills/sync.py::_persist_skill_files`
- `src/gobby/skills/updater.py::SkillUpdater._has_changes`
- `src/gobby/skills/updater.py::SkillUpdater._apply_update`
- `src/gobby/skills/parser.py::*` — scope-reason: modifies parse_skill_text and adds the shared validate_runtime_metadata helper
- `src/gobby/storage/skills/_metadata.py::SkillMetadataMixin.create_skill`
- `src/gobby/storage/skills/_metadata.py::SkillMetadataMixin.update_skill`
- `src/gobby/storage/skills/_manager.py::*` — scope-reason: widens update_skill_with_files to the full bundled-sync field set with an atomic deleted_at clear, adds the create_skill_with_files publication seam beside it, and enforces the shared runtime validator at both
- `src/gobby/servers/routes/skills.py::create_skill`
- `src/gobby/servers/routes/skills.py::update_skill`
- `src/gobby/servers/routes/skills.py::import_skill`
- `src/gobby/servers/routes/skills.py::install_from_hub`
- `src/gobby/servers/routes/skills.py::restore_skill`
- `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/install_skill.py::install_skill`
- `src/gobby/storage/skills/_metadata.py::SkillMetadataMixin.restore`
- `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_install_skill.py::*` — scope-reason: appends initial-install file-publication and forced-failure atomicity cases to the existing install-tool suite
- `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_install_skill_scanning.py::*` — scope-reason: owns the install-tool scan gate whose per-item failure classification this section changes
- `src/gobby/cli/_skills_metadata.py::*` — scope-reason: surfaces the storage-level malformed-runtime rejection as a clean CLI error in the set and unset paths
- `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_metadata.py`
- `tests/servers/routes/test_skills_routes.py::*` — scope-reason: appends malformed-runtime rejection cases for the REST create and update routes
- `tests/storage/test_skill_sync.py::*` — scope-reason: extends the existing sync characterization suite with drift, removal, empty-set, and sole-file cases
- `tests/skills/test_parser.py::*` — scope-reason: appends new test functions for runtime-metadata validation
- `tests/skills/test_updater.py::*` — scope-reason: appends empty-inventory drift and replacement cases to the existing updater suite
- `tests/cli/test_skills_cli.py::*` — scope-reason: appends malformed-runtime rejection cases for the skills metadata CLI

**Drift fix.** `_handle_existing_gobby_skill` (sync.py lines 162–172) computes `needs_update` from SKILL.md-derived fields only (description, content, metadata, etc.). A scripts-only change to a bundled skill therefore syncs as `skipped` and the `skill_files` table keeps stale bytes forever. Port the file-hash comparison clause already implemented at `SkillUpdater._has_changes` (updater.py:373): build `{path: content_hash}` maps for the existing skill's stored files and for `parsed.loaded_files`, and include map inequality in `needs_update`. A scripts-only edit must mark the row `updated`, re-syncing `skill_files`.

**Atomic revision publication.** The persistence path currently skips `set_skill_files` when `parsed.loaded_files` is empty, so deleting a skill's **last** file can leave a stale `skill_files` row forever; and every branch commits the skill row and the file set as two separate transactions, so a crash or a concurrent reader between them observes a mixed revision — new `runtime`/`skill_release` metadata over old script bytes, exactly the pairing the materializer (2.2) would cache and execute under a stale content hash. `LocalSkillManager.update_skill_with_files` is the right seam — it already replaces metadata and the complete file set inside a single `self.db.transaction()`, and `files=[]` replaces the stored set with the empty set — but its current column list is narrower than what bundled sync writes, so adopting it as-is would silently drop fields. Its `UPDATE` covers description, content, version, license, compatibility, allowed_tools, and metadata only, while `_handle_existing_gobby_skill` also writes `always_apply`, `injection_format`, and a branch-selected `enabled`, and reaches the restore branch through a separate `storage.restore(existing.id)` call that clears `deleted_at` in its own transaction. Widen the seam by exactly that gap: optional keyword parameters for `always_apply`, `injection_format`, and `enabled` (omitted means unchanged, so `SkillUpdater`'s existing call keeps its semantics), plus an explicit `clear_deleted_at` flag folding the restore into the same transaction.

All three sync branches then publish one revision atomically:

- **Active update** routes through the widened seam with `enabled=existing.enabled` — the user's toggle is preserved as it is today, now inside the publication transaction.
- **Soft-deleted restore** calls the seam directly with `clear_deleted_at=True` and `enabled=True`, never `restore()` first; the pre-publication commit window that the separate restore call opens is the exact window this repair exists to close.
- **Create** — the no-existing-row branch of `_sync_single_skill` — has the same split today (`create_skill(...)` then `_persist_skill_files(...)`), which can leave or expose a metadata-only scriptful skill that the materializer would resolve with an empty inventory. A matching `create_skill_with_files` seam beside the update one closes it; the branch routes through that. That seam is **not** a second INSERT: `create_skill` carries a contract wider than its column list — it normalizes `source` to `"project"` whenever `project_id` is set and rejects a project row whose `source_path` is a bundled template path, derives its id as `uuid5(_NS_SKILLS, "<name>:<project|global>:<source>")` with a `uuid4` fallback on collision, translates `UniqueViolation` into the scoped `ValueError`, and emits `_notify_change("create", ...)` only after the transaction commits. A seam that reproduced the INSERT alone would publish bundled rows whose identity, scope rejection, duplicate reporting, or search-reindex notification differ from every other create path. Both public constructors therefore delegate to **one transaction-aware internal constructor** that performs the complete contract above plus the shared runtime validation and accepts an already-open connection; `create_skill` calls it with its own transaction and no files, `create_skill_with_files` calls it with the file set (empty list included) in the same transaction, and each wrapper notifies after its own commit — never inside the transaction, so a rolled-back publication emits no event.

`_persist_skill_files` is retired into these calls rather than kept as a second write step, so the N→0 transition purges every stored row and no branch publishes metadata and files separately.

**Adjacent writer parity.** The same executable bytes reach the materializer (2.2) from hub-, ZIP-, and filesystem-installed skills through `SkillUpdater`, which has the N→0 defect independently: `_has_changes` compares stored and parsed `{path: content_hash}` maps only under `if parsed.loaded_files:`, and `_apply_update` builds `skill_files` only under the same guard, passing `files=None` for an empty inventory — which the seam reads as "leave the stored set alone". An updater-managed skill that deletes its final script therefore keeps executing that script forever, and fixing only bundled sync would leave the invariant half-true. `ParsedSkill.loaded_files` is `list[LoadedSkillFile] | None`, so the two states are already distinguishable: compare the maps whenever `loaded_files is not None` (an empty parsed map against a non-empty stored map is a change), pass `files=[]` for a successfully loaded empty inventory, and reserve `files=None` for the case where no inventory was loaded at all.

**Initial-install writer parity.** `SkillUpdater` is the *update* path for those same hub-, ZIP-, and filesystem-sourced skills; their **initial** writers are three separate production entry points, and none of them publishes a complete revision today. The MCP `install_skill` tool creates the row and then calls `set_skill_files` in a second transaction — exactly the split this section closes for bundled create, on the path that installs the most script-ful skills. The REST `import_skill` (local/ZIP/GitHub) and `install_from_hub` handlers are worse: both call `create_skill(...)` and never persist `parsed.loaded_files` at all, so a script-ful skill installed through either route lands with **zero** `skill_files` rows and materializes (2.2) to a permanently empty inventory. The plan's DB-backed execution promise for hub-, ZIP-, and filesystem-installed skills cannot hold while its own install paths discard the executable bytes. Route all three through `create_skill_with_files`, carrying the same loaded-versus-unavailable distinction the updater uses: `files=[]` for a successfully loaded empty inventory, `files=None` only when no inventory was loaded at all. This introduces no new seam — three call sites move onto the constructor this section already adds. Routing them there does change which failures they can see, and one caller mis-reads them today: the REST import handler's per-item guard is `except ValueError` logging "Skipping duplicate skill", while the constructor raises `ValueError` both for a duplicate scope and for the validation failures this plan adds (a malformed `runtime` block here, an oversized file path at 1.2's write seam). An invalid inventory would therefore return HTTP 200 with `imported: 0` and a log line blaming a duplicate that does not exist. Give the duplicate case its own narrow `ValueError` subclass raised by the shared internal constructor — every existing `except ValueError` caller keeps working — and have the import handler skip only that subclass, reporting every other validation failure as a per-item structured error. That per-item error needs one stated wire contract, because the handler's response today is HTTP 200 carrying only an imported count and the stored skill rows — it has no field in which a non-imported item can appear at all, so an unspecified "structured error" leaves the classification unobservable and the route case with nothing to assert. Specify it additively. The batch keeps returning HTTP 200 whenever the source itself loaded, including a batch in which every item failed: item outcomes are per item, and a source-level failure keeps its existing error response. The imported count and the returned skill list keep their present meaning — rows actually stored. A new `errors` array accounts for every item that was not stored, each entry carrying the skill `name`, a stable `code` that is exactly `duplicate` or `validation_error`, the numeric per-item `status` that code maps to (409 and 422 respectively), and a `detail` naming the offending key for a validation failure. A duplicate is therefore reported rather than only logged, a mixed batch returns both arrays populated, and an imported count of zero is never silent: it always arrives with the errors that produced it. The MCP install tool and the hub-install route already surface their failures, so this is what makes the three unified writers agree on an invalid inventory.

**Restore aggregate atomicity.** Bundled sync's restore branch is repaired above by folding the `deleted_at` clear into the publication transaction, but the *general* restore path carries the same split and is reachable from both the MCP `restore_skill` tool and the REST `/skills/{skill_id}/restore` route. `SkillMetadataMixin.restore` clears `deleted_at` in one transaction and then calls `restore_skill_files`, which opens its own — so a materializer scheduled between them resolves an active skill row whose script rows are still soft-deleted and caches an empty or partial inventory under a digest that looks valid. Make `restore` one transaction over both updates on a single connection, emitting its change notification only after that transaction commits, exactly as the publication seams do. The REST route carries a second, independent defect: the runner-init storage wiring binds `LocalSkillManager` as the server's skill manager, and `restore_skill` exists only on the higher-level `SkillManager` wrapper those routes never traverse, so `POST /skills/{skill_id}/restore` raises `AttributeError` and returns 500 against the production binding. Point the route at the storage-level restore and assert the production manager binding in the route test, so a wrapper-only method cannot be reintroduced there.

**Runtime-metadata validation.** Skills may declare a runtime block in frontmatter, forwarded verbatim through `ParsedSkill.metadata` into the opaque JSON metadata column (the `metadata.gobby.levels` block from the brevity skill is the shipped precedent):

```yaml
metadata:
  gobby:
    runtime:
      node: ">=22.18.0"
      cli: {npm: "impeccable", version: "3.5.0", bin: "impeccable"}
      skill_release: "4.0.4"
```

In `src/gobby/skills/parser.py`, add a small fail-loud validation helper `validate_runtime_metadata` following the raise pattern of `extract_audience_config` (parser.py:321) that validates the **whole consumed surface** of the declaration, not just `cli.npm`/`cli.version`: `runtime` and `cli` must be mappings when present; `cli.npm`, `cli.version`, and `cli.bin` must be nonempty strings; `runtime.node` must use the supported constraint syntax (`>=MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`); `skill_release` must be a well-formed version string. A violation raises the parser's validation error naming the offending key. Absent `runtime` (or any absent sub-key) stays valid — `SkillValidator` otherwise accepts unknown metadata keys silently. Call the helper from `parse_skill_text` so bundled sync fails loudly on a bad declaration, **and at the storage write chokepoint**. The daemon installs `LocalSkillManager` as the server's skill manager (runner-init storage wiring, line 227), so the REST create/update routes invoke the inherited `SkillMetadataMixin.create_skill` and `SkillMetadataMixin.update_skill` directly — the higher-level `SkillManager` wrapper is a separate class those routes never traverse, and it delegates to the same storage methods, so validating at the mixin covers it too. Enforce the shared helper inside `SkillMetadataMixin.create_skill` and `SkillMetadataMixin.update_skill`: one guard covers REST, the metadata CLI (`src/gobby/cli/_skills_metadata.py` calls `storage.update_skill(skill.id, metadata=new_metadata)` directly in its set and unset paths, lines 62 and 84), and every other `LocalSkillManager` constructor site. Those two mixin methods are not the only metadata writer: `LocalSkillManager.update_skill_with_files` issues its own `UPDATE skills ... metadata = %s` outside them (it is the seam `SkillUpdater` uses for hub-sourced updates, and the seam bundled sync adopts above), so a malformed block would still be stored through it. Enforce the same helper there, before its transaction opens, so metadata and files both stay unwritten on rejection. Those methods — plus the `create_skill_with_files` seam above, which shares the internal constructor's guard — are then the complete set of metadata write chokepoints. The helper lives in `gobby.skills.parser` but must **not** be imported at module scope from `src/gobby/storage/skills/`: the `gobby.storage.skills` package initializer binds `LocalSkillManager` before it binds the models module, and a module-scope `from gobby.skills.parser import ...` inside `_metadata.py` or `_manager.py` executes the `gobby.skills` package initializer, which eagerly imports `gobby.skills.loader`, which in turn runs `from gobby.storage.skills import SkillSourceType` against the still-initializing package — an `ImportError` on the storage-first cold-import order (`_manager.py` already carries an "avoid circular import" comment on its notifier parameter for exactly this hazard). Each storage write method imports the helper **function-locally**, after package initialization; `parse_skill_text` keeps its ordinary module-scope reference. The REST create and update route handlers translate the validation error into a client-error response naming the offending key (never a 500), and the metadata CLI surfaces it as a clean CLI error. With the chokepoint guard, a malformed `runtime` block is rejected on every persistence path. Downstream behavior is thereby defined: materialization (2.2) only ever sees validated-or-absent values — an absent `node` floor means no version gate (`satisfies_floor: null`) — and because pre-existing rows may still hold never-validated metadata, the materializer additionally re-validates defensively, treating an invalid block as absent with a warning. No DB schema changes; `_handle_existing_gobby_skill` already includes `existing.metadata != parsed.metadata` in `needs_update`, so a version bump inside `runtime` marks the bundled row stale for free.

The sync cases land in the repository's existing sync characterization suite, `tests/storage/test_skill_sync.py` (no parallel new suite): cover scripts-only drift (row reports `updated`, stored file bytes refresh), unchanged skills still `skipped`, metadata-only bumps refreshing, and a remove-or-rename case — start with two stored script paths, sync a parsed tree with one path removed (or renamed), assert the result is `updated`, and verify the obsolete `skill_files` row is gone while the stored set exactly matches `parsed.loaded_files` — plus a sole-file deletion case: start with exactly one stored script, sync a parsed tree with zero files, and assert no active `skill_files` rows remain. Atomic publication is covered per branch: with the file-set write forced to fail inside the shared transaction, the create, active-update, and soft-deleted-restore branches each leave the prior state wholly intact — no new row survives a failed create, no metadata or `skill_files` row changes on a failed update, and a failed restore leaves the row still soft-deleted — and after a successful sync a reader never observes new metadata paired with pre-sync file bytes, nor a new row without its files. The field-parity cases run against the widened seam: an `always_apply` or `injection_format` change alone marks the row `updated` and lands, the active branch preserves a user-disabled `enabled` value across a template refresh, and the restore branch clears `deleted_at` and sets `enabled=True` in the same transaction that publishes the file set. Extend `tests/skills/test_updater.py` with the adjacent-writer parity cases: an updater-managed skill whose parsed inventory drops its last file reports changed and ends with zero stored file rows, a partial removal leaves exactly the parsed set, and a skill parsed with no inventory loaded at all (`loaded_files is None`) leaves its stored files untouched. Extend `tests/skills/test_parser.py` with malformed/valid/absent runtime-block cases covering every validated shape, the parent containers included (a present but non-mapping `runtime` root, `cli` shape, empty `npm`/`version`/`bin`, unsupported `node` constraint syntax, malformed `skill_release`). Storage-seam cases land in the new `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_metadata.py`: `SkillMetadataMixin.create_skill` and `SkillMetadataMixin.update_skill` reject a malformed `runtime` block through the shared helper before any row is written; and both public constructors are exercised through the shared internal one for every contract element — malformed metadata leaves zero skill rows and zero file rows, a `project_id` argument normalizes `source` to `"project"`, a bundled-template `source_path` on a project row raises, an id collision falls back to `uuid4`, a duplicate scope raises the scoped `ValueError` rather than a raw `UniqueViolation`, a successful create emits exactly one post-commit change notification, and a file-write failure rolls the skill row back with no notification emitted. Two fresh-subprocess cases pin the import boundary: a new interpreter importing `gobby.storage.skills` then `gobby.skills`, and one importing them in the reverse order, both exit 0. REST-route cases in `tests/servers/routes/test_skills_routes.py` prove POST and PUT reject it with a client error naming the offending key and persist nothing. Extend `tests/cli/test_skills_cli.py` with a metadata-CLI case proving `gobby skills metadata set` rejects a malformed `runtime` block before any storage write. The initial-install writers are proved at their own seams: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_install_skill.py` gains an exact-file-set case (a loaded inventory lands complete, a loaded-empty inventory stores nothing while an unavailable one stays distinguishable) and a forced-failure case leaving zero skill rows and zero file rows; the REST route suite gains the same pair for `import_skill` across local, ZIP, and GitHub sources and for `install_from_hub`, each asserting the installed skill's stored file set exactly matches its parsed inventory rather than being empty. The classification itself is proved at the handler whose response depends on it: a named parameterized route case runs local, ZIP, and GitHub imports and asserts that a true duplicate is skipped and logged as one, while a malformed `runtime` block and an oversized file path each produce a `validation_error` entry naming the offending key. The case asserts the whole response — status, imported count, stored skills, and the `errors` array — for an all-success, a duplicate-only, a validation-only, and a mixed batch, so a silent `imported: 0` cannot pass it. Restore is covered in both suites: a storage case proving a forced failure inside the restore transaction leaves the row still soft-deleted with its files still deleted and emits no notification, a concurrent-reader case proving no reader observes an active row over deleted script rows, and a route case invoking `POST /skills/{skill_id}/restore` against the production `LocalSkillManager` binding rather than a wrapper double.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.1.1 - A scripts-only file change in a bundled skill re-syncs the row as `updated` and refreshes `skill_files`, driven by a `{path: content_hash}` comparison. symbol: `_handle_existing_gobby_skill`.
- 1.1.2 - A malformed `metadata.gobby.runtime` declaration — a present but non-mapping `runtime` root, bad `cli` shape, empty `npm`/`version`/`bin`, unsupported `node` constraint syntax, or malformed `skill_release` — fails skill parsing with an error naming the offending key; absent blocks and sub-keys parse unchanged. symbol: `parse_skill_text`.
- 1.1.3 - Sync drift behavior is pinned by tests. test: `tests/storage/test_skill_sync.py::test_scripts_only_change_marks_updated`.
- 1.1.4 - Runtime-block validation is pinned by tests. test: `tests/skills/test_parser.py::test_malformed_runtime_cli_raises`.
- 1.1.5 - Script deletion or rename purges stale `skill_files` rows on re-sync, leaving exactly the parsed file set. test: `tests/storage/test_skill_sync.py::test_removed_script_purges_stale_rows`.
- 1.1.6 - Deleting a skill's sole script file synchronizes the empty set: no active `skill_files` rows remain after re-sync. test: `tests/storage/test_skill_sync.py::test_sole_script_deletion_purges_all_rows`.
- 1.1.7 - The shared runtime-metadata helper rejects malformed blocks at every metadata write chokepoint — `SkillMetadataMixin.create_skill`, `SkillMetadataMixin.update_skill`, `LocalSkillManager.update_skill_with_files`, whose own metadata UPDATE bypasses the mixin, and the new `LocalSkillManager.create_skill_with_files` — so no persistence surface stores an invalid declaration, and POST /skills and PUT /skills/{id} return a client error naming the offending key before persistence. test: `tests/servers/routes/test_skills_routes.py::test_rest_writes_reject_malformed_runtime`.
- 1.1.8 - The skills metadata CLI rejects a malformed `runtime` block before writing, closing the storage-direct bypass. test: `tests/cli/test_skills_cli.py::test_metadata_set_rejects_malformed_runtime`.
- 1.1.9 - Every synchronized bundled revision — create, active update, and soft-deleted restore — publishes its owned metadata and its complete file set in one transaction over the full written field set (`always_apply`, `injection_format`, the branch-selected `enabled`, and the restore's `deleted_at` clear included), so a forced mid-sync failure leaves the prior state wholly intact and no reader observes new metadata over pre-sync file bytes or a new row without its files; both public constructors delegate to one transaction-aware internal constructor, so scope normalization, bundled-template rejection, id derivation with collision fallback, duplicate translation, and the single post-commit change notification are identical whether a caller passes files or not. symbol: `_sync_single_skill`. test: `tests/storage/test_skill_sync.py::test_sync_publishes_revision_atomically`.
- 1.1.10 - The adjacent `SkillUpdater` write path enforces the same file-set replacement invariant: a loaded-but-empty parsed inventory registers as a change and clears every stored file row, while a skill parsed with no inventory loaded leaves its stored files untouched. symbol: `SkillUpdater._has_changes`. test: `tests/skills/test_updater.py::test_empty_parsed_inventory_clears_stored_files`.
- 1.1.11 - The shared runtime validator is consumed across the `gobby.skills` / `gobby.storage.skills` package boundary without a module-scope cycle: storage write methods import it function-locally, and both cold-import orders succeed in a fresh interpreter. symbol: `validate_runtime_metadata`. test: `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_metadata.py::test_runtime_validator_import_order_is_safe`.
- 1.1.12 - Every initial-install writer publishes its skill row and its complete loaded-file inventory in one transaction: the MCP install tool and the REST import and hub-install handlers all route through `create_skill_with_files`, storing `files=[]` for a loaded-empty inventory and reserving `files=None` for an inventory that was never loaded, so no install path leaves a script-ful skill with zero stored files and a forced mid-publication failure leaves no skill row and no file rows; a duplicate scope raises its own narrow `ValueError` subclass so the REST import handler skips only true duplicates and surfaces every other validation failure as a per-item error rather than a silent `imported: 0`: the batch returns HTTP 200 whenever the source loaded, the imported count and stored skills keep their meaning, and every unstored item appears in an `errors` entry carrying `name`, a stable `code` of `duplicate` or `validation_error`, its numeric per-item `status` (409 or 422), and a `detail` naming the offending key — asserted as a whole payload across local, ZIP, and GitHub imports for all-success, duplicate-only, validation-only, and mixed batches. symbol: `install_skill`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_install_skill.py::test_install_publishes_files_atomically`. test: `tests/servers/routes/test_skills_routes.py::test_import_distinguishes_duplicate_from_validation_failure`.
- 1.1.13 - Restoring a soft-deleted skill clears `deleted_at` and restores every owned `skill_files` row in one transaction, notifying only after commit, so no reader observes an active row paired with still-deleted scripts and a forced failure leaves the skill soft-deleted; and the REST restore route calls an API present on the production `LocalSkillManager` binding rather than the wrapper-only `restore_skill`. symbol: `SkillMetadataMixin.restore`. test: `tests/servers/routes/test_skills_routes.py::test_restore_route_uses_production_manager_api`.

### 1.2 Trim the get_skill manifest and add path_prefix file filtering [category: code] (depends: 1.1)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `src/gobby/storage/skills/_files.py::*` — scope-reason: extends get_skill_files with prefix and keyset parameters and adds one private filtered-set/CTE builder backing the bounded composite statements, leaving get_skill_files' public list contract unchanged
- `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/get_skill.py::*` — scope-reason: reshapes the get_skill manifest and adds the bounded get_skill_files MCP listing tool beside the get_skill_file registration this module already owns
- `src/gobby/servers/routes/skills.py::list_skill_files`
- `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_files.py::*` — scope-reason: appends path_prefix filtering tests
- `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::*` — scope-reason: appends manifest-shape tests
- `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill_serve_scan.py::*` — scope-reason: owns get_skill's serve-scan gate, which the reshaped handler and its composite read seam run through
- `tests/servers/routes/test_skills_routes.py::*` — scope-reason: appends path_prefix query-param tests

**Storage filtering.** `SkillFilesMixin.get_skill_files` supports only `file_type` filtering. Add an optional `path_prefix: str | None` parameter implemented as an escaped SQL `path LIKE %s` prefix match (escape `%`, `_`, and the escape character in the user-supplied prefix; hub-database psycopg `%s` placeholders per the repo DB convention), plus optional keyset-pagination parameters `after_path: str | None` (a strict `path > %s` continuation bound) and `limit: int | None` applied as an ordered server-side `LIMIT` — the method's existing query is an unbounded ordered SELECT, and a capped caller that slices in Python would still read every row. This is sufficient for the materialize tool (`scripts/` pulls), the bounded MCP listing tool, and UI needs. The filter surface is expressible in both directions: an optional `exclude_file_type: str | None` sits beside `file_type`, because the manifest's non-script listing selects everything that is **not** a script and a positive-only filter cannot express that without enumerating every other type (a set the classifier is free to grow). The shared predicate also keeps `get_skill_files`' existing `exclude_license: bool = True` flag, which the new signatures must not silently drop: `SkillUpdater` is a live caller passing `exclude_license=False`, so a predicate missing this dimension would change existing `get_skill`/REST listing behavior and let a page disagree with its own total whenever a LICENSE row is present. Every listing, count, summary, page, and composite signature below carries it with the legacy default. `get_skill_files`' list return shape is preserved unchanged for its existing callers. The counts and aggregates the manifest and listing tool need are **not** new public APIs: no caller in this plan wants a bare count or a bare summary, so a public `count_skill_files`/scripts-summary pair would be surface without a consumer. Instead one **private** filtered-set builder — the shared predicate (`path_prefix`, `file_type`, `exclude_file_type`, `exclude_license`, `after_path`) rendered once as a CTE — backs the two composite statements defined below, which compute the capped page, the cursor-relative remaining count, and the collapsed per-top-level-directory summary (aggregate over the first path segment under the prefix) in SQL. The manifest's collapsed summary and the listing tool's `total_files` come from those composite statements — never from materialized rows, and never from raw SQL in an MCP handler. Every response that combines them is internally consistent, and consistency here means **one statement, not one transaction**: the hub opens `conn.transaction()` at PostgreSQL's default READ COMMITTED (nothing in the storage layer overrides the isolation level), where every statement takes its own fresh snapshot, so wrapping three SELECTs in a shared transaction would not pin them to one revision. Each bounded response is therefore produced by **one composite SQL statement** — CTEs over the same filtered set returning the capped page rows, the cursor-relative remaining count, and, for the manifest, the scripts summary — issued in one `ctx.run_db` call. A concurrent skill-file replacement (1.1 publishes the whole set at once) can then only be observed wholly before or wholly after, never split across a page, a total, and a summary. One further composite seam serves `get_skill` itself. The handler resolves the skill row and then builds its file manifest, and 1.1 now publishes that row and the whole file set as a single revision — so two separate reads under READ COMMITTED can pair one revision's `runtime`/`skill_release` metadata with another revision's manifest, the same defect 2.2's materializer seam closes on the execution side. Add a composite `get_skill`-row-plus-bounded-manifest seam that resolves the row and builds both the capped non-script page and the scripts summary in **one SQL statement**, issued in one `ctx.run_db` call, so returned metadata and manifest always describe one revision. The seam takes optional `skill_id`, optional `name`, and `project_id`, and its resolving CTE reproduces the handler's current precedence exactly: `skill_id` wins when supplied and resolves a live row, an unresolvable `skill_id` falls back to `name` when one was given, and the name branch applies `get_by_name`'s project-first / global-fallback / `deleted_at IS NULL` / bundled-template-shadow rules. Resolving the row outside the composite statement to preserve that precedence would recreate the mixed-revision defect the seam exists to close, so the precedence lives in the query.

**Manifest trim.** The MCP `get_skill` response currently inlines every file's `path`, `file_type`, `size_bytes`, and a 64-char `content_hash` — ~7–9K tokens at ~190 files. Reshape the inline manifest built in `get_skill` (inline dicts only — leave `SkillFile.to_dict()` untouched so the REST surface is unchanged): non-script files list `path`, `file_type`, `size_bytes` with `content_hash` dropped — sorted by path and capped at exactly 100 entries (`MAX_MANIFEST_FILE_ENTRIES`) with `total_files` and a `remaining_file_count` overflow field, because the loader has no file-count limit and an uncapped listing recreates the unbounded manifest for reference-heavy trees; beyond the cap, discovery goes through the new bounded `get_skill_files` MCP tool (below) with `path_prefix` or the REST files route, never the manifest — and the overflow guidance carries the resolved skill id, so the continuation names the same skill the manifest described rather than re-resolving a name; `scripts/**` collapses to a summary `{total_files, total_bytes, per_top_level_dir: {name: count}, remaining_directory_count, remaining_file_count}` plus a short note pointing at `materialize_skill_scripts` for execution and `get_skill_file` for individual reads. `per_top_level_dir` is itself capped: at most 20 sorted directory entries (`MAX_MANIFEST_DIRECTORY_ENTRIES`), with the overflow reported only through the two `remaining_*` counts — otherwise a tree with one unique directory per script recreates the unbounded manifest the trim exists to prevent (the loader deliberately has no file- or directory-count limit). Agents need reference paths for `get_skill_file`; scripts are executed via the materialized cache, never fetched inline. Entry caps alone cannot bound serialized bytes, because a path is a variable-width string with no length limit in `skill_files` today: 100 entries of unbounded path width is an unbounded response. Two exact constants close that. `MAX_SKILL_FILE_PATH_BYTES = 1024` is enforced as a UTF-8 byte limit at the storage file-write seam in `_files.py` (already this section's Target), so no new row can carry a wider path; rows written before the limit existed get an explicit policy rather than an unbounded payload — an oversized legacy path is omitted from every listing and reported in its own `omitted_oversized_path_count` field with a warning, never truncated into a path `get_skill_file` cannot resolve. It is deliberately **excluded** from `total_files` and `remaining_file_count`: those two describe retrievable rows only, so an exhaustive walk always ends with `next_after_path` null and `remaining_file_count` zero. Counting an unemittable row into the remaining overflow would end the walk with a nonzero remainder the caller can never fetch and could not distinguish from truncation. One eligibility predicate governs rows, pages, totals, remaining counts, and cursors alike. `MAX_MANIFEST_RESPONSE_BYTES = 16384` is the serialized budget for the whole manifest and for each `get_skill_files` page; pagination stops on **measured encoded bytes as well as entry count**, whichever binds first. The entry that would cross the budget is left **unconsumed**, and `next_after_path` is the last *emitted* path — never the crossing one. `after_path` is a strict `path > %s` bound, so naming the crossing entry as the cursor would skip it permanently and break the exhaustive-walk guarantee this section makes. The scripts summary and the capped non-script listing are both produced by the manifest's single composite statement — the manifest build never reads the full inventory.

**Async discipline.** The current async `get_skill` handler calls synchronous storage directly on the event loop and materializes every file row to build its manifest; the rewrite must fix both defects, not inherit them. Every storage call on the request path runs through `ctx.run_db(...)` (the same discipline 2.2 mandates for the materializer), so a bounded response is bounded in its backing reads and never blocks the daemon event loop. That covers the whole handler, not only the composite query: `get_skill` also resolves a session reference, records skill usage, and persists a level variable through `SessionVariableManager` synchronously today, and those best-effort side effects run after the query on the same request path — leaving them inline would let the handler block the loop with the composite read already correct. Each moves behind `ctx.run_db` with its existing best-effort error handling intact.

This section depends on 1.1 because both edit `src/gobby/servers/routes/skills.py` and append to `tests/servers/routes/test_skills_routes.py` — shared targets are ordered, never concurrent (the 3.2/3.3 precedent).

**REST surface.** `list_skill_files` gains an optional `path_prefix` query parameter passed through to storage. The web UI (`SkillsTab`) uses `GET /{id}/files` and never the MCP manifest, so it is unaffected by the trim; flat file-nav growth to ~130 rows is a recorded cosmetic follow-up.

**MCP listing tool.** The gobby-skills MCP registry currently exposes `get_skill_file` (which requires an already known exact path) and no file-listing tool at all — the storage/REST `path_prefix` surface has no MCP consumer, so an MCP agent that hits the manifest cap has no route to the omitted entries. Register a bounded `get_skill_files` tool in `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/get_skill.py` (the module that already registers `get_skill` and `get_skill_file`): arguments optional `skill_id`, optional `name`, optional `path_prefix`, optional `file_type`, and optional `after_path` (the continuation key), with `skill_id` and `name` resolved by the same ID-first / name-fallback rule `get_skill` and `get_skill_file` already apply. A name-only continuation would silently change which skill is being paginated: name resolution prefers a project row over a same-named global one, so following an overflow from a manifest a caller resolved by explicit global id would paginate the project skill's inventory instead. The tool reuses the resolving CTE the composite seam above already defines rather than adding a second resolver; the response returns a sorted, capped page of `{path, file_type, size_bytes}` entries plus `total_files` and `remaining_file_count` (from the same composite statement that produced the page — same filters, no second round trip, no row materialization) and `next_after_path` (the last path of the page, or null when the listing is exhausted), under the same bounded-response discipline as the manifest. The page is fetched through the storage keyset parameters as an ordered cap-plus-one server-side query preserving both filters — never by slicing an unbounded read — so every omitted entry is reachable by iterating `after_path`, and more-than-a-page of same-prefix entries can never pin the listing to one repeated first page. The manifest's overflow note names this tool as the discovery route.

Extend `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_files.py` (prefix matches, escaping of `%`/`_` in prefixes, combined with `file_type`; `exclude_file_type` selecting exactly the complement of `file_type` over a mixed tree; keyset `after_path`/`limit` ordering and server-side cap behavior; count parity with the listing filters, including cursor-relative counts that shrink page by page as `after_path` advances; `exclude_license` parity across the listing, count, summary, page, and composite seams over a tree containing a LICENSE row, asserting both the legacy `True` default and the explicit `False` the updater passes, with page, total, and remaining counts agreeing at each value; the path-byte limit rejecting a 1025-byte UTF-8 path at the write seam and accepting a 1024-byte one, and an oversized-legacy row inserted below the seam being omitted from listings and from `total_files`/`remaining_file_count` while reported in `omitted_oversized_path_count`, including a terminal page whose only remaining rows are oversized legacy ones — the walk ends with a null cursor, zero remaining, and a stable omission total; the scripts-summary aggregate against nested trees), `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py` (the new `get_skill_files` MCP tool: registration, prefix filtering, capped page with correct `total_files`/`remaining_file_count`, a selector case in which a project skill shadows a same-named global one — an explicit global `skill_id` continues only that global inventory while the name-only form resolves the project skill, matching what `get_skill` resolves for the same arguments — and a flat same-prefix pagination case — more entries under one prefix than the cap, walked via `after_path` until `next_after_path` is null, retrieving every entry exactly once with correct remaining counts at each step; manifest shape: no `content_hash`, scripts collapsed, references listed; plus a manifest-bound guard — a synthetic ~190-file script tree spanning several top-level directories serializes to only the fixed scripts summary with per-directory counts, no script paths and no content hashes, under a concrete serialized byte ceiling — including an adversarial maximum-cardinality shape with one unique long-named top-level directory per zero-byte script, which must stay under the same ceiling via the directory-entry cap, and a large mixed tree with many long-named non-script files, which must stay under the ceiling via the non-script entry cap with correct overflow counts; and an event-loop discipline case — with storage stubbed to block, a concurrent coroutine heartbeat keeps firing while both handlers serve, and a counting storage stub proves the manifest build issues exactly one composite query, never an unbounded row read; plus byte-budget cases — a page of maximum-width 1024-byte paths stops on measured encoded bytes before the entry cap and returns the last emitted path as `next_after_path`, the crossing entry is the first row of the following page, a full byte-bound walk returns every eligible row exactly once with no gap and no duplicate, and no response exceeds the ceiling by a single byte — plus snapshot cases run against the hub PostgreSQL backend: a full skill-file replacement committed concurrently with the manifest build yields a page, total, and summary that are wholly pre-replacement or wholly post-replacement, never mixed, and an atomic 1.1 publication changing both skill metadata and the complete file set concurrently with `get_skill` yields metadata and manifest from one revision, never new `runtime` over an old manifest — plus selector-parity cases for the composite seam (id-only, valid id alongside a different name, unresolvable id alongside a valid name, soft-deleted id, and a project row shadowing a global one) each resolving the same skill the current handler resolves — and a script-first ordering case where every script path sorts before every non-script path, proving the non-script cap is spent on non-script entries), and `tests/servers/routes/test_skills_routes.py` (query param passthrough). The manual token measurement in section 4.1 stays as corroborating evidence.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.2.1 - `get_skill_files` filters by an escaped SQL prefix via a new `path_prefix` parameter, composable with `file_type`, with an `exclude_file_type` complement filter, and with the preserved `exclude_license` flag, and supports keyset pagination via `after_path`/`limit` applied as an ordered server-side LIMIT. symbol: `SkillFilesMixin.get_skill_files`.
- 1.2.2 - The MCP get_skill manifest drops `content_hash`, lists non-script files under a fixed sorted cap with total and overflow counts, and collapses `scripts/**` to a capped per-top-level-dir summary with `remaining_directory_count`/`remaining_file_count` overflow fields and an execution note. symbol: `get_skill`. file: `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/get_skill.py`.
- 1.2.3 - The skill files REST route accepts `path_prefix`. symbol: `list_skill_files`.
- 1.2.4 - Filtering and manifest shape are pinned by tests. test: `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_files.py::test_get_skill_files_path_prefix`.
- 1.2.5 - The whole manifest stays bounded against a synthetic 190-file tree, an adversarial one-unique-directory-per-script shape, and a large long-named non-script tree: capped listings plus overflow counts only, under the exact `MAX_MANIFEST_RESPONSE_BYTES = 16384` serialized ceiling, with `MAX_SKILL_FILE_PATH_BYTES = 1024` enforced at the write seam and oversized legacy paths omitted from listings and from `total_files`/`remaining_file_count`, reported only through `omitted_oversized_path_count`, so an exhaustive walk always terminates with a null cursor and zero remaining. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_manifest_bounds_scripts_summary`.
- 1.2.6 - A bounded `get_skill_files` MCP tool is registered on gobby-skills with `skill_id`/`name` resolved by the same ID-first / name-fallback rule as `get_skill`, `path_prefix`/`file_type`/`after_path` arguments, and a capped server-side page plus overflow counts and a `next_after_path` continuation, so manifest overflow is discoverable and exhaustively walkable from the MCP surface itself and a continuation never paginates a different skill than the manifest it continues. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_get_skill_files_tool_bounded_page`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_get_skill_files_tool_resolves_same_skill_as_manifest`.
- 1.2.7 - Same-prefix listings larger than the page cap are retrieved exactly once per entry by walking `after_path`, with correct remaining counts at every step. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_get_skill_files_tool_paginates_same_prefix`.
- 1.2.8 - One private filtered-set builder renders the shared predicate — `path_prefix`, `file_type`, `exclude_file_type`, `exclude_license`, and `after_path` — once, and backs every composite statement, so a page's remaining count is counted beyond the cursor rather than inferred and no unconsumed public count or summary API is added; `get_skill_files`' list contract is unchanged for existing callers, and the manifest summary and the listing tool's `total_files` come from the same statement that produced their page, with no full-row materialization. file: `src/gobby/storage/skills/_files.py`. test: `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_files.py::test_count_and_summary_aggregates`.
- 1.2.10 - Every listing, count, summary, page, and composite seam applies the identical complete predicate including `exclude_license`, preserving the legacy `True` default and the updater's explicit `False`, so pages never disagree with their totals when a LICENSE row is present. symbol: `SkillFilesMixin.get_skill_files`. test: `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_files.py::test_exclude_license_parity_across_seams`.
- 1.2.11 - `get_skill` derives its skill row and its bounded manifest from one composite SQL statement in a single `ctx.run_db` call whose resolving CTE preserves the handler's existing selector precedence — `skill_id` first, name fallback when the id does not resolve, and `get_by_name`'s project/global/deleted/bundled-shadow rules on the name branch — so an atomic 1.1 revision publication yields wholly-old or wholly-new metadata-plus-manifest, never a mixed pair, and no call resolves a different skill than it does today. file: `src/gobby/storage/skills/_files.py`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_get_skill_reads_one_revision`.
- 1.2.9 - Both MCP handlers run every storage call on the request path through `ctx.run_db` off the event loop — the composite query and `get_skill`'s session resolution, skill-usage recording, and level-variable persistence alike, each independently proven by a blocking stub — with each response's page, count, and summary served by one composite SQL statement rather than a multi-statement transaction (READ COMMITTED gives each statement its own snapshot): a blocked-storage stub keeps the loop responsive, a maximum-cardinality tree serves the bounded manifest through that single query, and a concurrent full file-set replacement against PostgreSQL yields a wholly pre- or wholly post-replacement response. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_handlers_keep_event_loop_responsive`.

### 1.3 Install the pinned impeccable CLI as a Gobby-managed tool [category: code]

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py`
- `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py`
- `src/gobby/cli/install_setup.py::run_daemon_setup`

- `src/gobby/utils/deps.py::*` — scope-reason: adds get_impeccable_version alongside the existing version probes
