- `tests/cli/test_install_setup_gdaemon.py::*` — scope-reason: extends the shared non-schema setup stub to mock the impeccable installer alongside the existing SRT stub
- `tests/cli/test_install_ghook.py::*` — scope-reason: owns the direct ghook install paths whose helper mocks are permissive today, so the retargeted PATH helper's argument is asserted at that caller rather than only through the wrapper suite
- `tests/utils/test_dependency_requirements.py::*` — scope-reason: updates the exact optional-map assertion and patches impeccable_dependency_status in the report helper
- `tests/utils/test_deps.py::*` — scope-reason: owns the managed-version probes, so the new get_impeccable_version is exercised directly rather than only through the diagnostics report
- `tests/utils/test_status.py::*` — scope-reason: extends the existing format_status_message deps case to assert the optional managed impeccable rendering

Create `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py` (~250 lines) modeled on the SRT runtime installer `_install_srt_runtime` — the exact managed-npm-tool precedent:

- **Release pin**: a frozen dataclass `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE` in `src/gobby/utils/dependency_requirements.py` beside the SRT release pin, with `package="impeccable"`, `version="3.5.0"`, and `lockfile_sha256` pinning the vendored install lockfile.
- **Install**: follow the SRT installer's reproducible form exactly — write the install `package.json` in code (the SRT `_PACKAGE_JSON` pattern) pinning `impeccable@3.5.0`, copy the vendored `src/gobby/install/impeccable-package-lock.json` into staging, verify it against `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.lockfile_sha256`, then run `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund --omit=dev` in a tempfile staging dir under `get_gobby_home()/"tools"/impeccable/<version>/`, guarded by `export_file_lock`; ~180s timeout. `npm ci` enforces the lockfile's integrity hashes, so the complete transitive graph is pinned — the top-level-only post-install verification of `node_modules/impeccable/package.json` name+version against the pin stays as the identity check. Write `receipt.json`, then publish. Publication is **crash-atomic, not merely exception-rolled-back**: the SRT precedent's `_promote_install` renames the live target to a fixed `.<name>.previous` backup and only then renames staging into place (`install_setup_srt.py:163-173`), so process death between those two renames leaves the version directory absent — the launcher's exec target vanishes, a concurrently launched `impeccable` fails, and no `except` clause runs. Publish instead as an **immutable generation**: stage the complete tree at `<tools>/impeccable/<version>-<generation>/`, fsync it, then swap a stable `<tools>/impeccable/<version>` pointer from the old generation to the new one with a single atomic `os.replace` of a temporary symlink; the old generation is **retained until explicit `gobby uninstall --tools` cleanup (2.3)**, never removed at swap time. A Node process launched through the previous generation resolves modules and package files lazily, so recursive removal immediately after the swap can break an invocation already in flight even though the pointer itself never dangles. This is the same retention rule 2.2 applies to materialized cache generations for the same reason, and it reuses the uninstall boundary that already removes the whole tool root — no lease or refcount subsystem. Every path that executes or inspects the tool — the launcher's exec target, the diagnostics, and the reuse predicate — resolves through the stable pointer, so no window exists in which it names nothing **once a generation has been published**. The crash contract differs by state and is stated per state, because a first install has no prior generation to fall back to and no guarantee about a resolvable exec target can hold there. On a **repair**, the previous generation stays live and the pointer keeps resolving through staging and fsync alike; only the single `os.replace` moves it. On a **fresh install**, death before that replace leaves nothing reachable through the stable version path — no pointer, and therefore no launcher, receipt, or stamp any caller can resolve — and the reuse predicate reports the tool absent, so the next `gobby install` performs a clean fresh install rather than adopting wreckage. What it does leave on disk is the unactivated generation itself, its generation-scoped receipt included, since that receipt is fsynced inside the tree before the swap; being neither the pointer target nor named in the retained-generations record, it is collectible under the recovery rule below and the next locked run removes it. What death leaves in both states is the staged tree, so the states a collector must tell apart are defined by record rather than by name. There is no promotion rename: a generation is created directly at its final directory name, every generation name carries the reserved ownership-recognizable pattern (the same convention 2.2 applies to its cache stages), and the pointer swap is the sole activation step — so no window exists in which a finished-but-never-activated directory is indistinguishable from a retained one. Liveness is recorded, never inferred from naming: the outgoing pointer target is written into a durable retained-generations record **before** the swap, and recovery under the install `export_file_lock` preserves the current pointer target and every recorded retained generation while removing every other owned generation directory. Repeated failures therefore cannot accumulate abandoned directories, and no collector can remove a generation an in-flight process still resolves through. The receipt belongs to the generation, not to the activation: it is written and fsynced inside the staged tree before the swap, exactly where the install flow above already places it, so it can never be independently missing afterwards without mutating an immutable generation. The surfaces published after the swap are therefore the launcher and the stamp alone. Death **after** the swap — between those two writes — is covered by the same full-state reuse predicate: the pointer stays live, the install reads as incomplete because one of those artifacts is missing, and the next `gobby install` repairs exactly the missing surfaces rather than reinstalling blindly.
- **Optional deps**: optional dependencies install by default under `npm ci`, so puppeteer lands from the locked graph; `--ignore-scripts` suppresses puppeteer's postinstall Chrome download, making the explicit locked fetch the **only** Chrome producer. Fetch Chrome by invoking the verified installation's own executable directly — the staged tree's `node_modules/.bin/puppeteer browsers install chrome` under `PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR=~/.gobby/cache/puppeteer` — never plain `npx`, whose auto-install fetches an unpinned puppeteer from the registry when the local binary is absent or corrupt, escaping the locked graph. A missing local executable is a warn-only failed Chrome fetch (recorded by the Chrome readiness marker and retried by later installs), never a registry fallback. Chrome download failure is warn-only. The fetch runs under the single cross-process puppeteer-cache lock with an artifact readiness probe, so installer and materializer never mutate the shared cache concurrently. **That shared browser-cache contract is created here, not downstream**: this installer is the first producer of the cache, and 2.2 (which depends on 1.3) is the second, so the lock identity and readiness schema cannot be owned by the later leaf without 1.3 either implementing an unowned module early or duplicating a protocol that only works if both copies agree. `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py` — a neutral module outside `mcp_proxy.tools`, which the CLI must not import from — is therefore created in this deliverable holding exactly two things: the `export_file_lock` identity scoped to `~/.gobby/cache/puppeteer`, and the readiness record's schema plus its read/write/validate helpers. Readiness is **compatibility-keyed, not a bare ready flag**: the record carries the platform, the producing Puppeteer package version, the expected browser build identifier, and the channel, each derived by the producer from its own verified local Puppeteer installation. Puppeteer resolves a specific build per package version, so the managed CLI's locked puppeteer and a materialized tree's own puppeteer can legitimately expect different builds; a producer treats a record whose key does not match its own expectation as not-ready and performs its own serialized fetch rather than reporting readiness for a build its Puppeteer cannot launch. 2.2 imports this module and extends it with the materializer's own cache roots, owner marker, lock identities, and async acquisition wrapper.
- **Subprocess ownership**: the `npm ci` invocation and the direct `node_modules/.bin/puppeteer` Chrome fetch run through a synchronous owned-process-group helper mirroring the materializer's async one (2.2): each child starts in its own process group (`start_new_session=True`); on timeout the helper TERMs the whole group, waits a bounded grace period, escalates to a group KILL, and reaps with a final wait **before** any staging rollback or lock release — a timed-out npm- or puppeteer-spawned descendant must never still be writing while rollback deletes staging or after the puppeteer-cache lock releases. That helper covers timeout and cancellation, which are the paths where the parent survives to do the reaping; parent **death** is the path it cannot cover, because `start_new_session=True` is exactly what lets a descendant outlive a SIGKILLed installer while the dead process's file lock is released by the kernel. So liveness is recorded rather than assumed: each stage directory and each in-flight puppeteer-cache fetch records the process-group id of the child writing it, and a later lock holder — the next installer's stale-stage collector, the materializer (2.2), or `gobby uninstall --tools` (2.3) — skips and reports any stage or fetch whose recorded group still has a live member, collecting only once the group is gone. Two details are what make that record trustworthy, and both are cheaper than the watchdog they replace. The record is published **before the child can write**: the child is spawned into its own group holding an inherited synchronization descriptor and blocks on it until the parent has written and fsynced the owner record, so a parent killed in the spawn-to-record window closes the descriptor and the child exits at its barrier having written nothing — there is no window in which a live writer is unrecorded. And the record carries the group leader's process-start identity beside the numeric id, because the kernel only withholds a process-group number while the group still has a member: once the whole group exits the number is reusable, and a bare numeric probe would then read an unrelated live group as the abandoned stage's writer and leak that stage forever. A probe that finds the leader alive under a different start identity treats the record as dead and collects. One barrier descriptor, one recorded start identity, and one `killpg(pgid, 0)` probe answer "has every writer stopped?" without a supervisor process or a control channel. No installer subprocess invokes `npx` (Constraints item 6; acceptance 1.3.8).
- **Engine floor**: one `IMPECCABLE_NODE_MIN_VERSION` constant (`22.18.0`, the skill's declared node floor) in `src/gobby/utils/dependency_requirements.py` beside the release pin; the installer gates on the local Node version **before staging or `npm ci`** — npm does not enforce `engines` by default, so Node 22.17 would otherwise produce a promoted, receipted install outside the supported range that full-state verification then reuses forever. Missing or too-old Node raises the installer-specific error (the `run_daemon_setup` wiring stays warn-only) with zero publication of target dir, receipt, launcher, or stamp; a rejected install is repaired by a later `gobby install` once Node is upgraded. The floor also governs reuse: current-Node compatibility is part of the reusable-state predicate below, so after a post-install downgrade the installer rejects — mutating nothing, since repair cannot fix Node — instead of no-op-reusing a now-unsupported install and reporting it healthy; the SRT precedent checks Node only after its reuse early-return and is deliberately not followed on this point. The 4.1 pin test asserts the constant equals the skill's declared floor so installer gate and runtime declaration cannot drift.
- **Launcher**: a small shell launcher at `~/.gobby/bin/impeccable` (net-new surface — existing managed bins are copied files, not symlinks) that exports `PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR` and execs `~/.gobby/tools/impeccable/<version>/node_modules/.bin/impeccable "$@"`. Place it atomically via `stage_and_promote_binary_file` from `src/gobby/install/bin_freshness_promotion.py`, `chmod 0o755`, write a `.impeccable-version` stamp (mkstemp → fsync → os.replace idiom), then `_ensure_gobby_bin_on_path()`. Deliberately NOT added to `MANAGED_BIN_VERSION_PINS`. Every path this installer publishes, executes, diagnoses, or cleans — tool root, launcher, stamp, caches — derives from one `get_gobby_home()` value (the `~/.gobby` spellings are shorthand for that resolved home); `_ensure_gobby_bin_on_path()` is retargeted to accept that exact bin directory as a parameter, because its current implementation hardcodes the literal user-home bin path in both the PATH check and the shell rc lines it writes, which diverges from the install root whenever `GOBBY_HOME` is configured. That signature change has three existing production callers — `install_gcode`, `install_ghook`, and `install_gwiki` each invoke the helper with no argument today — so all three are updated in the same deliverable to pass their own resolved bin directory, and the existing installer-suite cases for them assert that exact argument rather than accepting any call. A permissive mock that ignores arguments would hide the break, so the assertion is on the value, not merely on invocation — and it has to land in each caller's own owning suite. `tests/cli/test_install_ghook.py` holds the direct ghook install paths and patches the PATH helper permissively today, so a successful ghook-install case there asserts the helper was called exactly once with that installer's own resolved bin directory; asserting only through the wrapper suite would leave the ghook caller unproven. The helper's own direct callers move too: `TestEnsurePath` in `tests/cli/test_install_setup.py` invokes the zero-argument form four times — normal insertion, repeat no-op, non-UTF-8 rc content, and unreadable rc content — and each must pass the configured resolved bin directory while keeping its existing assertions, including rc-file placement under the configured home. A required parameter that leaves its direct callers unmigrated fails the focused command before it can validate anything.
- **Platform contract**: every primitive this installer uses is POSIX — a shell launcher, a symlink pointer swapped by `os.replace`, extensionless `node_modules/.bin` shims, and process-group signalling. The repository supports native Windows (the gcode, ghook, gwiki, and gdaemon installers all carry `.exe` names and `sys.platform == "win32"` branches), so leaving the platform ungated would let the default warn-only setup path attempt a lifecycle whose core primitives do not exist. Gate the managed impeccable runtime on native win32 explicitly, mirroring the Homebrew skip already in Constraints rather than porting the mechanism: setup warns and publishes nothing (no tool root, launcher, stamp, or receipt), diagnostics report the platform limitation as a supported state that never marks preflight or health unhealthy, materialization (2.2) returns a scripts-only degraded result with no npm or browser execution, and uninstall (2.3) finds every path absent and reports nothing removed. WSL runs the ordinary POSIX path. Native Windows support for this tool is a recorded follow-up, not a gap this plan silently leaves.
- **Wiring**: call from `run_daemon_setup` in `src/gobby/cli/install_setup.py`, non-Homebrew branch, alongside the SRT install — warn-only, never raise.
- **Packaging**: the installer opens the vendored lockfile from the installed package directory, the way the SRT installer resolves `install/srt-package-lock.json`, so the file has to ship in the wheel. The `[tool.setuptools.package-data]` `gobby` entry enumerates that SRT lockfile explicitly and otherwise covers only `install/shared/**/*`, neither of which matches the new lockfile — the entry gains `install/impeccable-package-lock.json`. This deliverable owns the proof, because an acceptance item whose only evidence lives in the downstream validation leaf (4.1 depends on 1.3) cannot be satisfied when 1.3 closes. The installer suite asserts both halves of shippability without building a wheel: that the lockfile's package-relative path is matched by a declared `gobby` package-data pattern in `pyproject.toml`, and that the installer resolves and checksum-validates the lockfile **through that package-relative path** — exercised against a synthetic installed-package layout containing only package-data-matched files, so a resolution that reaches outside the installed package (a repo-relative or checkout-relative path) fails the test even though the checkout would satisfy it. Declaration plus package-path resolution together are what wheel-installability means; 4.1's matrix then corroborates them against a real built and installed wheel.
- **Repairable idempotency**: reuse is full-state verification, never receipt-only. The early return requires the receipt AND package identity (`node_modules/impeccable/package.json` name+version against the pin), the launcher present with the correct exec target and mode, the exec target itself — the installed tree's `node_modules/.bin/impeccable` — present, executable, and **identity-verified**, the locked puppeteer executable (`node_modules/.bin/puppeteer`, installed unconditionally from the locked graph since `--ignore-scripts` suppresses only its Chrome download, not the package) present and identity-verified — its absence would leave the warn-only Chrome retry permanently futile — a current Node satisfying `IMPECCABLE_NODE_MIN_VERSION` (the floor governs reuse, not just fresh installs), and a matching `.impeccable-version` stamp; any independently missing or corrupt artifact triggers repair on the next `gobby install` — the diagnostics' "run `gobby install` to repair" guidance must actually repair. Executable identity is verification, never mere presence: each `.bin` entry must resolve — following links, to a real path — **inside** the pinned installation root and land on the bin target its own package manifest declares, and puppeteer's installed package version must match the locked graph. Presence-and-executable-bit alone would accept a replaced file or a link escaping the install tree, and both the launcher and the Chrome fetch execute those paths directly, so a passing reuse check would hand execution to unintended code. Chrome readiness is tracked with its own marker so a previously failed warn-only Chrome download is retried by later installs instead of being permanently skipped.
- **Diagnostics**: `impeccable_dependency_status()` in `src/gobby/utils/dependency_requirements.py` modeled on `srt_dependency_status` (exact-version match, "run `gobby install` to repair" guidance). Classification is **optional managed tool, never required**: setup is warn-only and Homebrew installs skip npm-managed tools, so the status registers in `collect_dependency_report`'s optional section and must never enter the `required` map that `_run_install_preflight` converts into install-blocking errors — the SRT precedent needs `include_srt=False` there for exactly this reason. A missing or mismatched impeccable CLI never fails preflight (it would block installation before `run_daemon_setup` can install the tool) and never marks a supported degraded mode unhealthy. Add `get_impeccable_version()` to `src/gobby/utils/deps.py`, exercised directly in the suite that owns the adjacent managed-version probes with missing-tool, valid, version-mismatched, configured-home, and corrupt-artifact cases rather than only through the diagnostics report; update the dependency render in `src/gobby/utils/status.py`: the `format_status_message` labels map gains `impeccable` and the managed policy becomes `managed=name in {"srt", "impeccable"}` — and because impeccable registers in the **optional** section, whose render loop today prints raw names via `_format_dependency_record(record)` with `managed` defaulting to False (the labels map and managed flag currently exist only in the required-dependencies loop), the optional loop must apply the same labels map and managed-name policy, or the change would never reach impeccable's rendered line.

New `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py` follows the SRT installer test file's structure: staged-install generation publication and pointer swap, post-verify mismatch rollback, lockfile checksum-mismatch rejection, an argument assertion that the install invocation is `npm ci` with `--ignore-scripts` (lifecycle suppression — no Chrome producer outside the locked fetch), a Chrome-fetch assertion that the fetch invokes the installation's `node_modules/.bin/puppeteer` directly and that a missing local executable warn-skips with no `npx` or registry fallback, receipt contents, idempotent second run, launcher creation and stamp, diagnostics status states — plus repair-path cases: each independently corrupted or missing artifact (package tree, launcher, stamp, the `node_modules/.bin/impeccable` exec target, the `node_modules/.bin/puppeteer` locked fetch executable) triggers reinstall/repair, and a previously failed Chrome fetch is retried on the next install. Identity corruption is tested per binary and per failure mode: a wrong executable file at the `.bin` path, a link resolving to the wrong target inside the tree, and a link escaping the installation root are each rejected and repaired independently, as is a puppeteer package version diverging from the locked graph. A home-override case runs the lifecycle with `GOBBY_HOME` pointing at a directory distinct from the user home: install, launcher exec target, PATH bin directory, cache environment, and diagnostics all resolve under the configured home and the literal user-home dot-directory stays untouched (uninstall-side home resolution belongs to 2.3, which introduces the removal path, and the full install-through-uninstall lifecycle is exercised in 4.1). Timeout cases prove group termination: a stubbed hung npm child whose grandchild forks a delayed writer is TERM/KILL-reaped as a group before rollback, leaving no live process and no post-rollback writes. Owner-record cases prove the recorded id is trustworthy at both ends: an installer killed between spawn and the record's fsync leaves a child still blocked at its barrier that exits on the closed descriptor without writing, so a competing collector finds no live writer and collects the stage; and a stage whose recorded group number has been taken over by an unrelated live group — the leader's start identity no longer matching the record — is collected rather than skipped forever. Engine-floor cases test the exact boundary: Node 22.17 is rejected before staging with nothing published, Node 22.18.0 is accepted, a previously rejected install succeeds after a Node upgrade, and a valid install followed by a downgrade to 22.17 is rejected at reuse without mutating any installed artifact. Crash safety is tested by injected process death, not raised exceptions alone, and separately per state: on a **repair**, kill points before the fsync and between the fsync and the pointer swap each leave the previous generation live and the pointer resolvable, and a concurrent loop invoking the launcher with `--help` throughout never observes a missing exec target or a failed invocation; on a **fresh install**, the same kill points leave no pointer, launcher, receipt, or stamp, the reuse predicate reports the tool absent, and the next run installs cleanly. Successive kills leave no growing set of generation directories: the next locked run removes every owned generation that is neither the current pointer target nor named in the retained-generations record, and never removes one that is. Kill points cover the whole publication sequence, not only the pre-swap half — after the retained record is written and before the pointer replace, and after the replace between the launcher and stamp writes, each leaving the pointer live, the reuse predicate reporting the install incomplete, and the next run repairing exactly the missing artifact. A process launched from the superseded generation that defers a module load until after the swap still completes, proving generations outlive the pointer that named them. Two more cases pin the surfaces this deliverable owns for downstream consumers: a packaging case asserting the lockfile's package-relative path is matched by a declared `gobby` package-data pattern and that the installer resolves and checksum-validates it from a synthetic installed-package layout holding only package-data-matched files; and a shared-browser-cache case asserting the Chrome fetch writes a compatibility-keyed readiness record under the shared lock, reuses a record whose key matches its own verified Puppeteer, and refetches under the lock when platform, Puppeteer version, expected build, or channel diverges.

The wiring change also lands in the existing caller seams: update the `TestRunDaemonSetup` cases in `tests/cli/test_install_setup.py` — success, warn-only failure, and Homebrew — to mock the impeccable installer and assert it is invoked (or skipped on Homebrew). The same applies to `tests/cli/test_install_setup_gdaemon.py`, whose shared `_stub_non_schema_setup` helper stubs `install_srt_runtime` but not the new installer — its three `run_daemon_setup` invocations would perform a real impeccable install without an added stub. And `tests/utils/test_dependency_requirements.py` asserts the optional report map by exact equality (`report.optional == {"tailscale": healthy}`, line 243), so registering impeccable requires updating that assertion and patching `impeccable_dependency_status` in its report helper while preserving the required/preflight assertions. Finally, `tests/utils/test_status.py` extends `TestStatusUtils.test_format_status_message_deps` — the existing seam that exercises both dependency render loops — with an optional impeccable record asserting the rendered label and `(managed, verified)` output at the formatted-message boundary.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.3.1 - A pinned `impeccable@3.5.0` install lands under `~/.gobby/tools/impeccable/3.5.0/` with staging, locking, post-verify, receipt, and atomic promote. file: `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py`.
- 1.3.2 - Daemon setup wires the installer warn-only in the non-Homebrew branch, and repeat runs no-op only after verifying the complete installed state (receipt, package identity, launcher, stamp). symbol: `run_daemon_setup`.
- 1.3.3 - Dependency diagnostics report impeccable as an optional managed tool with repair guidance, and a missing impeccable never produces an install-preflight error. symbol: `collect_dependency_report`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_preflight_succeeds_without_impeccable`.
- 1.3.4 - `gobby status` renders the managed impeccable dependency in the optional section through the shared labels map and managed-name policy. symbol: `format_status_message`. test: `tests/utils/test_status.py::TestStatusUtils::test_format_status_message_deps`.
- 1.3.5 - Installer behavior is pinned by tests. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_second_install_is_noop`.
- 1.3.6 - Existing daemon-setup caller tests mock and assert the impeccable installer across the success, warn-only failure, and Homebrew-skip branches. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup.py::test_run_daemon_setup_homebrew_skips_impeccable`.
- 1.3.7 - Partial corruption of any installed artifact — including an independently missing, wrong-file, wrong-target, or installation-escaping `node_modules/.bin/impeccable` exec target or `node_modules/.bin/puppeteer` locked fetch executable, and a puppeteer package version diverging from the locked graph — and a previously failed Chrome fetch are repaired or retried by a later `gobby install`; reuse verifies executable identity and containment, never presence alone. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_repairs_partial_corruption`.
- 1.3.8 - The install is lockfile-reproducible with lifecycle scripts suppressed: the vendored install lockfile verifies against `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.lockfile_sha256`, the invocation is `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, and the explicit locked fetch — the installation's own `node_modules/.bin/puppeteer`, with a missing executable warn-skipping rather than falling back to `npx` or the registry — is the only Chrome producer. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_install_uses_locked_ci_without_scripts`.
- 1.3.9 - A timed-out installer subprocess is terminated and reaped as a whole process group — including npm-spawned descendants — before staging rollback or lock release; and when the installer itself is killed mid-`npm ci`, its orphaned group keeps the stage's recorded owner alive, so a competing collector skips that stage and reports it rather than deleting a tree still being written, collecting it only after the group exits; the owner record is published before the child can write — a parent killed in the spawn-to-record window leaves the child blocked at its inherited barrier, exiting unwritten — and it carries the group leader's start identity, so a reused group number is collected rather than skipped forever. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_timeout_reaps_process_group`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_orphaned_group_blocks_stage_collection`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_owner_record_precedes_child_writes`.
- 1.3.10 - The installer rejects Node below `IMPECCABLE_NODE_MIN_VERSION` (22.18.0) before staging with zero artifact publication, accepts exactly 22.18.0, repairs after a Node upgrade, and rejects reuse after a post-install downgrade below the floor without mutating installed artifacts. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_node_floor_gate`.
- 1.3.11 - Every managed-tool path this deliverable publishes — tool root, launcher, stamp, puppeteer cache, PATH bin entry, and diagnostics — derives from one `get_gobby_home()` value; with `GOBBY_HOME` overridden they all resolve under the configured home and never touch the literal user-home dot-directory. symbol: `_ensure_gobby_bin_on_path`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_gobby_home_override_consistency`.
- 1.3.12 - The retargeted PATH helper takes the resolved bin directory, and its three existing production callers pass their own — asserted by value in each caller's own owning suite, including the direct ghook install path, so no caller is left invoking the old zero-argument form or covered only by a permissive mock; the helper's four direct `TestEnsurePath` invocations are migrated to the new signature with their existing rc-file assertions intact. symbol: `install_ghook`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup.py::test_binary_installers_pass_resolved_bin_dir`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_ghook.py::test_install_ghook_passes_resolved_bin_dir`.
- 1.3.13 - The vendored managed-CLI install lockfile is shippable and resolvable from an installed distribution: its package-relative path matches a declared `gobby` package-data pattern, and the installer opens and checksum-validates it through that package-relative path against a layout containing only package-data-matched files. file: `pyproject.toml`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_packaged_lockfile_is_shippable_and_resolvable`.
- 1.3.14 - The shared browser-cache contract — the puppeteer-cache lock identity and the compatibility-keyed readiness schema keyed by platform, producing Puppeteer version, expected browser build, and channel — is created in this deliverable, and the installer's Chrome fetch writes, reuses, and refetches against it under that lock. file: `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_chrome_fetch_uses_compatibility_keyed_readiness`.
- 1.3.15 - The managed install publishes as an immutable generation behind a stable version pointer swapped by one atomic replace, with a crash contract stated per state: injected process death before the fsync or between the fsync and the swap leaves a repair's previous generation live and resolvable — a concurrent launcher loop never observing a missing exec target — while a fresh install is left with nothing resolvable through the stable version path — no pointer, launcher, receipt, or stamp — the tool reported absent, and the unactivated generation that remains on disk with its generation-scoped receipt collected by the next locked run, so the next run installs cleanly; recovery under the install lock preserves the current pointer target and every generation named in the durable retained-generations record written before the swap and removes every other owned generation, so repeated crashes accumulate nothing and nothing still resolvable is collected; the receipt is written inside the generation before the swap while the launcher and stamp are the only post-swap surfaces, and a crash between those two writes leaves the install readable as incomplete and repaired by the next run; and a superseded generation survives until `gobby uninstall --tools` so an in-flight process can still complete a deferred module load from it. file: `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_publication_survives_kill_at_every_point`.
- 1.3.16 - The managed runtime is gated on native win32 rather than attempting POSIX primitives that do not exist there: setup warns and publishes no tool root, launcher, stamp, or receipt; diagnostics report the platform limitation without producing an install-preflight error or an unhealthy state; WSL takes the ordinary POSIX path. The downstream behavior this gate implies belongs to the leaves that implement it — scripts-only materialization to 2.2.23 and cleanup of what that materialization leaves behind to 2.3.6 — because neither production surface exists in this deliverable. symbol: `impeccable_dependency_status`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_native_win32_is_gated_and_reported`.

## P2: Vendoring and materialization

`kind: framing`

**Goal**: The full 4.0.4 impeccable skill lives in the bundled tree with its runtime declaration, and DB-backed scripts execute from a content-addressed cache with full engines.

### 2.1 Vendor the full impeccable 4.0.4 tree with runtime metadata [category: config] (depends: 1.1, 1.2, 1.3)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/SKILL.md`
- `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/NOTICE.md`
- `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/scripts/package.json`
- `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/scripts/package-lock.json`
- `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/references/craft-floor.md`
- `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/references/operate.md`
- `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/references/adapt.md`
