The in-flight uncommitted diff (17 refreshed steering references with per-file adaptation notes, new craft-floor and operate references, SKILL.md Visitor Modes + script-execution sections, the NOTICE.md rewrite, the `.impeccable/` gitignore entry, and the interim 32-file scripts subset) is committed under rescoped task #19679 in the pre-expansion reconciliation step (Constraints, Decision Record item 12). This deliverable builds on that commit and completes the vendor. Release staging runs the pinned managed CLI installed by 1.3 — hence the hard dependency:

- **Full scripts tree.** The vendored tree lives under the skill's `scripts/` directory. Replace the interim 32-file subset wholesale with the full 107-file generated scripts tree from the **released** 4.0.4 output — staged by running the pinned CLI's `install` into a throwaway harness directory (the release artifact, whose generated SKILL.md frontmatter reads `version: 4.0.4`), never from git HEAD. Files are copied unmodified; never hand-edited. The full tree is enumerated file-by-file in Targets from the staged release inventory (pinned 2026-08-06), so expansion ownership is complete at planning time.
- **Gobby-added dependency pin files.** Create `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/scripts/package.json` pinning upstream's exact dependency mapping, recorded from the npm registry for `impeccable@3.5.0` (2026-08-06): dependencies `css-select ^7.0.0`, `css-tree ^3.2.1`, `domutils ^4.0.2`, `fflate ^0.8.3`, `htmlparser2 ^12.0.0`, `marked ^18.0.5`; optional dependency `puppeteer ^25.1.0` — these literals are the independent expected values the 4.1 pin test asserts against. Generate the companion `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/scripts/package-lock.json` from those pins so materialize-time `npm ci` (2.2) installs one locked, reproducible dependency graph — without the lockfile, unpinned transitives let identical script bytes produce different `node_modules` trees under one cache key. ESM `import()` ignores `NODE_PATH`, so the materialized cache needs its own `node_modules`; Chrome (the heavy part) is shared via `PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR`.
- **Runtime declaration.** Add the `metadata.gobby.runtime` block (exact YAML in section 1.1) to `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/SKILL.md` frontmatter: `node: ">=22.18.0"`, `cli: {npm: "impeccable", version: "3.5.0", bin: "impeccable"}`, `skill_release: "4.0.4"`.
- **4.0.4 reference deltas.** Diff the already-refreshed references against the staged 4.0.4 release output and fold the small content deltas (verified example: the 4.0.4 release drops the Browser-surfaces bullet from `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/references/craft-floor.md`); the established Gobby adaptation rules stay as catalogued in NOTICE.md. The delta work set is pinned at planning time and enumerated in Targets: the nineteen already-refreshed references (seventeen refreshed steering references plus the new `craft-floor.md` and `operate.md`), each checked against the staged output with deltas folded where present. The delta fold also updates each reference's resolver paragraph to export the tool's returned `environment.PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR` for any browser-engine invocation (2.2) — without it, a fetched Chrome is invisible to the documented direct-node commands.
- **Provenance.** Update `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/NOTICE.md`: vendored scripts section now covers the full 107-file tree sourced from the release channel pinned by `skill_release: 4.0.4`, plus the Gobby-added dependency pin file.

Packaging is already correct: `pyproject.toml` ships `install/shared/**/*`, and the dot-prefixed `.upgrade/` directory is excluded by glob semantics. Loader limits verified: largest file 492KB (1MB per-file cap), total ~3.3MB (10MB skill cap), all files text.

Task reconciliation happens before expansion, never through it, and it is a **handoff gate rather than an acceptance item of this deliverable**: #19679 is retitled and moved under the new epic, owns exactly the in-flight uncommitted diff (including the `.impeccable/` entry in `.gitignore`), and closes with that commit — verified complete before any generated leaf is created. Because a separate task performs that work, no acceptance item and no coverage-ledger row here claims it; an item this deliverable's leaf cannot itself satisfy would let coverage attest work that had not run. The expansion-generated leaf for this section owns everything else above — the full 107-file tree swap, the dependency pin file, the runtime declaration, the 4.0.4 reference deltas, and the NOTICE.md provenance update — so exactly one executable task identity exists per deliverable and expansion never creates a duplicate 2.1 task.

**Acceptance:**

- 2.1.1 - The bundled skill carries the full 107-file released 4.0.4 scripts tree, copied unmodified, with provenance recorded. behavior: "Vendored scripts" in `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/NOTICE.md`.
- 2.1.2 - SKILL.md frontmatter declares the `metadata.gobby.runtime` block with node floor, CLI pin, and skill release. file: `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/SKILL.md`.
- 2.1.3 - Gobby-added scripts-level dependency pin files exist: `package.json` with upstream's parser deps and optional puppeteer, and its generated `package-lock.json` locking the full graph. file: `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/scripts/package.json`.
- 2.1.4 - The refreshed references match the 4.0.4 release content modulo catalogued Gobby adaptations. file: `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/references/craft-floor.md`.

### 2.2 Add the materialize_skill_scripts MCP tool [category: code] (depends: 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 2.1)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/materialize_skill_scripts.py`
- `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/__init__.py::create_skills_registry`
- `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py`
- `src/gobby/storage/skills/_files.py::*` — scope-reason: adds the composite skill-row-plus-scripts-inventory read seam the materializer needs, beside the filtering and aggregate seams 1.2 lands first (1.2 is a declared dependency, so these edits are ordered, never concurrent)
- `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py`
- `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_registry.py::*` — scope-reason: owns skills-registry construction, which this deliverable extends with the new tool registration

Create `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/materialize_skill_scripts.py` exposing `materialize_skill_scripts(name)` on gobby-skills, registered via `create_skills_registry` in `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/__init__.py`. The cache's shared contract lives in the small neutral module `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py` — created by 1.3, the first producer of the shared browser cache, holding the puppeteer-cache lock identity and the compatibility-keyed readiness schema. This deliverable **extends** that module with the skill-scripts side of the contract: the cache layout paths, owner-marker read/write/validate, the namespace and root lock identities, the reserved stage-name pattern, and the async cancellation-safe acquisition wrapper. Both this tool and the 2.3 uninstall path import it (the CLI must not import from `mcp_proxy.tools`):

- **Cache layout**: writes the skill's `scripts/**` bytes from `skill_files` to `~/.gobby/cache/skill-scripts/<skill_id>/<content_hash>/scripts/`, where `<content_hash>` is a digest over the sorted `{path: content_hash}` map of the skill's script files **and the normalized runtime metadata this tool consumes** (`runtime.node`, `runtime.cli`, `skill_release`) **and the normalized trust tier** (`is_external_source(source_type)`, which decides whether this generation may hold dependencies at all) — content-addressed over the whole consumed revision, so any script change self-invalidates and a metadata-only revision cannot silently reuse a generation whose recorded provenance describes the previous one. Materialization never prunes sibling `<content_hash>` dirs: a directory already returned to an agent must remain valid for that consumer's lifetime (long-lived live-mode processes hold script paths — see 3.3), so cleanup of materialized trees belongs solely to the explicit uninstall path (2.3). On first materialization for a skill, a versioned `owner.json` marker is written at the `<skill_id>/` cache root so cleanup can prove ownership from disk alone (2.3), independent of a live DB row and of the skill-creation UUID collision fallback. That root marker holds **only immutable ownership facts** — schema version, skill id and name, and the source kind at first materialization as *origin provenance*, never as the current trust state, which is mutable and belongs to the generation key — because it is written once and never rewritten; mutable revision provenance (`skill_release`, the runtime block's `node` floor and `cli` pin) belongs to a per-generation provenance record written inside `<content_hash>/` at publication, alongside the bytes it describes. Keeping `skill_release` in a write-once root marker would report the first-ever materialized release forever — exactly the mixed-revision state the composite read below exists to prevent. Every cache path in this section derives from the configured Gobby home (`get_gobby_home()`, honoring `GOBBY_HOME`) — the `~/.gobby` spellings throughout are shorthand for that resolved home, never literal user-home paths (1.3).
- **Why cache, not source_path**: hub-installed, ZIP-installed, read-only, and DB-only skills have no usable disk tree; the DB bytes are authoritative. Scripts run from the cache, never from `src/gobby/install/shared/`.
- **Path safety**: reject absolute paths and any path containing `..` segments before writing; write via a temp dir + atomic rename so a partial materialize never serves.
- **Concurrency**: same-process calls serialize on a per-skill-and-content-hash `asyncio.Lock`; the cross-process lock is **one stable `export_file_lock` scoped to the `<skill_id>/` cache root** (identity defined in `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py`), acquired for every publication and attach regardless of content hash — the identical lock the 2.3 uninstall path acquires, so a publisher for a previously unseen content hash can never race root deletion. `script_cache.py` additionally defines **one stable namespace/discovery lock at the skill-scripts cache namespace root** — without it, marker-scan discovery races cold-root creation past the per-root lock (an empty-namespace scan can report success just before a cold materializer publishes its first root). First-root publication is also **crash-atomic, not merely lock-serialized** — locks serialize live processes but cannot make mkdir-then-write atomic against process death, which would leave a permanently unclaimable unmarked root: the producer stages the complete `<skill_id>/` root (owner marker included, fsynced) in a sibling temporary directory and publishes it with **one atomic rename** while holding the namespace lock, so a crash before the rename leaves only an abandoned stage directory (cleaned opportunistically under the namespace lock) and no visible root ever lacks its marker. The 2.3 uninstall path holds the namespace lock across enumeration, marker validation, root-lock acquisition, and deletion. Lock ordering is namespace lock then root lock, and the producer-side namespace critical section is only first-root staging plus the publication rename — never dependency installs or content publications. **Warm-waiter recovery preserves that order**: any root-lock acquisition that had to wait re-checks root existence after acquiring; if uninstall deleted the root in the interim, the holder releases the root lock and restarts at namespace-then-root — it never acquires the namespace lock while holding a root lock. Every cross-process lock acquisition in the async materializer — namespace, root, and puppeteer-cache alike — goes through one shared cancellation-safe wrapper defined in `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py`: nonblocking acquisition attempts with async retry, never a blocking acquire moved to `asyncio.to_thread`, whose worker thread survives caller cancellation and can acquire the file lock afterwards with no surviving owner to release it, stranding the lock for the daemon process's lifetime. The wrapper holds nothing at the moment of cancellation, and it keeps the event loop responsive while a holder awaits subprocess work — a blocking sync acquire here would deadlock the loop against its own lock holder. Cache re-check after lock acquisition. Publication is a two-step per-hash state machine with published content immutable: (1) the scripts tree stages in a sibling temp directory and publishes once by atomic rename — script files are never mutated after publication; (2) dependencies stage in a separate sibling directory with the readiness marker written **inside** the staged directory, and, only on success, attach by **one** atomic rename of the staged `node_modules` (marker included) into the published generation — marker presence is therefore equivalent to attach completion, no on-disk state can hold `node_modules` without its marker, and an attach adds a directory without rewriting script files, so a directory already returned to a caller stays valid throughout. On dependency-install failure the staging directory is discarded; after a crash it is orphaned only until the next operation — script and dependency stages use an ownership-recognizable reserved stage-name pattern (defined in `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py` beside the lock identities), and every materialization collects stale sibling stages under the locks it already holds (the namespace lock for first-root stages, the root lock for content and dependency stages) before staging anew, so repeated crashes before a publication or attach rename never accumulate abandoned trees. The scripts-only generation remains published and usable in parser-only mode, and no rename target exists; a later call that finds a published generation without its marker collects any stale dependency stage and re-runs only the dependency staging and attach under the same locks — the single-rename attach guarantees the retry's target is always absent.
- **Async discipline**: all DB reads go through `ctx.run_db(...)` — the ~2.8MB `include_content=True` pull must not block the event loop; use the `path_prefix="scripts/"` filter from 1.2. The resolved skill row and the complete filtered scripts inventory come from **one composite read seam** — a single SQL statement over `skills` and `skill_files`, added beside 1.2's seams in `src/gobby/storage/skills/_files.py` — invoked in one `ctx.run_db` call. The seam takes `name` and `project_id` (which `SkillsContext` already carries), and its resolving CTE encodes `SkillMetadataMixin.get_by_name`'s exact selector semantics rather than an ad-hoc name match: project-scoped row first, global fallback when `include_global`, `deleted_at IS NULL` filtering, and rejection of a project row whose `source_path` is a bundled template path (the stale-shadow rule from #17606). A resolver that skipped those rules could materialize and execute a different skill than `get_skill(name)` returns for the same name. Two separate reads would straddle 1.1's atomic publication commit under READ COMMITTED, pairing one revision's `runtime`/`skill_release` metadata with another's script bytes; every value the tool derives — runtime validation, the scan gate, the content digest that names the cache generation, the owner marker's provenance, and the returned state — comes from that one result, so a materialized directory always corresponds to a revision that actually existed. Subprocesses (npm install, Chrome fetch) run via `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` with `asyncio.wait_for` (~180s), the mcp_proxy precedent from daemon_control — wrapped in a shared async subprocess helper that owns the failure path and launches every installer subprocess in its own process group (`start_new_session=True`): on timeout or caller cancellation it TERMs the **whole group**, waits a bounded grace period, escalates to a group KILL, and reaps with a final wait **before** any filesystem recovery (discarding staging trees, attaching or skipping the marker, releasing the materialization lock). A timed-out child — or any npm-spawned descendant — must never still be writing while recovery mutates or publishes cache state. Daemon death is the case the helper cannot reach, since the orphaned group survives while the kernel releases the dead process's file lock, so this tool applies the same recorded-owner rule 1.3 defines: every stage and every in-flight browser fetch records the writing child's process-group id, and a later holder of the same lock collects a stale stage or starts its own fetch only once that group has no live member, skipping and reporting it otherwise. Subprocesses and DB reads are not the only blocking phases: cold-cache staging (~2.8MB across 109 files), stale-stage and stale `node_modules` collection, the fsync sweep, and the publication copy are all synchronous filesystem work, and each runs in a worker rather than inline on the loop. Cancellation is defined against those workers too — a cancelled caller awaits any in-flight worker to a safe completion boundary **before** releasing the lock it holds, so no worker is still writing into a staging tree that recovery is deleting, or into a root another process has just acquired.
- **Dependency install**: when the materialized tree contains a scripts-level `package.json`, copy that `package.json` and the vendored `scripts/package-lock.json` (2.1) into the isolated sibling dependency-stage directory and run `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund` **only there** (best-effort), with `PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR=~/.gobby/cache/puppeteer` in the subprocess env. `npm ci` creates `node_modules` in its working directory, so running it inside the published generation would mutate published state directly and make the forbidden `node_modules`-present/marker-absent state reachable; only the staged `node_modules` (readiness marker inside) is renamed into the published generation's `scripts/` directory, per the Concurrency state machine. The lockfile is one of the skill's script files, so it already participates in the content-addressed digest — one cache key therefore reproduces one locked dependency graph across machines and time, and the readiness marker records the lockfile's content hash. Offline or missing npm degrades gracefully: the tool still returns the cache dir with a warning, and the detector runs in line-based mode. **Trust tier.** The scan gate can authorize only the bytes it inspected — SKILL.md and the stored `scripts/**` payloads. `npm ci` then downloads an executable dependency graph selected by a lockfile that is itself one of those external files, and the browser fetch executes `node_modules/.bin/puppeteer` out of that tree; integrity hashes authenticate what the lockfile named, they do not make it trusted, and `node_modules` is never scanned. Dependency installation and browser-binary execution are therefore restricted to **non-external sources** (`is_external_source` false — the bundled impeccable skill and any other Gobby-owned source). An external-source skill still materializes: it passes the same fail-closed scan and publishes a scripts-only generation, with `parser_deps.installed` and `browser.ready` both false and each carrying a warning naming the trust restriction, and no npm or `.bin` process is spawned for it at all. `source_type` is mutable while `skill_id` is stable, so trust is part of the generation key rather than a runtime branch over a shared generation: without it, a skill that was trusted and is re-sourced as external with byte-identical scripts and runtime metadata would hash to the existing generation and be handed back its `node_modules` and browser-ready marker, silently defeating the restriction. Widening this to an operator-approved external allowlist is a recorded Constraints follow-up, not part of this plan — no skill in scope needs it.
- **Shared browser cache**: any Chrome fetch runs under one cross-process puppeteer-cache lock (an `export_file_lock` scoped to `~/.gobby/cache/puppeteer`) with an artifact readiness probe before and after — the lock identity and readiness schema 1.3 defines in `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py`, so installer, materializer, and upgrade tooling read and write one format — so distinct skill hashes, or installer and materializer, never mutate the shared cache concurrently. This tool is a consumer of that compatibility-keyed record, never a second definition of it: it derives its own expectation (platform, its generation's Puppeteer package version, expected browser build, channel) from the materialized tree's verified local Puppeteer, reuses a record whose key matches, and treats any other record as not-ready — performing its own serialized fetch and rewriting the record for its own key — rather than reporting `browser.ready: true` for a build its Puppeteer cannot launch. Lock ordering: the puppeteer-cache lock is always acquired last and never held across the per-hash publish or attach rename. The materializer's Chrome fetch invokes the materialized generation's own `scripts/node_modules/.bin/puppeteer` executable directly — never plain `npx`, whose auto-install would fetch an unpinned puppeteer from the registry when the local binary is absent; an absent executable reports `browser.ready: false` with a warning instead.
- **Gates**: fail-closed scanning for external sources covering the **whole materialized surface**. `_serve_scan_error` scans one content string per call, and iterating it across 109 payloads would run 109 synchronous scanner invocations — each materializing its own temp file — directly on the daemon event loop. `src/gobby/skills/scanner.py` already exposes the whole-surface entry point this needs: `scan_skill_content(content, name, files=...)` takes the auxiliary path-to-content map and scans SKILL.md plus every file in one call (it is what `scan_parsed_skill` delegates to). The materializer calls it **once**, with the DB-loaded `scripts/**` payload map, inside a worker (`asyncio.to_thread`), before any cache tree is created — a safe SKILL.md paired with one unsafe script refuses materialization with zero cache state published. Bundled and other non-external sources keep the existing bypass (`is_external_source`). The scanner's per-process content-hash cache belongs to the one-content serve path and does not apply here: `scan_skill_content` materializes and scans its whole supplied surface on every call, so the whole-surface scan runs once per external-source materialization, in a worker. Its cost stays the recorded Constraints follow-up on hub-distributed script-ful skills. Also a node-version warning compared against `metadata.gobby.runtime.node` when node is present but below the floor. The floor value arrives validated (1.1) — an absent floor means no gate (`satisfies_floor: null`) — and rows written before validation existed are defensively re-validated here: an invalid `runtime` block is treated as absent, with a warning. On native win32, where 1.3 gates the managed runtime off, materialization still writes the cache tree and returns it scripts-only: no npm and no `.bin` process is spawned, `parser_deps.installed` and `browser.ready` are false with a warning naming the platform limitation, and the line-based detector remains usable.
- **Return shape**: `{scripts_dir, files_written, environment: {PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR: str}, parser_deps: {installed: bool, warning: str | null}, browser: {ready: bool, warning: str | null}, node: {version: str | null, satisfies_floor: bool | null}}`. The `environment` map carries the absolute browser-cache path every consumer process must export: Puppeteer resolves its browser cache from the environment at **launch** time, so `browser.ready: true` for a Chrome fetched into `~/.gobby/cache/puppeteer` is useless to a `node <scripts_dir>/detect.mjs` invocation that inherits the default cache location — the adapted references (2.1, 3.2, 3.3) make every direct-node resolver command apply this value, mirroring what the 1.3 launcher already exports for the CLI path. — each independent execution prerequisite reports its own state: parser dependencies, browser readiness (Chrome present in the shared cache), and the node executable, so callers can distinguish a usable parser-only cache from a failed Chrome setup or a missing runtime. Missing node is defined: `node.version: null`, `satisfies_floor: null`, materialization still succeeds (the cache is static files) with the gap visible to the caller. Readiness markers are validated against the artifacts they describe — a marker without its `node_modules`, or a browser marker without the browser binary, reads as not-ready and triggers repair on the next call.

New `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py`: cache path shape and self-invalidation on content change, sibling-generation retention (a directory returned before a newer content hash publishes remains present and readable afterwards), path-safety rejection, npm-absent degradation, scan-gate refusal (including a safe-SKILL.md/unsafe-script external-source case asserting refusal with zero cache publication), registration, first-ever-root creation publishing the owner marker under the namespace/discovery lock (no marker-less root observable), a DB-only materialization case (the skill's only script bytes live in `skill_files` with no usable `source_path`; the exact DB bytes are written and returned from the cache, and a deliberately divergent read-only source tree proves the implementation never falls back to bundled-source files), a concurrent-call case (two simultaneous cold materialize calls: one dependency-install attempt, one published directory, identical results, no leaked temp tree — with the first holder blocked in an awaited stubbed dependency subprocess, proving the event loop stays responsive while the lock is held), and subprocess-failure cases (a stubbed hung npm child on timeout, and caller cancellation mid-install: the whole process group — including a stub grandchild that forks a delayed writer — is terminated and reaped before recovery, and no live process or post-publication write remains). Additional cases: failed-first-install recovery (dependency staging fails, the scripts-only generation publishes and is usable; the next call attaches dependencies without mutating any published script file, and a directory held by a concurrent reader stays byte-identical across the retry), owner-marker write and validation at the `<skill_id>` root, independent prerequisite reporting (parser-only cache vs failed browser vs missing node, each failing and repairing independently with markers validated against artifacts), installer/materializer puppeteer-cache overlap serialized by the shared lock, a fault-injection case killing the flow between dependency staging and the attach rename (the generation stays marker-less and scripts-only, and the next call attaches cleanly because the single-rename attach guarantees an absent target — no state with `node_modules` present and marker absent is reachable, and at no point during staging does any `node_modules` appear inside the published generation), a first-root crash case (killed between root staging and the publication rename: no `<skill_id>/` root is visible, only an abandoned stage that the next call cleans and republishes atomically), a missing-local-puppeteer case (with `scripts/node_modules/.bin/puppeteer` absent, captured subprocess attempts show no `npx` or registry command, `browser.ready` is false with a warning, no browser marker is published, and a later valid local binary repairs readiness), an environment-contract case (the returned `environment.PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR` is absolute and matches the shared cache the fetch populated), a held-lock cancellation case per lock class (with another holder on the namespace, root, or puppeteer-cache lock, a waiter is cancelled mid-acquisition; nothing is stranded — after the holder releases, a fresh caller acquires immediately, proving the nonblocking-retry wrapper held nothing at cancellation), a repeated-crash stage-accumulation case (successive kills before the publication and attach renames leave no growing set of stages; the next successful call collects every stale owned stage and publishes cleanly), a browser-compatibility case (a readiness record produced under one Puppeteer version and browser build is treated as not-ready by a consumer expecting another, which performs its own serialized fetch under the shared lock and rewrites the record for its own key, while a matching key is reused without refetching), a revision-snapshot case (an atomic 1.1 publication committed concurrently with a materialization yields either the wholly-old or the wholly-new revision — the resolved metadata, the written bytes, the content digest, and the owner marker's recorded provenance always agree, and never the old `skill_release` over new script bytes or the reverse), a name-resolution parity case (project-shadowed, global-fallback, soft-deleted, and stale bundled-template-sourced project rows each resolve identically through `materialize_skill_scripts` and `get_skill`), a metadata-only revision case (a `skill_release` or `runtime.node` change with byte-identical scripts publishes a new generation whose per-generation provenance and reported node floor describe the new revision, while the immutable root marker is unchanged, the prior generation stays held and readable, and 2.3 cleanup still proves ownership), an external-source trust-tier case (a scan-clean external skill materializes scripts-only with zero npm and zero `.bin` invocations captured, `parser_deps.installed` and `browser.ready` both false with restriction warnings, and the returned cache dir usable in line-based mode), trust-transition cases in both directions (the same skill id with byte-identical scripts and runtime metadata flipped trusted-to-external returns a fresh scripts-only generation with no `node_modules` and no browser-ready marker, and external-to-trusted likewise starts a fresh generation rather than adopting the degraded one), cold-path responsiveness cases (a concurrent heartbeat keeps firing during the whole-surface scan, during cold staging of the full tree, and during stale-dependency cleanup and publication) with a cancellation case per phase proving the in-flight worker completes to a safe boundary before its lock releases, and a malformed legacy `runtime` block treated as absent with a warning.

**Acceptance:**

- 2.2.1 - The tool is registered on gobby-skills and returns the materialized scripts directory with independent `parser_deps`, `browser`, and `node` prerequisite states. symbol: `create_skills_registry`.
- 2.2.2 - Scripts materialize from `skill_files` bytes into the content-addressed cache with atomic writes and self-invalidation; previously returned generations are never pruned by materialization. file: `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/materialize_skill_scripts.py`.
- 2.2.3 - Missing npm degrades to a warning with a usable cache dir. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_degrades_without_npm`.
- 2.2.4 - Unsafe file paths are rejected before any write. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_rejects_unsafe_paths`.
- 2.2.5 - Materialization succeeds from `skill_files` bytes alone and never falls back to bundled-source files. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_materializes_from_db_bytes_only`.
- 2.2.6 - Concurrent cold materialization performs one dependency install and publishes one directory, and the event loop stays responsive while the file lock is held across an awaited subprocess. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_concurrent_materialize_single_install`.
- 2.2.7 - Subprocess timeout and caller cancellation terminate and reap the whole process group — including npm-spawned descendants — before any filesystem recovery, leaving no live process and no post-publication writes. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_timeout_reaps_subprocess`.
- 2.2.8 - A materialization directory returned before a newer content hash publishes remains readable afterwards. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_old_generation_survives_new_publish`.
- 2.2.9 - A failed dependency install — or a crash at any point before the attach rename — leaves a usable scripts-only generation with no reachable `node_modules`-present/marker-absent state; the next call attaches dependencies via the always-absent rename target without mutating published script files, and a concurrently held directory stays byte-identical. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_failed_install_retry_attaches_deps`.
- 2.2.10 - Execution prerequisites report and repair independently, and a missing node executable is non-fatal with defined null states; a stored `runtime` block written before write-side validation existed is defensively re-validated here and, when invalid, treated as absent — no version gate, `satisfies_floor: null`, and a warning — never trusted and never fatal. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_missing_node_reports_independently`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_malformed_legacy_runtime_treated_as_absent`.
- 2.2.11 - The `<skill_id>` cache root carries a validated versioned owner marker after first materialization, published under the namespace/discovery lock so no root is ever observable without its marker. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_owner_marker_written`.
- 2.2.12 - External-source materialization scans SKILL.md and every DB-loaded `scripts/**` payload fail-closed before any cache tree is created; a safe SKILL.md with one unsafe script refuses with zero cache publication. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_scan_gate_covers_all_script_files`.
- 2.2.13 - The tool returns `environment.PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR` as an absolute path so consumer processes can resolve the fetched browser. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_returns_browser_cache_environment`.
- 2.2.14 - First-root publication is crash-atomic: a crash before the staged-root rename leaves no visible `<skill_id>/` root, only an abandoned stage cleaned under the namespace lock, and the next materialization publishes cleanly. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_first_root_crash_leaves_no_unmarked_root`.
- 2.2.15 - A materializer whose local puppeteer executable is absent never invokes `npx` or any registry fallback: `browser.ready` is false with a warning, no browser marker is published, and a later valid local binary repairs readiness. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_materializer_missing_local_puppeteer_never_invokes_npx`.
- 2.2.16 - Cancelling a waiter on any cross-process cache lock strands nothing: acquisition is nonblocking-with-async-retry through the shared cancellation-safe wrapper, and after cancellation a subsequent caller acquires the lock. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_lock_waiter_cancellation_leaves_lock_free`.
- 2.2.17 - Script and dependency staging directories carry the reserved ownership-recognizable stage names and record the writing child's process group, and stale stages are collected under the held locks before each retry only once that group has no live member — repeated crashes before publication or attach never accumulate abandoned stages, and a daemon killed mid-install leaves an orphaned group whose stage a competing collector skips and reports rather than deleting under an active writer; the same 1.3 record rules apply here, so a materializer killed in the spawn-to-record window leaves its child blocked at the inherited barrier with nothing written, and a stage whose recorded group number was taken over by an unrelated live group is collected on the start-identity mismatch rather than skipped forever. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_repeated_crashes_leave_no_stage_accumulation`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_orphaned_group_blocks_stage_collection`.
- 2.2.18 - The materializer consumes the 1.3-defined compatibility-keyed readiness record: it reuses a record only when platform, Puppeteer version, expected browser build, and channel match its own verified local Puppeteer, and otherwise refetches under the shared lock and rewrites the record for its own key — never a `browser.ready: true` for an unlaunchable build. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_browser_readiness_is_compatibility_keyed`.
- 2.2.19 - The resolved skill row and the complete scripts inventory are read by one composite statement in a single `ctx.run_db` call whose resolving CTE reproduces `get_by_name`'s exact selector semantics — project-first, global fallback, deleted filtering, and bundled-template shadow rejection — so a concurrent atomic revision publication yields a wholly-old or wholly-new materialization and the tool never resolves a different skill than `get_skill` does for the same name. file: `src/gobby/storage/skills/_files.py`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_materialization_reads_one_revision`.
- 2.2.20 - Cache generation identity covers every field that changes what the generation may contain or execute, the normalized trust tier included: a metadata-only revision (`skill_release` or `runtime.node` changed, scripts byte-identical) publishes a new generation carrying its own provenance record, a trust-tier flip in either direction with byte-identical scripts also publishes a fresh generation rather than inheriting the other tier's dependencies, the write-once root marker holds only immutable ownership facts, and previously returned generations stay held and readable. file: `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_metadata_only_revision_publishes_new_generation`.
- 2.2.21 - External sources never execute unscanned bytes: dependency installation and browser-binary execution are restricted to non-external sources, and a scan-clean external skill materializes scripts-only with no npm or `.bin` invocation, false `parser_deps` and `browser` states with restriction warnings, and a usable degraded cache. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_external_source_never_installs_dependencies`.
- 2.2.22 - Every synchronous phase runs off the event loop — one whole-surface `scan_skill_content` call, cold staging, stale collection, fsync, and publication all in workers — and a cancellation during any phase awaits its worker to a safe boundary before releasing the held lock. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_cold_materialization_keeps_event_loop_responsive`.
- 2.2.23 - On native win32, where 1.3 gates the managed runtime off, materialization still writes and returns the cache tree scripts-only: no npm and no browser process is spawned, `parser_deps.installed` and `browser.ready` are false with warnings naming the platform limitation, and the line-based detector stays usable — so the platform gate leaves a real cache root behind rather than nothing. symbol: `materialize_skill_scripts`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_native_win32_materializes_scripts_only`.

### 2.3 Add managed-tool uninstall behind gobby uninstall --tools [category: code] (depends: 1.3, 2.2)

`kind: deliverable`

**Selector semantics.** `uninstall` today converts the absence of every CLI selector flag into `all_flag = True`, so a new `--tools` flag added without touching that condition would make `gobby uninstall --tools` remove every detected CLI hook alongside the managed tools — a scoped destructive request silently widened. `tools_flag` therefore participates in the implicit-default resolution: `--tools` alone performs managed-tool cleanup **only** and leaves every installed hook in place; no flags at all keeps today's implicit hook `--all`; and `--tools` combined with named CLI selectors or `--all` is additive, doing both. The command also carries a `--project` scope modifier, and it does not compose with `--tools`: managed tools, the launcher, the stamp, and the materialization cache are all home-global, so a project-scoped request cannot mean anything for them and must not silently delete them. `--tools` with `--project` is a `UsageError` regardless of `--all` or any named selector, and `--project`/`-C` continues to select only the hook project path. Tests cover `--tools` alone against a home with hooks installed (hooks survive), `--tools` with `--all`, `--tools` with one named CLI, and the three rejected project-scoped combinations.

Targets:
- `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py`
- `src/gobby/cli/install.py::uninstall`
- `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py`
- `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py`
- `tests/cli/test_install_coverage.py::*` — scope-reason: owns the uninstall command's existing selector cases, which the new flag's default resolution changes

Add `remove_impeccable_runtime()` to `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py` (the file created in 1.3), clearing **only impeccable-owned artifacts**: `~/.gobby/tools/impeccable/`, the `~/.gobby/bin/impeccable` launcher and its `.impeccable-version` stamp, and the impeccable skill's own materialization entries under `~/.gobby/cache/skill-scripts/<impeccable-skill-id>/`. Shared resources are preserved: other skills' materializations stay untouched, and the shared `~/.gobby/cache/puppeteer/` Chrome cache (usable by any consumer) is not deleted — reclaiming the roughly 1GB Chrome cache is a separately named global cleanup operation recorded as an epic follow-up in Constraints. Report each removed artifact; missing paths are quiet no-ops. A native-win32 home is a partial case of that rather than an empty one: 1.3's platform gate published no tool root, launcher, or stamp there, but materialization still writes a scripts-only cache on that platform (2.2.23), so the impeccable-owned materialization entries are present and removed while every managed-tool path is absent and reported as such. Deletion synchronizes with every producer: `remove_impeccable_runtime()` acquires the tool-install `export_file_lock` and the `<skill_id>`-root materializer lock — the identical stable lock identity every 2.2 publication and attach acquires, imported from `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py` — before removing anything, so a concurrent `gobby install` or materialization (including a publisher for a previously unseen content hash) serializes with deletion into one linearizable order: uninstall removes every artifact observable while it holds the locks, a producer serialized before it loses its artifact, and a producer serialized after it legitimately recreates state — last operation wins; uninstall's success report claims only the artifacts it observed and removed, never that nothing can exist afterwards. Root discovery itself is serialized by the shared namespace/discovery lock from `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py` (2.2): uninstall holds it across enumeration, marker validation, root-lock acquisition, and deletion, while producers hold it across first-root staging plus the atomic publication rename — empty-namespace enumeration is serialized against first-root publication, and the mkdir-before-marker window does not exist because a root becomes visible only as one rename of a fully staged, marker-carrying directory (2.2). A warm producer that waited on a root lock re-checks existence after acquiring and, if uninstall deleted the root, releases and restarts at namespace-then-root (2.2), so the global lock order — tool lock, then namespace lock, then skill-root locks — is never reversed. Materialization roots are removed only when their on-disk owner marker (written at materialize time, 2.2; read and validated via the same shared module) proves impeccable ownership: resolution never depends on a live DB row (hook-free or damaged homes, and the skill-creation UUID collision fallback make the id underivable) and never follows symlinks; an unmarked or malformed-marker root is skipped and reported. Holding the locks is not by itself proof that every writer has stopped, and this deliverable inherits the exact orphan path 1.3 records against: a killed installer or daemon has its file locks released by the kernel while its own detached npm or browser descendants keep writing. So before removing anything, uninstall reads the recorded writer ownership of every owned tool generation stage, dependency stage, and in-flight browser fetch beneath a root it is about to remove — the same records 1.3 and 2.2 write, read through the same shared module and under the same start-identity rule — and skips and reports any containing root with a live recorded writer, tombstoning and removing only once every recorded group beneath it is gone. Deletion itself is crash-atomic, mirroring publication (2.2): locks serialize live operations but cannot make recursive removal atomic against process death, and an interrupted removal that has already unlinked the owner marker would leave an unmarked live root the preservation rule then protects forever. So after ownership validates under the namespace-then-root locks, uninstall atomically renames the root to an ownership-preserving deletion tombstone — a reserved name pattern defined in `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py` beside the lock identities, itself proof of impeccable ownership — fsyncs the namespace directory, and only then recursively removes the tombstone. A crash mid-removal leaves a tombstone, never an unmarked live root; stale tombstones are finished (recursively removed) under the namespace lock by the same opportunistic cleanup that collects abandoned stages (2.2); and a producer that finds its root tombstoned or gone treats it as absent and republishes through the atomic first-root path.

In `src/gobby/cli/install.py`, extend the `uninstall` command with an opt-in `--tools` flag whose handler is a short call (~10 lines) into `remove_impeccable_runtime()`. Default uninstall behavior is unchanged. Flag reachability: the existing command returns successfully when no CLI hooks are installed — `--tools` handling must execute before (or as part of) that no-hooks early exit, so a hook-free installation still removes the requested tool artifacts instead of short-circuiting to success. Ceiling watch: `install.py` is at 955/1000 lines — only flag plumbing lands there, the net addition stays within the remaining 44 lines, and the file must stay under 1,000 lines.

**Acceptance:**

- 2.3.1 - `remove_impeccable_runtime()` clears the impeccable tool dir, launcher, stamp, and impeccable-owned materialization entries only — other skills' materializations and the shared puppeteer cache survive — reporting what it removed, and removing a root only once every recorded writer group beneath it has exited. file: `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py`.
- 2.3.2 - `--tools` participates in the implicit-default resolution rather than inheriting it: bare `gobby uninstall --tools` performs managed-tool cleanup only and leaves every installed hook in place, no flags at all keeps today's implicit hook `--all`, and `--tools` combined with `--all` or with a named CLI selector is additive — each combination pinned by its own case, so a scoped destructive request can never silently widen to hook removal; and `--tools` with `--project` raises a `UsageError` with or without `--all` or a named CLI selector, because every managed tool artifact is home-global and cannot be project-scoped. symbol: `uninstall`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_uninstall_tools_selector_matrix`.
- 2.3.3 - `src/gobby/cli/install.py` remains under 1,000 lines. file: `src/gobby/cli/install.py`.
- 2.3.4 - Cleanup behavior is pinned by tests: preservation of unrelated skill materializations and the shared puppeteer cache, producer-overlap synchronization (install-vs-uninstall, materialize-vs-uninstall including a publisher for a previously unseen content hash serialized by the shared `<skill_id>`-root lock, empty-namespace enumeration serialized against a cold materializer's atomic first-root publication — including under the skill-creation UUID-collision fallback id — and a deterministic warm-waiter-versus-delete case where the waiter finds the root gone after acquiring, releases, and restarts at namespace-then-root), marker-proven ownership (a random-UUID root with a valid marker is removed; unmarked or malformed-marker roots are preserved and reported), live-writer quiescence (uninstall run immediately after an installer parent and after a materializer parent are each SIGKILLed with their detached groups still writing skips and reports the containing root, and removes it only once those groups exit), crash-atomic deletion (killed after the tombstone rename but before removal completes: no unmarked live root exists, the tombstone alone remains, and a later uninstall or the opportunistic namespace-lock cleanup finishes removing it while producers treat the root as absent and republish atomically), and home-override resolution (with `GOBBY_HOME` pointing at a directory distinct from the user home, removal resolves the tool root, launcher, stamp, and materialization entries under the configured home and leaves the literal user-home dot-directory untouched — the uninstall half of the 1.3 lifecycle guarantee, testable here because this deliverable introduces the removal path). test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_remove_impeccable_runtime`.
- 2.3.5 - On a hook-free installation, `gobby uninstall --tools` still removes the tool artifacts instead of early-exiting to success. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_uninstall_tools_without_hooks`.
- 2.3.6 - On native win32, `gobby uninstall --tools` removes and reports the impeccable-owned scripts-only materialization cache that degraded materialization (2.2.23) leaves behind, while every managed-tool path is absent; a home that never materialized reports nothing removed. symbol: `remove_impeccable_runtime`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_native_win32_removes_degraded_cache`.

## P3: Upgrade tooling and reference adaptation

`kind: framing`

**Goal**: The vendor is upgradeable against the upstream release channel, and all 39 upstream reference files are adapted or vendored into the Gobby architecture.

### 3.1 Rewrite the .upgrade transform tooling for the release channel [category: code] (depends: 1.3, 2.1, 3.3)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/.upgrade/transform.py`
- `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/.upgrade/README.md`
- `src/gobby/install/impeccable-package-lock.json`
- `src/gobby/utils/dependency_requirements.py::*` — scope-reason: the transform rewrites the IMPECCABLE_RELEASE version, the node engine floor, and the lockfile hash so no installer pin can drift from the re-vendored release
- `src/gobby/install/manifest.py::should_include_bundled_file`
- `tests/install/test_bundled_content_manifest.py::*` — scope-reason: appends membership-parity cases proving the manifest enumerates exactly the shippable bundled files
- `src/gobby/install/bundled_content_manifest.json::*` — scope-reason: machine-regenerated bundled-content hash inventory rewritten by the manifest writer in every shared-tree commit
- `tests/skills/test_upgrade_transform.py`

The existing dot-prefixed (loader-skipped, packaging-excluded) upgrade tooling automated the original vendor's reference transforms but is stale for the new upstream layout. Rewrite `transform.py` and its README for the release-channel model:

1. **Stage and preflight**: the transform takes an exact-semver **candidate version** as a required positional argument naming the CLI release to vendor, with **no default** — omitting it is a usage error, and the same-release reproduction run (3.1.3) passes `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version` explicitly. A defaulted candidate would make the riskiest invocation the shortest one and leave the leaf unable to tell whether omission means "reproduce" or "upgrade"; a run that could only reinstall the already-pinned CLI could never discover, let alone publish, a new `cli.version` at all. The argument must match exact `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` — a range, a tag, or a partial version is rejected before staging. Install that candidate from npm into a temp harness directory to obtain the release artifact (never git HEAD), then validate every authority the later steps consume **before any destination file is written** — the staged installation's package name and resolved version against the requested candidate (an npm range or a registry redirect resolving to something else is a rejection, not a silent substitution), upstream's dependency pins, the lockfile inputs, and the representability of the `engines.node` constraint in both destination grammars (step 4). Every pin the run publishes — `cli.version`, `skill_release`, both node-floor fields, both lockfiles, and their hashes — derives from that one identity-verified staged candidate, so no destination field can name a release the run never staged. Any rejection aborts the whole run with a judgment-needed report and zero mutation. Preserving only the pin fields on a late rejection is not preserving a coherent release: new `scripts/**` and references paired with an old runtime declaration, installer pin, lockfile, and provenance are a vendored state no downstream gate would accept. Mutation begins only after preflight passes.
2. **Scripts**: wholesale-replace the vendored `scripts/**` from the staged output, preserving the Gobby-added dependency pin file while refreshing its dep pins from upstream's package manifest, and regenerating the vendored scripts lockfile against the refreshed pins so the locked graph tracks every re-vendor.
3. **References**: apply the mechanical transform set (session-continuation preamble merge, `{{scripts_path}}` → `<scripts_dir>` with resolver paragraph — including the `environment.PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR` export from 2.2's return contract — cross-reference expansion to `get_skill_file` loads, `DESIGN.md` → `.impeccable.md`, placeholder substitutions), then print a judgment-needed diff report for the curated files whose adaptations are catalogued in NOTICE.md.
4. **Pins**: bump `metadata.gobby.runtime` (`cli.version`, `skill_release`) and the NOTICE.md provenance lines; when `cli.version` changes, rewrite the authoritative installer pin in the same pass — `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version` **and** `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.lockfile_sha256` in `src/gobby/utils/dependency_requirements.py` — and regenerate the managed-CLI install lockfile `src/gobby/install/impeccable-package-lock.json` that hash covers. Updating the hash without the version is the exact failure this step exists to prevent: 4.1's pin guard asserts `cli.version == IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version` by exact equality, so a transform that moved only the skill declaration would leave the 1.3 installer requiring the superseded release and fail its own downstream guard. The Node engine floor is the same class of coupled pin and moves in the same pass, with an explicit authority and an explicit conversion. The authority is the identity-verified pinned CLI manifest inside the staged install — `node_modules/impeccable/package.json`, field `engines.node`. Its current literal is the two-component `>=22.18`, which neither destination accepts verbatim: `runtime.node` must match the `>=MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` grammar 1.1 validates, and `IMPECCABLE_NODE_MIN_VERSION` is a bare `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` with no comparator. Parse a single representable inclusive lower bound and zero-fill missing components: `>=22.18` becomes `runtime.node: ">=22.18.0"` and `IMPECCABLE_NODE_MIN_VERSION = "22.18.0"`. An absent field, a malformed constraint, or any shape without a single inclusive lower bound — a compound range, an upper bound, `^` or `~`, a pre-release tag — is rejected in step 1's preflight, before any destination write, and reported as judgment-needed rather than guessed; the parse is therefore a preflight check whose already-validated result this step merely applies. 4.1's guard asserts those two equal to each other, so a release that raises its floor while both Gobby fields stay stale passes that guard while the installer keeps accepting and reusing a Node version the new release does not support. 4.1's literal expectation is a deliberate second witness and is not machine-edited; the transform reports it in the judgment-needed diff instead.

**Manifest membership parity.** This deliverable owns the `.upgrade/` tree, and that tree is repository-only: setuptools' `install/shared/**/*` package-data glob does not match leading-dot path components, so `.upgrade/` never ships. The bundled-content manifest generator does not apply the same rule — `should_include_bundled_file` excludes only `__pycache__`, `.DS_Store`, and `.pyc`/`.pyo` — so the committed manifest already carries `skills/impeccable/.upgrade/README.md` and `skills/impeccable/.upgrade/transform.py`, two entries no wheel contains. A freshly installed distribution is therefore born with manifest entries for absent files, integrity marks them dirty, and the `skills` content type is classified as tampered before any deliberate mutation — which blocks ordinary production sync outright. Align the generator with packaging by construction: exclude any relative path component beginning with `.`, mirroring the glob semantics rather than special-casing this one directory, and regenerate the manifest in the same commit. Freshness probe: upstream update availability surfaces through `impeccable check` via the deps diagnostics from 1.3; the transform tool is run manually at re-vendor time. This section depends on 3.3 (and transitively 3.2) because the same-release reproduction run (3.1.3) compares transform output against the fully integrated reference state — with reference adaptation still in flight, its no-uncatalogued-diff criterion would be unstable; 4.1 already runs after this section.

A deterministic fixture test (new `tests/skills/test_upgrade_transform.py`) pins the transform's mechanical steps against a staged mini release tree: scripts replacement, preservation of the Gobby dependency pin file with refreshed dep pins, resolver and cross-reference transforms, runtime and NOTICE pin bumps, judgment-needed reporting, and an idempotent second execution. A simulated CLI-version bump enters through the candidate-version argument — the same input production uses — and asserts the complete pin tuple moves together — `runtime.cli.version`, `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version`, the regenerated lockfile, and `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.lockfile_sha256` hashing that exact file — so no member can be updated alone. A separate simulated engine-floor bump uses the real two-component source shape and asserts `runtime.node` and `IMPECCABLE_NODE_MIN_VERSION` both land in their own grammar with components zero-filled, that the 4.1 literal is reported as judgment-needed, and that an absent, malformed, or unrepresentable `engines.node` is rejected in preflight and reported as judgment-needed while the whole destination tree stays byte-for-byte unchanged — vendored scripts, references, SKILL.md, NOTICE.md, both lockfiles, the dependency constants, and the bundled-content manifest — not merely the pin fields. Candidate-argument cases cover the interface itself: an omitted argument is a usage error, a malformed or range-shaped version is rejected before staging, a staged package whose resolved version differs from the request is rejected as an identity failure rather than silently substituted, and passing the current pin explicitly reproduces the tree unchanged. A membership case asserts the regenerated manifest contains no leading-dot path component and matches the set of files the package-data globs would ship. The full same-release reproduction run (3.1.3) remains the integration proof.

**Acceptance:**

- 3.1.1 - The transform tool implements the 4-step release-channel flow (stage, scripts, references, pins). file: `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/.upgrade/transform.py`.
- 3.1.2 - The README documents the flow and the judgment-needed report. file: `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/.upgrade/README.md`.
- 3.1.3 - Invoked with `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version` passed explicitly as the candidate version, so the staged release is identical to the current pin, the tool reproduces `scripts/**` byte-identical and reports no judgment diffs beyond the adaptations catalogued in NOTICE.md. behavior: "upgrade flow" in `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/.upgrade/README.md`.
- 3.1.4 - The transform's mechanical steps are pinned by a deterministic fixture test, including idempotent re-execution, the complete release-pin tuple after a simulated CLI bump, and both node-floor fields normalized into their own grammars after a simulated engine-floor bump from the real two-component source literal, with the candidate version supplied as a required no-default positional argument whose omitted, malformed, range-shaped, and resolved-version-mismatch cases are each rejected before staging, and with unrepresentable constraints rejected in preflight before any destination write, leaving the whole vendored tree — scripts, references, SKILL.md, NOTICE.md, both lockfiles, dependency constants, and the bundled-content manifest — byte-for-byte unchanged. test: `tests/skills/test_upgrade_transform.py::test_transform_mini_release_idempotent`.
- 3.1.5 - The bundled-content manifest enumerates exactly the files a built wheel ships: the generator excludes every leading-dot path component the package-data globs also exclude, the regenerated manifest carries no `.upgrade/` entry, and a freshly installed wheel verifies clean so production sync is not blocked on an untampered install. symbol: `should_include_bundled_file`. test: `tests/install/test_bundled_content_manifest.py::test_manifest_membership_matches_wheel`.
