## P4: End-to-end validation

`kind: framing`

**Goal**: The whole chain — sync, manifest, CLI, materialize, engines, degradation, uninstall — is proven working against the vendored skill.

### 4.1 End-to-end validation pass and pin-consistency test [category: test] (depends: 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 3.1, 3.2, 3.3)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `tests/skills/test_impeccable_pins.py`

New `tests/skills/test_impeccable_pins.py`: a pin-consistency test that builds one canonical expected pin object from the governing constants and asserts **exact equality on every field**: `cli.npm == IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.package`, `cli.version == IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version`, `cli.bin == "impeccable"`, `node == ">=22.18.0"` (asserted equal to the `IMPECCABLE_NODE_MIN_VERSION` floor so the installer's engine gate and the skill declaration cannot drift), and `skill_release == "4.0.4"` exactly (not merely well-formed); exact equality of the vendored `scripts/package.json` dependency and optional-dependency mappings against the literal expected values recorded in 2.1 (`css-select ^7.0.0`, `css-tree ^3.2.1`, `domutils ^4.0.2`, `fflate ^0.8.3`, `htmlparser2 ^12.0.0`, `marked ^18.0.5`; optional `puppeteer ^25.1.0`); and the vendored managed-CLI install lockfile hashing to `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.lockfile_sha256` — the guard that keeps installer, skill, and dependency pins from drifting apart in any field. Lockfile shippability is not asserted here — the package-data declaration and the installer's package-relative resolution are proven by the installer suite that owns them (1.3.13); this section corroborates them against a real build artifact in matrix step 8. The reference inventory is guarded **bidirectionally against independent expected sets, each a literal in the test and none derived from an artifact under test**: `RELEASE_REFERENCES` pins the released 4.0.4 inventory (the 35 top-level reference files plus the four `degraded/` files, 39 in all) and asserts set equality with the vendored `references/` tree modulo an explicit itemized allowlist of Gobby-retained extras (the seven steering references kept from the original vendor: color-and-contrast, interaction-design, motion-design, responsive-design, spatial-design, typography, ux-writing); `DISPATCHABLE_REFERENCES` pins which references must appear in the SKILL.md dispatch table and asserts bidirectional equality with the table's actual entries; and `EXPECTED_NOTICE_CLASSIFICATIONS` pins the per-file divergence classification and asserts bidirectional equality with the NOTICE.md catalogue — deriving any expected set from the dispatch table or NOTICE under test would let the same omission vanish from both sides. A resolver-environment guard rounds this out: every vendored reference whose body invokes `node <scripts_dir>` must also instruct exporting the materializer's returned `environment.PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR` — asserted mechanically over the vendored `references/` tree, so a 3.2/3.3 adaptation cannot drop the export without failing the pin suite. A lifecycle-ownership guard gives Constraints item 11 the same treatment, because a user-confirmed prohibition that lives only in prose is one refresh away from being undone: asserted mechanically over SKILL.md and the whole vendored `references/` tree, no file instructs running upstream `impeccable install` or `impeccable update` against the bundled skill, and every setup or maintenance instruction routes through Gobby (`gobby install`, teach mode) — upstream lifecycle commands are admissible only where the surrounding text scopes them to the repository-only re-vendor harness (3.1). Every inventory, dispatch, and provenance guard can pass on a tree that has quietly reacquired upstream self-management; this one cannot.

The three expected memberships are specified here, so the implementer never reconstructs them from the artifacts under test (source: the staged 4.0.4 inventory and Commands table, pinned 2026-08-06, adjusted by this plan's dispatch decisions). `RELEASE_REFERENCES` (39) is the 35 top-level names — adapt, adapt.native, android, animate, audit, audit.native, bolder, clarify, colorize, craft, craft-floor, critique, delight, distill, doctor, document, extract, harden, hooks, init, ios, layout, live, live-setup, new-work, onboard, operate, optimize, overdrive, polish, quieter, routing, shape, typeset, visualize — plus the four degraded entries: asset-producer, documenter, finish-reviewer, manual-edit-applier. `DISPATCHABLE_REFERENCES` (25, the staged Commands-table reference column): craft, shape, init, document, extract, critique, audit, audit.native, polish, bolder, quieter, distill, harden, onboard, animate, colorize, typeset, layout, delight, overdrive, clarify, adapt, adapt.native, optimize, live. `EXPECTED_NOTICE_CLASSIFICATIONS` assigns every file exactly one class, matching the provenance catalogue's vocabulary (the same catalogue the guard paragraph above asserts against): refreshed-with-catalogued-adaptations (the nineteen 2.1 references: craft-floor, operate, adapt, animate, audit, bolder, clarify, colorize, critique, delight, distill, harden, layout, optimize, overdrive, polish, quieter, shape, typeset), named-default-adapted (init, document, hooks, routing, craft, new-work from 3.2; live and live-setup from 3.3), standard-adapted (onboard, visualize, extract, doctor), near-verbatim (ios, android, adapt.native, audit.native), vendored-as-is (the four degraded entries), and gobby-retained-extra (the seven steering references, release-external). Counts: 19 + 8 + 4 + 4 + 4 = 39 release files plus 7 extras.

Execute and record the end-to-end matrix in the task session against an **isolated test daemon**, per the repo contract that daemon-dependent validation never touches the user's live daemon: start a dedicated daemon with temporary Gobby state (an overridden Gobby home via `GOBBY_HOME` — the canonical `get_gobby_home()` override that every managed-tool and cache path in this plan derives from, 1.3) and dynamically allocated ports; route every MCP assertion to that daemon, noting that `gobby sync` is not a daemon call — the CLI process opens its own bootstrap-configured database connection, which is why the bootstrap file below governs both; serve HTTP/browser fixtures on separate temporary ports; keep every filesystem output (materialization cache, critique data) under temporary test roots; and record the isolation parameters alongside the results. Filesystem isolation alone is insufficient, because this workflow also touches the hub database and the operator's shell profile: the daemon runs against an **isolated test database**, not merely a separate schema inside the live hub. `tests/fixtures/postgres.py` reduces a DSN to a `(host, port, dbname)` identity and fails any run whose DSN matches the operator's bootstrap hub identity — schema is not part of that comparison, so a unique `gobby_test_*` schema inside the live hub database is rejected outright under `GOBBY_TEST_PROTECT=1`. Use the repository's established isolated test database (the `docker compose -f docker-compose.test.yml up -d postgres-test` instance that fixture's own guidance names). The requirement is the fixture's own: the **normalized** `(host, port, dbname)` tuple must differ from the operator hub's — loopback spellings collapse to a single host, so a test instance legitimately shares `localhost` and differs by port and database name. Requiring all three fields to differ would reject the repository's own configuration; requiring only a different schema would be rejected by the guard. Allocate a unique `gobby_test_*` schema in that database for the daemon, reset and dropped on teardown. `HOME` points at a separate temporary directory, because the installer's PATH step appends lines to shell rc files under the user's home and would otherwise edit the operator's real profile. Two DSNs derive from that instance, and they go to different consumers. A **base DSN** carries no query string; it creates and drops the daemon schema and is what step 7 passes to pytest as `DATABASE_URL`, because `postgres_db` builds its own connection string by appending `?options=-csearch_path%3D<worker-schema>` to whatever it is given — handing it an already-scoped DSN yields two `?` query strings and a malformed URL, and pytest's schema is fixture-allocated per worker rather than shared with the daemon. A **schema-scoped DSN** — the base DSN plus a URI-encoded `options=-c search_path=<daemon-schema>` parameter — is written into the temporary Gobby home's bootstrap file at mode `0600` before anything launches, and it is what the daemon and every `gobby` CLI invocation use. `DATABASE_URL` is a pytest-fixture input, never daemon configuration: bootstrap loading resolves the runtime hub DSN from the Gobby home's bootstrap file and raises when it is absent, with no `DATABASE_URL` fallback anywhere on that path, so exporting it alone would leave the isolated daemon and every CLI invocation either failing to start or pointed at whatever bootstrap file they do find — and a DSN without the scoped `search_path` would silently operate in the test database's `public` schema. Placement is proved from the data the matrix already creates rather than from a diagnostic surface that does not exist — no `gobby` command or daemon API reports `current_schema` today, and inventing one is outside this plan. After step 1's baseline sync, which the CLI process writes through its own bootstrap-configured connection, assert through the daemon's MCP surface that the bundled skill resolves, then query the base DSN directly and assert the skill row exists in the recorded daemon schema and does **not** exist in `public`. One CLI writer, one daemon reader, and one out-of-band witness together prove both processes are in the recorded schema; a silent fall back fails the matrix at step 1 instead of invalidating every downstream result. Record the test database identity and the daemon schema identifier alongside the state root and ports; pytest's worker schemas are fixture-owned and recorded from its output:

1. **Sync**: a non-development daemon performs no bundled startup sync, so this row establishes its own baseline before asserting anything. Order it strictly: build the wheel and install it into a throwaway virtual environment outside the checkout (step 8 reuses the same venv); confirm installed-wheel manifest parity is clean (3.1.5); launch the isolated daemon with that venv's `gobby` from a working directory outside the source tree; assert up front that **no** bundled skill revision exists yet; run that venv's `gobby sync --force --type skills` against the isolated database to create the baseline revision. Only then assert the shape: `get_skill(name="impeccable")` returns the collapsed scripts summary reporting **109** script files (the 107 released files plus the two Gobby-added dependency pin files — the loader classifies everything under `scripts/` as script), and an exhaustive `get_skill_files` pagination walk (`path_prefix="scripts/"`, iterating `after_path`) asserts exact membership of those 109 paths. The drift proof runs against an **installed package copy**, never the checked-out tree and never an unwired temp root: `get_bundled_skills_path()` is `get_install_dir() / "shared" / "skills"`, and `get_install_dir()` walks parents preferring any `src/gobby/install` it finds, so a daemon launched from this checkout resolves to the checkout regardless of what was copied, and no bundled-root override exists. Ordinary `gobby sync` runs the integrity check that an intentionally mutated tree fails, so the drift edge uses the same explicitly test-only forced sync as the baseline. Save the original bytes of one named script — the vendored critique-storage entry point — append a harmless trailing comment, re-run `gobby sync --force --type skills`, record the daemon's resolved `get_bundled_skills_path()` and assert it equals the mutated installed-package copy, and assert the row reports `updated`, not `skipped`, with refreshed stored bytes (1.1 proof). **Then restore before continuing**: write the saved original bytes back, re-verify installed-wheel manifest parity is clean, and force-sync once more so the stored revision is the canonical released one. Steps 2 through 9 assert properties of the released 4.0.4 artifact and would otherwise be measuring deliberately corrupted bytes and a drifted database revision. Afterwards assert the repository worktree status is unchanged and the checked-out vendored source hashes are untouched.
2. **Manifest**: the get_skill response inlines references without `content_hash` and collapses `scripts/**`; file-metadata token cost is bounded (~2K tokens, down from 7–9K projected) — the manual corroboration of the 1.2.5 guard test.
3. **CLI**: the installer smoke check runs `gobby install` against the overridden temporary Gobby home; the promoted launcher runs with `--help`; the receipt pins 3.5.0; a second `gobby install` is a verified no-op; `gobby status` shows the managed tool; `impeccable check` answers the freshness probe.
4. **Materialize + full engines**: `materialize_skill_scripts(name="impeccable")` installs deps into the cache; `node <scripts_dir>/detect.mjs --json` on fixtures catches multi-line gradient-text via the parser engine (missed in degraded mode), flags a flat type ladder with `--scope type`, exercises parser-backed layout rules with `--scope layout`, and exits 0 on a clean fixture; a puppeteer URL scan against a local `uv run python -m http.server` fixture on a temporary port returns browser-engine findings — run with the default puppeteer cache empty and only the Gobby cache populated, the detector process exporting the tool's returned `environment.PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR`, proving the fetched browser is the one actually resolved; the critique-storage script round-trips slug→write→latest→trend from a temporary working directory (snapshots land under its `.impeccable/critique/`).
5. **Degradation**: run from **cold cache state**, not after step 4 — materialization is content-addressed, so reusing step 4's skill identity and content hash would attach that step's ready `node_modules` and never exercise this path at all. Both conditions this row needs — a fresh Gobby home and a PATH without npm — are read inside the process that materializes, which is the daemon, so no client-side change can establish them against the already-running one. Run this row through a **second isolated daemon**: its own temporary `GOBBY_HOME`, `HOME`, ports, and bootstrap (schema-scoped as above), launched with a PATH that exposes node and excludes npm. Seed it with a distinct skill identity and a distinct content hash, assert up front that no dependency readiness marker and no `node_modules` exist for it, then route the degradation call to that daemon: materialize returns the cache dir with `parser_deps.installed` false and a warning, nothing is attached, and the detector still runs line-based. Record both daemon identities and tear both down.
6. **Uninstall**: `gobby uninstall --tools` against the temporary Gobby home removes the impeccable tool dir, launcher, stamp, and impeccable-owned materializations while preserving the shared puppeteer cache and unrelated materializations, and works on a hook-free home; default uninstall unchanged.
7. **Focused tests**: run exactly this command — the file list is enumerated here rather than described, because this leaf receives only section 4.1 at expansion and must never fall back to the repository's prohibited full suite:

   ```bash
   GOBBY_TEST_PROTECT=1 DATABASE_URL=<the isolated test DSN recorded above> uv run pytest \
     tests/storage/test_skill_sync.py \
     tests/storage/skills/test_skill_metadata.py \
     tests/storage/skills/test_skill_files.py \
     tests/skills/test_parser.py \
     tests/skills/test_updater.py \
     tests/skills/test_upgrade_transform.py \
     tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py \
     tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill_serve_scan.py \
     tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_install_skill.py \
     tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_install_skill_scanning.py \
     tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py \
     tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_registry.py \
     tests/servers/routes/test_skills_routes.py \
     tests/cli/test_skills_cli.py \
     tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py \
     tests/cli/test_install_coverage.py \
     tests/cli/test_install_setup.py \
     tests/cli/test_install_setup_gdaemon.py \
     tests/cli/test_install_ghook.py \
     tests/utils/test_dependency_requirements.py \
     tests/utils/test_deps.py \
     tests/utils/test_status.py \
     tests/install/test_bundled_content_manifest.py \
     tests/skills/test_impeccable_pins.py -v
   ```

   This step runs **without** the matrix's `HOME` and `GOBBY_HOME` overrides, in the ordinary operator environment the repository's suites already run in. The overrides exist for the daemon and CLI rows; carrying them here would point `_live_hub_identity()` at the temporary daemon bootstrap, so the fixture would compare `DATABASE_URL` against the test database itself — matching on the normalized identity that ignores the schema parameter — and refuse the run before collection. With the operator bootstrap resolvable, the guard does what it exists to do: it proves the focused suites are not pointed at the operator's hub. That guard is conditional by construction, though — it compares only when the operator's bootstrap loads and both DSNs parse, and returns silently otherwise, so a missing, unreadable, or malformed operator bootstrap turns it into a no-op and the step would run with no isolation proof at all while this section claims one. Precede it with a matrix-local preflight in the same ordinary operator environment: resolve the operator's configured hub DSN and the isolated base DSN, normalize both to `(host, port, dbname)`, and abort the matrix unless both resolve and the two differ. A missing or malformed operator bootstrap, an unparseable DSN on either side, and equal identities each abort; only two distinct parsed identities proceed. The preflight is matrix-local and adds no input to the shared test fixture, which keeps behaving exactly as it does today. The DSN passed here is the **base** DSN recorded above, never the daemon's schema-scoped one — stated explicitly because the PostgreSQL-backed cases (1.2.9, 1.2.11, 2.2.19) fail closed against the live hub identity, and passed unscoped because `postgres_db` appends its own worker-schema `options` parameter to it. Then run the repository's whole-tree gates verbatim — `uv run ruff check src/` and `uv run mypy src/` — rather than a reconstructed per-file list: both are bounded, both are the commands the repo already gates on, and neither requires this leaf to know which production files the earlier phases touched; both clean.
8. **Distribution parity**: using the same freshly built wheel installed into the throwaway virtual environment from step 1, the installer's lockfile resolution runs against that installed package — the lockfile is present at its package-relative path, opens, and checksum-validates against `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.lockfile_sha256`. Archive membership alone would not prove the installed runtime can read it, and running from the checkout would not prove it either; this corroborates the 1.3.13 guard against a real build artifact.
9. **Bundled-content manifest parity**: `src/gobby/install/bundled_content_manifest.json` matches a fresh regeneration exactly — the existing exact parity test `tests/install/test_bundled_content_manifest.py::test_bundled_content_manifest_matches_tree` passes — every shared-tree-editing commit already regenerated it in the same commit under the `bundled-content-manifest-freshness` pre-commit hook (Constraints), and this check proves none was missed.

**Acceptance:**

- 4.1.1 - The exact-equality pin guard, the bidirectional reference-inventory guard, the resolver-environment guard, and the lifecycle-ownership guard — no vendored SKILL.md section or reference instructing upstream `impeccable install`/`update` against the bundled skill, every setup and maintenance path routed through Gobby — all pass against the literal set memberships, export rule, and ownership boundary enumerated in this section. test: `tests/skills/test_impeccable_pins.py::test_bundled_runtime_matches_release_pin`. test: `tests/skills/test_impeccable_pins.py::test_vendored_tree_never_self_manages_upstream`.
- 4.1.2 - The end-to-end matrix (sync drift, manifest shape, CLI, materialize, full engines, cold-state degradation, uninstall) is executed against an isolated test daemon whose state root, ports, and `HOME` are all temporary and unique, and whose database is a test database whose normalized `(host, port, dbname)` identity differs from the operator's bootstrap hub — never the operator's home, shell profile, hub database, or a schema inside it, and a differing schema alone is not sufficient — with a unique `gobby_test_*` schema allocated for the daemon, both identifiers recorded alongside the state root and ports, the schema-scoped DSN written into the temporary Gobby home's bootstrap file at mode `0600` because daemon and CLI startup resolve the hub DSN from that file and honor no `DATABASE_URL` fallback, the unscoped base DSN passed to pytest because the fixtures append their own worker-schema parameter, and schema placement proved before the matrix continues by resolving the baseline-synced skill through the daemon while an out-of-band base-DSN query finds its row in the recorded schema and not in `public`; the focused pytest step runs outside the `HOME`/`GOBBY_HOME` overrides so the fixture's live-hub guard compares against the operator's own bootstrap rather than the temporary daemon's, and a matrix-local preflight makes that proof fail closed by aborting unless the operator's configured hub DSN and the isolated base DSN both resolve to parseable normalized identities that differ — a missing, malformed, or unparseable identity on either side aborts rather than letting the fixture's guard pass vacuously. behavior: "the numbered end-to-end matrix" in this section's matrix.
- 4.1.3 - The materialized-cache execution model works end to end as documented. behavior: "Materialize + full engines" in this section's matrix.
- 4.1.4 - The committed bundled-content manifest matches a fresh regeneration with no diff. behavior: "Bundled-content manifest parity" in this section's matrix. test: `tests/install/test_bundled_content_manifest.py::test_bundled_content_manifest_matches_tree`.

Repairs: 2.2 gained the 1.1 dependency and 3.3 gained 3.2; 2.1 Targets enumerate the full staged 4.0.4 inventory (132 entries: 107 script files, 19 refreshed references, pin and lock files, manifest, .gitignore) with the delta work set pinned at planning time; 3.2 enumerates all seventeen reference files including craft.md and the four degraded/ files; runtime-metadata validation moved to a shared helper covering SkillManager create/update with defensive re-validation in the materializer; the get_skill manifest caps non-script listings with overflow counts; a vendored scripts lockfile plus npm ci makes the cache key reproduce one locked graph (3.1 regenerates it on re-vendor); 2.2 publication became an immutable two-step state machine (scripts publish, deps attach) with process-group termination, a shared puppeteer-cache lock, independent parser_deps/browser/node return states, and an owner.json cache marker; 2.3 uninstall acquires both producer locks and removes only marker-proven roots; the pin test asserts exact equality on every governing field and guards the reference inventory bidirectionally against an independent pinned set with a Gobby-retained allowlist; bundled_content_manifest.json regeneration is bound per-commit via the pre-commit hook with 4.1 verifying final parity. Amendments recorded in votes: per-commit manifest regeneration (F015), 3.1 lockfile regeneration (F008), minimal-mechanism locking and marker implementations (F011, F012, F014). New acceptance items: 1.1.7, 2.2.9, 2.2.10, 2.2.11, 4.1.4.

- resolution_notes: All 8 findings accepted by the user (2026-08-06, "good on these") and repaired in place; every load-bearing repository claim was verified before adoption (the optional-dependencies status loop renders raw names with managed defaulting to False while the labels map lives only in the required loop, and TestStatusUtils.test_format_status_message_deps exists at tests/utils/test_status.py:250; _serve_scan_error scans one content string per call; storage get_skill_files is an unlimited ordered SELECT; the round-3 Dependency-install bullet contradicted its own single-rename state machine; plain npx auto-installs an unpinned package when the local binary is absent). Repairs: the bounded get_skill_files surface gained keyset pagination — after_path/limit as an ordered server-side LIMIT in storage, after_path/next_after_path with a bounded COUNT in the MCP tool, and a same-prefix multi-page walk pinned by new acceptance 1.2.7; acceptance 3.2.2 now assigns the standard transforms to extract.md and the dispatch-alias adaptation to craft.md, completing all eighteen files; section 4.1 enumerates RELEASE_REFERENCES (35 top-level plus four degraded names), DISPATCHABLE_REFERENCES (25, grounded in the staged 4.0.4 Commands-table reference column), and EXPECTED_NOTICE_CLASSIFICATIONS (a six-class grouping, 19+8+4+4+4=39 release files plus 7 gobby-retained extras) as in-plan literals; section 1.3 targets tests/utils/test_status.py and requires the optional render loop to apply the shared labels map and managed policy, pinned in 1.3.4; script_cache.py gained a namespace/discovery lock closing both cold-race interleavings between marker-scan uninstall discovery and first-root creation, with tool-then-namespace-then-root ordering and races pinned in 2.3.4; the materializer's serve-scan gate now iterates SKILL.md plus every DB-loaded scripts payload fail-closed for external sources, pinned by new acceptance 2.2.12; npm ci runs only in the isolated sibling dependency stage with the staged node_modules (marker inside) renamed into the generation, with fault injection proving no pre-attach node_modules; and every Chrome fetch — installer and materializer — invokes the local node_modules/.bin/puppeteer directly with warn-only missing-executable handling, with the Decision Record item 6 echo updated to match. Amendments recorded in votes: F001 keyset form, F002 grouping, F003 grouped-literal enumeration, F008 extended to the materializer and the Constraints echo. New acceptance items: 1.2.7, 2.2.12.

- resolution_notes: All 12 findings verified before acceptance and repaired; the user delegated finding judgment to this session for unattended rounds. Repo verification: pyproject.toml package-data enumerates install/srt-package-lock.json explicitly and otherwise ships only install/shared, so a new lockfile would miss the wheel (F004); three production callers invoke _ensure_gobby_bin_on_path() with no argument (F005); LocalSkillManager.update_skill_with_files issues its own metadata UPDATE outside the named mixin chokepoints (F006) and already replaces metadata plus the complete file set in one transaction while sync commits them separately (F007); dispatch routing sends docs-category tasks to tech-writer and the m0-shared-datastores-bridge ledger uses tech-writer for its docs section (F002); the repository's E2E daemon fixture allocates a unique gobby_test_* schema the matrix did not require (F012). Repairs: acceptance 2.1.5 and its ledger row removed in favor of an explicit pre-expansion handoff gate; the ledger generator now derives owner_agent from each section's category; 1.3.11 narrowed to install-side paths with the uninstall home-override assertion moved to 2.3.4; pyproject.toml added to Targets with the lockfile package-data pattern and a wheel-corroborated guard; the three PATH-helper callers added with by-value argument assertions; the shared runtime validator enforced at update_skill_with_files, which also becomes sync's single atomic publication seam (empty set included); the storage page contract gained exclude_file_type, cursor-relative counts, and one read transaction across listing, COUNT, and aggregate; reuse now verifies executable identity and containment rather than presence; shared browser readiness became compatibility-keyed by platform, Puppeteer version, expected build, and channel; matrix step 5 runs from asserted-cold state; and the matrix now requires a unique schema-scoped hub DSN and a temporary HOME. New acceptance items: 1.1.9, 1.3.12, 1.3.13, 2.2.18; removed: 2.1.5. Ledger regenerated at 75 items.

- resolution_notes: All 7 findings verified before acceptance and repaired; the user delegated finding judgment to this session for unattended rounds. Six of the seven trace to round-7 repairs that assumed a seam or an owner without checking it. Repo verification: LocalSkillManager.update_skill_with_files UPDATEs only seven columns while _handle_existing_gobby_skill also writes always_apply, injection_format, and a branch-selected enabled and reaches restore through a separate storage.restore call (F003); _sync_single_skill's new-row branch calls create_skill then _persist_skill_files in two transactions (F006); SkillUpdater._has_changes guards its hash comparison on truthy parsed.loaded_files and _apply_update passes files=None for an empty inventory, while ParsedSkill.loaded_files is typed list | None so the two states are already distinguishable (F007); HubDatabase.transaction opens psycopg conn.transaction() with no isolation override and src/gobby contains no REPEATABLE READ, isolation_level, or SET TRANSACTION, so READ COMMITTED gives each statement its own snapshot (F004); tests/skills/test_impeccable_pins.py is 4.1's sole Target and 4.1 depends on 1.3 (F001). Repairs: the update seam widens by exactly the missing field gap with an atomic deleted_at clear and the restore branch calls it directly; a matching create_skill_with_files seam covers the create branch; the updater's two symbols and its existing suite join 1.1 with the loaded-but-empty versus not-loaded distinction made explicit; each bounded 1.2 response becomes one composite CTE statement rather than a multi-statement transaction, with standalone seams kept for single-value callers; the materializer gains one composite skill-row-plus-inventory read; script_cache.py moves to 1.3 as the first producer of the shared browser cache with 2.2 extending it; and 1.3.13's proof moves into the installer suite as a package-data declaration plus package-relative resolution assertion, with matrix step 8 strengthened to install the built wheel and exercise that resolution. New acceptance items: 1.1.10, 1.3.14, 2.2.19. Ledger regenerated at 78 items.

- PR9-F008 / blocking / 2.2 external-dependency-execution-trust
- PR9-F009 / blocking / 2.2 async-materialization-blocking-io
- PR9-F010 / blocking / 1.3 crash-safe-managed-tool-promotion

- resolution_notes: All 13 findings verified before acceptance and repaired; the user delegated finding judgment to this session for unattended rounds. Coverage reconciled clean at 10 sections / 78 items against the companion ledger; the adversary correctly identified the per-section tuple in the round-9 prompt (2.3=6, 3.1=6) as prompt metadata drift rather than an artifact mismatch, and the artifact's own 2.3=5 / 3.1=4 stand. Nine findings were confirmed by direct code inspection: create_skill's scope normalization, bundled-template rejection, uuid5-with-uuid4-fallback identity, UniqueViolation translation, and post-commit notification (F001); the concrete import cycle through skills/__init__ line 11 to loader line 27 against the partially initialized storage package (F002); exclude_license defaulting True at _files.py:148 with updater.py:390 passing False (F003); get_by_name's project-first/global-fallback/deleted-filter/bundled-shadow-rejection semantics (F006); scan_skill_content's whole-surface files= entry point that scan_parsed_skill already delegates to (F009); _promote_install's target-to-backup-then-staging-to-target window at install_setup_srt.py:168-170 (F010); the (host, port, dbname) DSN identity in tests/fixtures/postgres.py that ignores schema (F012); and get_bundled_skills_path/get_install_dir's checkout-preferring resolution with no override (F013). Four were confirmed against the plan text: the separate get_skill parent-row read left behind by the PR8-F004 repair (F004), example-only caps with an unstated byte ceiling and unbounded path width (F005), the scripts-only generation digest paired with a write-once skill_release marker introduced by the PR8-F005 repair (F007), and the unscanned node_modules executed after a fail-closed scan gate (F008). Four of the thirteen trace directly to round-8 repairs. Repairs: one transaction-aware internal constructor behind both public create seams; function-local validator imports with fresh-subprocess import-order proof; exclude_license carried through every shared predicate and signature; a composite get_skill row-plus-manifest statement; exact MAX_MANIFEST_FILE_ENTRIES/MAX_MANIFEST_DIRECTORY_ENTRIES/MAX_SKILL_FILE_PATH_BYTES/MAX_MANIFEST_RESPONSE_BYTES constants with byte-measured pagination and an omitted-and-counted legacy policy; get_by_name's exact selector semantics encoded in the materializer's resolving CTE; an immutable-ownership root marker with per-generation provenance and runtime metadata folded into the generation digest; dependency install and browser execution restricted to non-external sources with the operator-approval tier explicitly deferred to Constraints; one whole-surface scan plus every synchronous phase in workers with cancellation-safe lock release; immutable generation directories behind an atomically swapped version pointer; IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version added to the transform's pin tuple and its source file to 3.1 Targets; the isolated test database replacing the live-hub schema with an explicit DATABASE_URL on the focused command; and the drift proof moved onto the installed-wheel venv the matrix already builds. New acceptance items: 1.1.11, 1.2.10, 1.2.11, 1.3.15, 2.2.20, 2.2.21, 2.2.22. Ledger regenerated at 85 items. Contract validation passes (10 deliverables, 4 phases, 0 warnings); symbol validation remains unavailable environment-wide because another session's in-flight gdaemon/schema work has the code-index tables down, and every new target was file-wide with a scope-reason.

Repaired by routing both public constructors through one transaction-aware internal constructor carrying the complete contract plus the shared runtime validation, with file insertion in the same transaction and notification only after each wrapper's own commit; 1.1.7 gains the fourth chokepoint, 1.1.9 gains the parity clause, and the storage-seam tests cover every contract element plus rollback-without-notification.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR9-F002","note":"Verified the concrete cycle: gobby/skills/__init__.py line 11 eagerly imports gobby.skills.loader, whose line 27 does 'from gobby.storage.skills import SkillSourceType', while gobby/storage/skills/__init__.py binds LocalSkillManager (line 13) before _models (line 14) - so a module-scope parser import inside _metadata.py or _manager.py fails on the storage-first cold-import order. _manager.py already carries an 'avoid circular import' comment on its notifier parameter for the same hazard. Repaired with a composite get_skill-row-plus-bounded-manifest seam doing project-aware resolution, the capped non-script page, and the scripts summary in one SQL statement and one ctx.run_db call, plus a PostgreSQL race case; new acceptance 1.2.11.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR9-F005","note":"Verified in the plan text: the caps were written as examples ('e.g. 100', 'e.g. 20') and 1.2.5 promised 'a concrete serialized byte ceiling' without stating one, while skill_files imposes no path-length bound - so 100 entries of unbounded path width is an unbounded response and the criterion is unfalsifiable. Repaired with exact constants: MAX_MANIFEST_FILE_ENTRIES=100, MAX_MANIFEST_DIRECTORY_ENTRIES=20, MAX_SKILL_FILE_PATH_BYTES=1024 enforced at the storage write seam already in this section's Targets, and MAX_MANIFEST_RESPONSE_BYTES=16384 with pagination stopping on measured encoded bytes as well as entry count. Oversized legacy rows are omitted-and-counted with a warning rather than truncated into unresolvable paths; maximum-width and one-byte-over cases added.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR9-F006","note":"Verified in code: SkillMetadataMixin.get_by_name performs project-scoped selection first, global fallback under include_global, deleted_at IS NULL filtering, and explicit rejection of a project row whose source_path is a bundled template path (the #17606 stale-shadow rule), while the round-8 composite seam specified only 'a single SQL statement over skills and skill_files'. Repaired by defining the seam over name and project_id with those exact selector semantics encoded in its resolving CTE, plus a parity case asserting materialize_skill_scripts and get_skill resolve the same row across all four shapes; 2.2.19 updated.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR9-F007","note":"Verified the contradiction the PR8-F005 repair introduced: the generation digest covers only the sorted script-file hash map, and the root owner marker carrying skill_release is written once at first materialization - so a metadata-only revision reuses the generation and keeps the first-ever release forever, falsifying 2.2.19's 'owner-marker provenance always from one revision'. Repaired by keeping only immutable ownership facts in the write-once root marker, moving mutable revision provenance to a per-generation record written with the bytes it describes, and folding the normalized runtime metadata into the generation digest; new acceptance 2.2.20.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR9-F008","note":"Accepted: the fail-closed scan authorizes SKILL.md and the stored scripts payloads, then npm ci downloads an executable graph selected by a lockfile that is itself one of those external files, and the materializer executes node_modules/.bin/puppeteer out of that never-scanned tree - integrity hashes authenticate what the lockfile named without making it trusted. Repaired with the smaller of the two available mechanisms: dependency installation and browser-binary execution are restricted to non-external sources, and an external skill still materializes scripts-only through the same scan gate with false parser_deps/browser states and no npm or .bin process spawned. The operator-approved external allowlist the finding also proposed is recorded as a Constraints follow-up rather than built - no skill in scope needs it. New acceptance 2.2.21.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR9-F009","note":"Verified in code: scanner.py already exposes scan_skill_content(content, name, files=...) - the whole-surface entry point scan_parsed_skill delegates to - while the plan specified iterating the one-content serve scanner over SKILL.md and every DB-loaded payload, which is 109 synchronous scanner invocations (each materializing a temp file via _materialize_scan_file) on the daemon event loop. Repaired by calling the whole-surface entry point once inside a worker before any cache tree exists, and by requiring cold staging, stale collection, fsync, and publication to run in workers too, with cancellation awaiting the in-flight worker to a safe boundary before releasing its lock; new acceptance 2.2.22.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR9-F010","note":"Verified in code: _promote_install (install_setup_srt.py:163-173) renames the live target to '.<name>.previous' at line 168 and only then renames staging into place at line 170, with rollback at 173 - a genuine target-absent window that no except clause covers and that leaves the launcher's exec target missing. The asymmetry is internal too: 2.2 already rejects lock-serialized-but-not-crash-atomic publication and uses a single rename. Repaired inside this deliverable's own new installer without touching the SRT one: immutable generation directories with a stable version pointer swapped by one atomic os.replace, old generation retained until the swap is durable, and every executing and inspecting path resolving through the pointer; kill-point and concurrent launcher cases added; new acceptance 1.3.15.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR9-F011","note":"Verified in the plan: step 4 bumped runtime cli.version, skill_release, NOTICE provenance, the managed lockfile, and IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.lockfile_sha256 - but never IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version, and dependency_requirements.py was not a 3.1 Target, while 4.1.1 asserts cli.version == IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version by exact equality. The transform could not satisfy its own downstream guard. Repaired by adding the file-wide target with a scope-reason, requiring version and lockfile hash to move together with the skill declaration, and extending the existing idempotency fixture to assert the complete pin tuple after a simulated bump - no new acceptance item needed.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR9-F012","note":"Verified in code: tests/fixtures/postgres.py reduces a DSN to a (host, port, dbname) identity and fails any run matching the operator's bootstrap hub identity; schema is not part of that comparison, so the plan's 'uniquely named test schema in the hub PostgreSQL instance' is rejected outright under GOBBY_TEST_PROTECT=1. Repaired by requiring the repository's isolated test database (differing host/port/dbname) with a unique gobby_test_* schema allocated inside it, recording both identifiers, and stating the explicit DATABASE_URL on the focused pytest command in matrix step 7.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR9-F013","note":"Verified in code: get_bundled_skills_path() returns get_install_dir()/'shared'/'skills' and get_install_dir() walks parents preferring any src/gobby/install it finds, with no override parameter or setting anywhere - so copying the skill into a temp root and 'pointing the daemon's sync at it' names no real seam, and a daemon launched from this checkout always reads the checkout. Four confirmed by direct code inspection: the get_skill tool's (name, skill_id) signature giving skill_id precedence with a name fallback at `if skill is None and name:` (F002); `should_include_bundled_file` excluding only __pycache__/.DS_Store/.pyc/.pyo with no leading-dot rule, while the committed manifest already carries two skills/impeccable/.upgrade entries the setuptools install/shared/**/* glob cannot ship (F007); `gobby sync --force` existing precisely to skip the integrity check that a mutated tree fails, under `if not dev_mode and not force` (F008); and `_serve_scan_cache` belonging to scan_served_content while scan_skill_content rescans its whole supplied surface every call (F005). Six of the nine trace to round-9 repairs. New acceptance item: 3.1.5. Ledger regenerated at 86 items. Contract validation passes (10 deliverables, 4 phases, 0 warnings); symbol validation still unavailable environment-wide from another session's in-flight gdaemon/schema work, and the one new exact-symbol target (`should_include_bundled_file`) was confirmed present at src/gobby/install/manifest.py:44 before use.

- resolution_notes: All 9 findings (8 blocking, 1 nit) verified before acceptance and repaired; the user delegated finding judgment to this session for unattended rounds. Coverage reconciled `matched` at 10 sections / 86 items with per-section counts identical to the artifact. Three confirmed by direct code inspection: get_skill.py calling resolve_session_reference (136), record_skills_used (139), and SessionVariableManager.set_variable (153) synchronously on the request path (F005); install.py:825-834 setting all_flag = True whenever no CLI selector is present, so a bare --tools would remove every hook (F006); and the plan's own recorded upstream literal `engines node >=22.18` being unrepresentable in both destination grammars (F001). Five confirmed against plan text: step 1's startup-sync opening surviving the round-10 repair that declared no startup sync happens (F002), the drift row leaving mutated bytes and a drifted revision in place for steps 2-9 (F003), trust tier absent from a generation digest that decides whether the generation may hold dependencies (F004), oversized legacy rows counted into an overflow they can never be walked out of (F007), and step 7 delegating suite discovery to a leaf that receives only section 4.1 (F009). One restraint finding accepted against my own plan (F008): with both bounded handlers on composite statements, the standalone public count and scripts-summary APIs had no consumer, so they were demoted to a private filtered-set/CTE builder and 1.2.10's symbol retargeted. Seven of the nine trace to round-9 or round-10 repairs. Repairs: the node floor gains a named authority (node_modules/impeccable/package.json engines.node), a single-representable-lower-bound parse, zero-fill normalization into each grammar, and an abort-with-judgment-needed path for unrepresentable shapes; step 1 is reordered to assert no revision exists, force-sync a baseline, then assert shape; the drift edge names its script and mutation, saves and restores the original bytes, re-verifies manifest parity, and force-syncs back to the canonical revision before step 2; is_external_source(source_type) joins the generation digest with the root marker's source kind redefined as origin provenance; the async guarantee is scoped to the whole get_skill request path with all three side-effect seams behind ctx.run_db; 2.3 gains explicit selector semantics with tools_flag in the implicit-default condition; oversized legacy rows leave total_files/remaining_file_count for a dedicated omitted_oversized_path_count so every walk terminates at zero; and step 7 carries the exact seventeen-file pytest command inline. F009's proposed enumerated ruff path list was declined in favor of the repository's own whole-tree gates `uv run ruff check src/` and `uv run mypy src/` — bounded, already the gate commands, and free of the target-coverage linter's read of mentioned paths as edit targets. No new acceptance items; the count stays 86. Contract validation passes (10 deliverables, 4 phases, 0 warnings); symbol validation still unavailable environment-wide from another session's in-flight gdaemon/schema work.

Add trusted-to-external and external-to-trusted tests with identical scripts and runtime metadata, proving the downgrade returns a fresh scripts-only generation with no node_modules or browser-ready marker. Define the root marker's write-once source kind as origin provenance rather than mutable current trust state.","location":"§2.2 Cache layout, Trust tier, and acceptance 2.2.20-2.2.21","prevention":"Enumerate every branch input that changes publication or execution policy and test byte-identical transitions across each value.","principle":"A content-addressed generation key must include every input that changes the permitted executable state of that generation.","root_cause":"The digest covers scripts and runtime metadata while source_type remains mutable and controls scanning, npm installation, browser execution, and readiness state.","section_id":"2.2","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"get-skill-all-db-work-off-event-loop","description":"get_skill can still block the daemon event loop after its composite query completes. Include tools_flag in the implicit-all condition and add tests proving --tools alone preserves installed hooks plus combination tests.","location":"§2.3 --tools wiring and acceptance 2.3.2/2.3.5","prevention":"For every new CLI selector, test it alone, with each compatible selector class, and with no selectors against both present and absent targets.","principle":"An opt-in destructive selector must participate in implicit-default resolution so its scope stays exactly what the user selected.","root_cause":"The existing uninstall command converts the absence of CLI selector flags into implicit --all; the plan covers the no-hooks early exit without specifying how tools_flag changes that branch.","section_id":"2.3","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"oversized-legacy-row-pagination-accounting","description":"A full walk can end with next_after_path null and remaining_file_count greater than zero because the remaining rows are deliberately unemit­table. The caller cannot distinguish truncation from permanently omitted legacy data.","finding_id":"PR11-F007","fix":"Restrict total_files and remaining_file_count to retrievable eligible rows. Add a separate omitted_oversized_path_count and warning for legacy exclusions, and add a legacy-row walk test asserting exact-once retrieval, monotonic remaining counts, terminal null cursor, and stable omission totals.","location":"§1.2 Manifest trim, MCP listing tool, and acceptance 1.2.5-1.2.7","prevention":"Define one eligibility predicate for rows, pages, totals, remaining counts, and cursors, then test terminal pages containing only excluded legacy rows.","principle":"Pagination counts and continuation cursors must describe the same retrievable row set.","root_cause":"Oversized legacy paths are excluded from every listing yet included in remaining overflow counts, while the API promises exhaustive traversal and a null cursor at exhaustion.","section_id":"1.2","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"over-engineering","check_key":"standalone-file-aggregate-consumer","description":"Two standalone public aggregate APIs, their contracts, and their dedicated tests add surface without a concrete plan consumer.","finding_id":"PR11-F008","fix":"Remove the standalone public count and scripts-summary APIs from the section and acceptance 1.2.8. Repaired by naming the authority (node_modules/impeccable/package.json, engines.node, inside the identity-verified staged install), specifying a single representable inclusive lower bound parsed and zero-filled into each destination's own grammar, and aborting all pin edits with a judgment-needed report for absent, malformed, or unrepresentable shapes; the fixture now uses the real two-component literal plus failure cases.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR11-F002","note":"Verified in the plan: my PR10-F008 repair added the correct statement that a non-development daemon performs no bundled startup sync while leaving step 1's opening clause - 'the isolated daemon's startup sync loads the bundled skill' followed immediately by get_skill and pagination assertions - untouched, so the row contradicted itself and asserted data with no producer. Repaired by rewriting the opening as a strict order: install, confirm manifest parity, launch, assert no bundled revision exists, force-sync to create the baseline, and only then run the shape assertions.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR11-F003","note":"Accepted: my PR10-F008 repair force-synced deliberately mutated installed bytes into the test database and left steps 2 through 9 running against that same venv and database, so every later assertion about the released 4.0.4 artifact would have been measuring corrupted bytes and a drifted revision. Repaired with the cheaper of the two offered options - restore rather than a second disposable venv and database: name the mutated script and its harmless mutation, save the original bytes, assert the drift update, write the bytes back, re-verify installed-wheel manifest parity, and force-sync once more to restore the canonical stored revision before step 2.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR11-F004","note":"Accepted and verified reachable: skill_id derives from name:project:source while source_type is a separate mutable column, so a skill can flip trusted-to-external under a stable id. With trust absent from the generation digest, byte-identical scripts and runtime metadata would hash to the existing trusted generation and hand an external skill its node_modules and browser-ready marker, silently defeating the PR9-F008 restriction. Repaired by folding normalized is_external_source(source_type) into the generation digest and per-generation provenance, defining the root marker's source kind as origin provenance rather than current trust, and adding transition cases in both directions; 2.2.20 updated.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR11-F005","note":"Verified in code: get_skill.py calls ctx.session_manager.resolve_session_reference (136), record_skills_used (139), and SessionVariableManager(ctx.db).set_variable (153) synchronously on the request path, while my 1.2 async-discipline paragraph scoped ctx.run_db to the manifest build and the listing handler - so the composite read could be perfectly correct and the handler would still block the loop. Repaired by scoping the guarantee to the whole request path, naming the three side-effect seams, requiring each behind ctx.run_db with best-effort error handling intact, and extending 1.2.9 to prove each independently with a blocking stub.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR11-F006","note":"Verified in code: install.py:825-834 sets all_flag = True whenever no CLI selector flag is present, so adding --tools without touching that condition makes 'gobby uninstall --tools' remove every detected CLI hook - a scoped destructive request silently widened, and both existing 2.3 tests would still pass. Repaired by adding a selector-semantics paragraph to 2.3: tools_flag participates in implicit-default resolution, --tools alone is managed-tool cleanup only, no flags keeps today's implicit hook --all, and --tools with CLI selectors or --all is additive, with tests for each combination.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR11-F007","note":"Verified as a direct contradiction inside my own PR9-F005 repair: oversized legacy paths were omitted from every listing yet counted into the remaining overflow, so an exhaustive walk would terminate with next_after_path null and remaining_file_count greater than zero, indistinguishable from truncation and incompatible with 1.2.7's exactly-once guarantee. Repaired by restricting total_files and remaining_file_count to retrievable eligible rows under one eligibility predicate and reporting legacy exclusions through a separate omitted_oversized_path_count with a warning; a terminal-page walk case was added and 1.2.5 updated.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR11-F008","note":"Accepted on the restraint criterion, and it is the same criterion I applied to my own repairs this round. With both bounded handlers served by composite statements, the standalone public count_skill_files and scripts-summary APIs had no consumer anywhere in the plan - only a hypothetical 'callers that need one of them alone'. Repaired by demoting the shared query logic to one private filtered-set/CTE builder backing the composite statements, leaving get_skill_files' public list contract untouched for its real existing callers; 1.2.8 rewritten and 1.2.10's symbol retargeted to SkillFilesMixin.get_skill_files. Severity nit, but a plan that ships unconsumed public storage surface violates guiding principle 12, so it was fixed rather than declined.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR11-F009","note":"Accepted: matrix step 7 said 'the suites touched across P1-P3', which an expansion leaf receiving only section 4.1 cannot resolve - it would either omit suites or fall back to the repository's prohibited full run. Repaired by enumerating the exact seventeen-file pytest command inline with GOBBY_TEST_PROTECT=1 and the recorded isolated DATABASE_URL. The finding also asked for an enumerated ruff path list; that was declined in favor of the repository's own whole-tree gates, 'uv run ruff check src/' and 'uv run mypy src/' - both bounded, both already the commands the repo gates on, and neither requiring this leaf to know which production files earlier phases touched. An enumerated src list also trips the target-coverage linter, which reads mentioned concrete paths as edit targets for the section.","vote":"accepted"}]},"session_id":"765cd308-4a93-411a-b7a5-8d8e824c604e"}

**Round 12** `kind: verification`

- reviewer_run: 45419f7b-5be4-41a5-b54d-11927048e130
- reviewer_session: dd86614f-4ffc-4bc7-958b-345322b851e4
- verdict: needs_review
- findings:
- PR12-F001 / blocking / 1.1 initial-install-file-publication-parity
- PR12-F002 / blocking / 1.1 restore-metadata-files-atomicity
- PR12-F003 / blocking / 1.1 runtime-root-shape-acceptance-coverage
- PR12-F004 / nit / 1.2 standalone-file-aggregate-target-residue
- PR12-F005 / blocking / 1.3 managed-install-fresh-vs-repair-crash-contract
- PR12-F006 / blocking / 2.2 legacy-runtime-metadata-acceptance-coverage
- PR12-F007 / blocking / 2.3 uninstall-selector-acceptance-parity
- PR12-F008 / blocking / 3.1 upgrade-preflight-before-mutation
- PR12-F009 / blocking / 3.2 vendored-lifecycle-ownership-guard
- PR12-F010 / blocking / 4.1 isolated-daemon-bootstrap-dsn
- resolution_notes: All 10 findings (9 blocking, 1 nit) verified before acceptance and repaired; the user delegated finding judgment to this session for unattended rounds. Coverage reconciled `matched` at 10 sections / 86 items before the repairs. Three were confirmed by direct code inspection. F001: the MCP install tool creates the skill row and then calls set_skill_files in a second transaction, while the REST import and hub-install handlers call create_skill and never persist parsed loaded_files at all — so hub-, ZIP-, GitHub-, and filesystem-installed script-ful skills land with zero stored files and materialize to a permanently empty inventory, contradicting the plan's own DB-backed execution promise for exactly those sources. F002: SkillMetadataMixin.restore clears deleted_at in one transaction and restores skill_files in another, and the REST restore route calls restore_skill — a method present only on the SkillManager wrapper — against a production binding that is LocalSkillManager, so that endpoint returns 500 today. F010: bootstrap loading resolves the runtime hub DSN from the Gobby home's bootstrap file and raises when absent, with no DATABASE_URL fallback on the daemon or CLI startup path, so the matrix's exported DATABASE_URL configured pytest and nothing else and the recorded schema identifier was untrue for every non-pytest process. The remaining seven were confirmed against plan text: a non-mapping `runtime` root required by the 1.1 body but absent from acceptance 1.1.2 and the parser matrix (F003); a Targets scope-reason still instructing the implementer to add the two public aggregate APIs the round-11 body had just removed (F004); acceptance 1.3.15 promising a resolvable exec target after any pre-swap crash while the body conceded a first install has no prior generation, with stage collection left undefined (F005); defensive re-validation of legacy runtime blocks required by both 1.1 and 2.2 yet stated in no 2.2 acceptance item (F006); the round-11 selector matrix living only in the 2.3 body while acceptance 2.3.2 still admitted an implementation that widens bare --tools to hook removal (F007); the 3.1 flow replacing scripts and references before the step that parses and can reject engines.node, with the abort scoped to pin fields alone (F008); and Constraints item 11's lifecycle-ownership boundary asserted nowhere, on a tree regenerated wholesale at every re-vendor (F009). Six of the ten trace to round-9 through round-11 repairs. Repairs: all three initial-install writers route through create_skill_with_files preserving files=[] versus files=None; restore becomes one transaction over both updates with a post-commit notification and the REST route is pointed at the production API; 1.1.2 and the parser matrix gain the non-mapping runtime root; 1.2's scope-reason is rewritten to the private filtered-set/CTE builder; 1.3's crash contract is stated per state (repair keeps the previous generation live, fresh install publishes nothing and reports absent) with reserved-name stages collected by the next locked run; 2.2.10 gains the defensive legacy-runtime clause and its own test; 2.3.2 enumerates bare --tools, no flags, and both additive combinations; 3.1's step 1 becomes stage-and-preflight so every authority validates before the first destination write, with rejection asserting byte-for-byte preservation of the whole tree; 3.2 gains a lifecycle-ownership bullet and 4.1's pin suite a mechanical guard over SKILL.md and the whole references tree; and 4.1 splits a base DSN from a search_path-scoped DSN written into the temporary Gobby home's bootstrap file at 0600, with current_schema asserted from both the daemon and a CLI command before the matrix runs. Two new acceptance items (1.1.12, 1.1.13) bring the count from 86 to 88; every other repair was folded into existing items. Contract validation passes (10 deliverables, 4 phases, 0 warnings); symbol validation still unavailable environment-wide from another session's in-flight gdaemon/schema work, and the validate CLI itself flaps on that same schema-identity mismatch, so validation ran through the daemon MCP route.

the rejected-engine branch aborts only pin edits.","section_id":"3.1","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"missing-requirement","check_key":"vendored-lifecycle-ownership-guard","description":"A refreshed SKILL.md or reference can reintroduce instructions that run upstream install/update against the bundled skill while all inventory, dispatch, and provenance guards still pass.","finding_id":"PR12-F009","fix":"Extend acceptance 3.2.5 or 4.1.1 with a lifecycle-ownership fixture/assertion covering SKILL.md and all vendored references: setup and maintenance route through Gobby/teach, and upstream install/update may only stage releases in the repository-only upgrade harness, never mutate the bundled skill.","location":"Constraints item 11; P3 / § 3.2; P4 / § 4.1","prevention":"Translate each decision-record prohibition into an acceptance assertion over every artifact that can instruct the agent.","principle":"A user-confirmed ownership boundary for lifecycle commands needs an independent artifact guard, especially when upstream documents are vendored and transformed.","root_cause":"The plan adapts init to teach mode and records that upstream install/update never manages the vendored skill, but no acceptance or pin-suite assertion checks SKILL.md and references for that ownership boundary.","section_id":"3.2","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"bad-sequencing","causal_finding_id":"PR10-F009","causal_section_ids":["4.1"],"check_key":"isolated-daemon-bootstrap-dsn","description":"Pytest may use the intended unique schema while the installed-wheel daemon and gobby sync fail startup or operate in the test database's public schema, invalidating the isolation and E2E evidence.","finding_id":"PR12-F010","fix":"Define a base test DSN for schema create/drop and a schema-scoped DSN with encoded search_path. The MCP install tool creates the row and then calls set_skill_files in a second transaction (install_skill.py:322), reproducing the exact split 1.1 closes for bundled create. The REST import handler and the REST hub-install handler are worse than the finding states: both call create_skill(...) and never reference parsed loaded_files at all, so a script-ful skill installed from a local path, a ZIP, GitHub, or a hub lands with zero skill_files rows and materializes to a permanently empty inventory. The plan's own DB-backed execution promise for those sources cannot hold while its install paths discard the bytes. Repaired by routing all three through create_skill_with_files with the updater's loaded-versus-unavailable distinction preserved (files=[] for a loaded-empty inventory, files=None only when none was loaded); no new seam is introduced, only three call sites moved onto the constructor 1.1 already adds. New acceptance 1.1.12 with exact-file-set and forced-failure cases at each seam.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR12-F002","note":"Verified in code, both halves. SkillMetadataMixin.restore clears deleted_at in one transaction (_metadata.py:527) and then calls restore_skill_files, which opens its own (_files.py:272), so a materializer scheduled between them resolves an active skill row over still-deleted script rows and caches a partial inventory under a digest that looks valid. Separately, runner_init/storage.py binds LocalSkillManager as the server's skill manager, while the REST restore route calls server.skill_manager.restore_skill — a method that exists only on the SkillManager wrapper those routes never traverse — so that endpoint raises AttributeError and returns 500 against the production binding today. Repaired by making restore one transaction over both updates on a single connection with a post-commit notification, pointing the route at the storage-level restore, and pinning the production manager binding in the route test. New acceptance 1.1.13.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR12-F003","note":"Verified against plan text: the 1.1 body requires runtime itself to be a mapping when present, while acceptance 1.1.2 and the parser test enumeration listed only cli shape, empty npm/version/bin, node constraint syntax, and skill_release — so an implementation could accept a non-mapping runtime root and satisfy every stated case. Repaired by adding the present-but-non-mapping runtime root to both the acceptance text and the enumerated parser matrix. No new acceptance item; the container-type branch belongs to the same validator contract 1.1.2 already covers.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR12-F004","note":"Accepted as residue from my own PR11-F008 repair: the 1.2 body demoted the count and scripts-summary logic to a private filtered-set/CTE builder, but the Targets scope-reason still instructed the implementer to add bounded count_skill_files and scripts-summary aggregate seams beside get_skill_files. An implementer reading Targets would resurrect the two unconsumed public APIs the body removed. Repaired by rewriting the scope-reason to the private-builder form the body requires, with get_skill_files' public list contract explicitly unchanged. Severity nit, and the sweep it asks for is the correct discipline after removing an API: Targets, scope reasons, acceptance text, and tests all have to move together.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR12-F005","note":"Verified as a direct self-contradiction inside my own PR9-F010/PR10-F003 repair line. Acceptance 1.3.15 asserted that death before the fsync or between the fsync and the swap always leaves a resolvable exec target, while the body's own closing sentence conceded that a first install has no prior generation — so the guarantee is unsatisfiable for fresh installs and the leaf's test would either be impossible or vacuous. The same sentence called abandoned pre-swap generations collectable without saying who collects them or how they are recognized, so repeated failures could accumulate directories indistinguishable from published ones. Repaired by stating the contract per state: a repair keeps the previous generation live through staging and fsync with only os.replace moving the pointer; a fresh install leaves no pointer, launcher, receipt, or stamp and reports the tool absent so the next run installs cleanly; and staging carries a reserved ownership-recognizable name pattern distinct from every published generation name, collected by the next run under the install lock. Body, test list, and 1.3.15 all updated together.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR12-F006","note":"Verified against plan text: 1.1 and 2.2 both require a pre-validation runtime block to be defensively re-validated at materialize time and treated as absent with a warning, the 2.2 test list names the case, and yet none of 2.2.1 through 2.2.22 stated the outcome — so a leaf could satisfy every server-derived criterion while omitting the defensive branch entirely, leaving behavior for already-stored rows undefined at close. Repaired by extending 2.2.10, which already owns prerequisite reporting and null semantics, with the defensive re-validation clause and its own cited test. Ledger count preserved at 22.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR12-F007","note":"Verified against plan text and the code it describes: my PR11-F006 repair added the full selector matrix to the 2.3 body while acceptance 2.3.2 still said only that --tools invokes the cleanup and default uninstall is unchanged — which the very implementation the finding warns about (install.py setting all_flag = True whenever no CLI selector is present, verified last round at lines 825-834) satisfies while removing every hook. A destructive selector whose semantics live only in prose is not pinned. Repaired by rewriting 2.3.2 to enumerate bare --tools, no flags, and both additive combinations with a cited matrix test. No ledger row added.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR12-F008","note":"Verified as a sequencing defect introduced by my own PR11-F001 repair. The ordered flow replaces scripts wholesale in step 2 and rewrites references in step 3, and only step 4 parses engines.node and can abort — and the abort I wrote scoped itself to the pin edits. An unrepresentable engine range therefore leaves new scripts and references paired with an old runtime declaration, installer pin, lockfile, and provenance: preserving the pin fields is not preserving a coherent release. Repaired with the cheaper of the two offered options — preflight rather than an isolated staging tree with deferred publication: step 1 becomes stage-and-preflight and validates staged identity, cli.version, dependency pins, lockfile inputs, and engine-range representability before any destination write, with mutation beginning only after it passes. The transform already stages upstream in a temp harness dir, so this is a reorder rather than new machinery. The rejected-engine test and 3.1.4 now assert byte-for-byte preservation of the whole destination tree, not just pins.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR12-F009","note":"Accepted: Constraints item 11 records the user-confirmed boundary that upstream install/update never manages the vendored skill, 3.2 routes init to teach mode, and nothing anywhere asserts it. Every existing guard — inventory, dispatch, provenance, resolver-environment — passes unchanged on a tree that has quietly reacquired upstream self-management, and the tree is regenerated wholesale at every re-vendor, so prose alone is one refresh from being undone. Repaired at the two places that matter: a lifecycle-ownership bullet in 3.2's named-defaults list stating the rule where the files are written, and a mechanical guard in 4.1's pin suite over SKILL.md and the whole vendored references tree, with upstream lifecycle commands admissible only where the text scopes them to the repository-only re-vendor harness. 4.1.1 extended rather than a new acceptance row added, because the guard runs inside the suite that item already owns.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR12-F010","note":"Verified in code, and it invalidates the isolation my PR10-F009 repair claimed. Bootstrap loading resolves the runtime hub DSN from the Gobby home's bootstrap file and raises HUB_BACKEND_DATABASE_URL_REQUIRED when it is absent; DATABASE_URL appears nowhere on the daemon or CLI startup path (only in a pipeline env passthrough and an AI tool-chat operator tuple). Exporting DATABASE_URL therefore configures pytest and nothing else: the isolated daemon and every gobby CLI invocation in the matrix would either fail to start or use whatever bootstrap file they found, and a DSN without a scoped search_path would work in the test database's public schema while the recorded schema identifier claimed otherwise. Repaired by splitting the DSNs — a base DSN for schema DDL and a search_path-scoped DSN written into the temporary Gobby home's bootstrap file at 0600 for daemon and CLI launches, the same scoped DSN passed to pytest — and by asserting current_schema from both the daemon and a CLI command equals the recorded schema before the matrix runs, so a silent fall back to public fails loudly instead of invalidating every downstream result. 4.1.2 updated.","vote":"accepted"}]},"session_id":"765cd308-4a93-411a-b7a5-8d8e824c604e"}

**Round 13** `kind: verification`

- reviewer_run: 8a09f449-5b6a-4f57-af04-e0f2e528d379
- reviewer_session: bc9764e0-9a97-4c72-9c41-8da8fa5d5ad1
- verdict: needs_review
- findings:
- PR13-F001 / blocking / 1.1 initial-install-error-classification
- PR13-F002 / blocking / 1.3 generation-activation-recovery
- PR13-F003 / blocking / 2.3 uninstall-tools-project-scope
- PR13-F004 / blocking / 4.1 pytest-base-dsn-contract
- PR13-F005 / blocking / 4.1 test-database-identity-contract
- PR13-F006 / blocking / 4.1 daemon-cli-schema-observation
- PR13-F007 / blocking / 3.1 existing-transform-target-scope
- PR13-F008 / blocking / 1.3 path-helper-ghook-caller-test
- PR13-F009 / nit / 2.3 install-cli-source-size-baseline
- PR13-F010 / blocking / 3.1 upgrade-candidate-version-authority
- resolution_notes: All 10 findings (9 blocking, 1 nit) verified before acceptance and repaired; the user delegated finding judgment to this session for unattended rounds. Seven were confirmed by direct repository inspection: the REST import handler's `except ValueError` guard logging "Skipping duplicate skill", which now swallows the validation failures my round-12 rerouting made reachable (F001); `uninstall`'s existing `--project` scope modifier, absent from the round-12 selector matrix even though every managed-tool artifact is home-global (F003); `postgres_db` building its DSN as an unconditional `base_url + "?options=-csearch_path%3D<worker-schema>"` append, so the scoped DSN I prescribed for pytest would produce two query strings and ignore the fixture's own worker schema (F004); `_dsn_identity` collapsing loopback spellings and comparing the whole normalized tuple, which my "host, port, and dbname all differ" wording contradicted for the repository's own docker test instance (F005); the absence of any `current_schema` product surface plus the CLI opening its own bootstrap-configured connection, which left the round-12 schema proof unexecutable (F006); `.upgrade/transform.py` already defining multiple functions under a bare Target (F007); `tests/cli/test_install_ghook.py` owning the direct ghook paths while my three-caller argument proof rested on a suite that patches the helper permissively (F008); and `src/gobby/cli/install.py` at 955 lines against a plan that still said 945 (F009). Two were confirmed against plan text: my round-12 reserved-stage-name repair implying a promotion rename whose crash window leaves a finished, never-activated generation the collector may not remove, with post-swap kill points missing from a contract whose test claims every point (F002); and a transform that installs the already-pinned CLI while promising to publish a new `cli.version`, leaving the production procedure unable to advance a release at all (F010). Nine of the ten trace to round-12 repairs — every finding except F007 and F010 touches text written last round. Repairs: the duplicate case gains its own narrow `ValueError` subclass so import skips only true duplicates and surfaces every other validation failure per item; 1.3 drops the promotion rename entirely, creating each generation at its final reserved-pattern name with the pointer swap as the sole activation step and a durable retained-generations record written before it, so recovery keeps the pointer target and every retained generation and removes the rest, with post-swap crashes folded into the existing full-state reuse predicate; `--tools` with `--project` becomes a `UsageError` in every combination; 4.1 gives each consumer the DSN it actually parses (base to pytest and schema DDL, scoped only into the bootstrap file), restates isolation as the fixture's normalized-tuple comparison, and proves schema placement from data the matrix already creates — the baseline sync written through the CLI, read back through the daemon, and witnessed out-of-band through the base DSN in the recorded schema and not in `public`; the transform Target becomes `::*` with a scope-reason; the ghook suite joins 1.3 Targets, 1.3.12, and the 4.1 pytest command; the ceiling baseline becomes 955/1000 with a 44-line budget; and the transform gains a required exact-semver candidate-version argument defaulting to the current pin, verified against the staged package before any destination write, from which every published pin derives. No new acceptance items; the count stays 88. Contract validation passes (10 deliverables, 4 phases, 0 warnings) and the semantic lint — target coverage included — is clean; symbol validation remains unavailable environment-wide from another session's in-flight gdaemon/schema work, and because the validate CLI flaps on that same schema-identity mismatch, validation ran through the daemon MCP route with the semantic lint invoked directly against the parser.

Routing import through create_skill_with_files therefore made invalid inventories reachable at a guard that reports them as duplicates, returning HTTP 200 with imported: 0. The MCP install tool and the hub route surface their failures, so the three writers I had just unified still disagreed on invalid input. Repaired with the smallest distinguishing mechanism: the duplicate case gets its own narrow ValueError subclass raised by the shared constructor, so every existing `except ValueError` caller keeps working, the import handler skips only true duplicates, and any other validation failure becomes a per-item structured error with a client-visible status. 1.1.12 extended; no new acceptance row.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR13-F002","note":"Accepted as a contradiction my PR12-F005 repair introduced. The body stages a generation at its final `<version>-<generation>` directory, and my repair then added reserved stage names distinct from published generation names — which only makes sense if a promotion rename exists, and that rename would open exactly the window the finding names: a finished, never-activated final generation that the collector is forbidden to touch. The finding also correctly notes that post-swap kill points between launcher, receipt, and stamp were absent from a contract whose test claims every point. Repaired by removing the naming distinction rather than adding activation provenance on top of it: there is no promotion rename, a generation is created directly at its final name, every generation name carries the reserved pattern, and the pointer swap is the sole activation step. Liveness is recorded — the outgoing pointer target is written to a durable retained-generations record before the swap, and recovery under the install lock keeps the pointer target and every retained generation and removes everything else owned. Post-swap crashes are folded into the full-state reuse predicate that 1.3.7 already owns. Body, test list, and 1.3.15 updated together.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR13-F003","note":"Verified: `uninstall` already carries a `--project` scope modifier alongside the CLI selectors, and my PR12-F007 selector matrix covered named selectors and --all while saying nothing about it.
