**Round 14** `kind: verification`

- reviewer_run: 565a128e-e172-4821-bace-9979f4766a0c
- reviewer_session: fac36499-dccf-43f5-b930-a81db2e9cb9a
- verdict: needs_review
- findings:
- PR14-F001 / blocking / 1.1 rest-import-error-classification-test
- PR14-F002 / blocking / 1.3 generation-receipt-publication-order
- PR14-F003 / blocking / 3.1 candidate-version-argument-omission-semantics
- PR14-F004 / blocking / 4.1 pytest-operator-hub-identity-source
- PR14-F005 / blocking / 1.3 managed-impeccable-win32-boundary
- PR14-F006 / blocking / 4.1 focused-pytest-owning-suite-inventory
- PR14-F007 / blocking / 1.3 path-helper-direct-test-callers
- PR14-F008 / blocking / 4.1 cold-degradation-daemon-environment
- PR14-F009 / blocking / 2.2 parent-death-subprocess-lock-ownership
- resolution_notes: All 9 blocking findings verified before acceptance and repaired; the user delegated finding judgment to this session for unattended rounds. Coverage reconciled `matched` at 10 sections / 88 items before the repairs. Five were confirmed by direct repository inspection. F004 is the sharpest: `default_bootstrap_path()` resolves through `get_gobby_home()`, which honors `GOBBY_HOME`, so running the focused suites under the matrix's temporary home makes `_live_hub_identity()` read the isolated daemon's own bootstrap, and because `_dsn_identity` ignores the schema query parameter the base DSN passed as `DATABASE_URL` normalizes to that same identity — `pytest_configure` would abort the run before collection. F005: the repository supports native Windows (the gcode, ghook, gwiki, and gdaemon installers all carry `.exe` names, msvc triples, and `sys.platform == "win32"` branches) while every primitive this plan's managed installer uses is POSIX. F007: `TestEnsurePath` calls `_ensure_gobby_bin_on_path()` with no arguments four times, so the required-parameter migration would fail the focused command on a TypeError before validating anything. F006: the exact step-7 command omitted the suite holding 1.1.12's own cited test, and `tests/utils/test_deps.py` was missing from both the command and 1.3 Targets. Four were confirmed against plan text: my round-13 repair citing only the MCP seam test for a route-level classification requirement (F001); the same repair placing a receipt write after the pointer swap while the install flow writes it into the staged tree before publication (F002); a candidate-version argument declared required and defaulted in one sentence (F003); and row 5 asserting a cold, npm-less state through client-side environment changes that cannot reach the already-running daemon which actually materializes (F008). F009 was accepted as reachable: `start_new_session=True` is what lets an npm or Puppeteer descendant outlive a killed parent while the kernel releases that process's file lock, so the next holder can delete a stage still being written. Eight of the nine landed on text written in rounds 12–13. Repairs: a named parameterized route case proves duplicate-versus-validation classification and is cited in 1.1.12; the receipt stays inside the generation with the launcher and stamp as the only post-swap surfaces; the candidate version becomes a required no-default exact-semver positional with 3.1.3 passing the pin explicitly and omitted/malformed/range/mismatch cases added; the focused pytest step runs outside the `HOME`/`GOBBY_HOME` overrides so the fixture's guard compares against the operator hub as designed; native win32 is gated rather than ported, mirroring the existing Homebrew skip, with new acceptance 1.3.16 and degraded behavior stated in 2.2 and 2.3; both missing suites join the exact command and the deps suite joins 1.3 Targets with direct probe cases; the four direct `TestEnsurePath` invocations migrate to the new signature; row 5 runs through a second isolated daemon launched without npm on its PATH; and stage collection gains a recorded owner: every stage and in-flight browser fetch records the writing child's process-group id, and a later lock holder collects or refetches only once that group has no live member. Two smaller-mechanism choices were made against the findings' proposed fixes: F004 was solved by dropping the home overrides for the pytest step rather than adding a new protected-test identity input to the shared postgres fixture, and F009 by one recorded process-group id plus a `killpg` liveness probe rather than a crash-owner watchdog with a supervisor process and control channel. One new acceptance item (1.3.16) brings the count from 88 to 89. Contract validation passes (10 deliverables, 4 phases, 0 warnings) and the semantic lint — target coverage included — is clean; symbol validation remains unavailable environment-wide from another session's in-flight gdaemon/schema work.

The crash-recovery state machine therefore lacks ownership across the exact parent-death path it claims to recover.","finding_id":"PR14-F009","fix":"Add a crash-owner watchdog used by both synchronous and asynchronous helpers. Add real installer-parent and daemon kill tests during npm and browser execution, followed immediately by competing recovery and uninstall calls that remain blocked until cleanup completes.","location":"Phase 2 / § 2.2 subprocess ownership and stale-stage recovery","prevention":"Kill the owning parent during each long-running subprocess, immediately contend for recovery and cleanup locks, and assert the contender cannot mutate state until every descendant is reaped.","principle":"Crash recovery may collect a subprocess stage only after it proves every writer to that stage and shared cache has stopped.","root_cause":"The timeout and cancellation helpers can reap children only while their parent remains alive; parent SIGKILL releases process-owned file locks while the separately-sessioned npm or Puppeteer group can continue writing.","section_id":"2.2","severity":"blocking"}],"reviewer_run":"565a128e-e172-4821-bace-9979f4766a0c","reviewer_session":"fac36499-dccf-43f5-b930-a81db2e9cb9a","round":14,"round_number":14,"verdict":"needs_review","votes":[{"finding_id":"PR14-F001","note":"Verified against plan text: my PR13-F001 repair put the duplicate-versus-validation requirement into acceptance 1.1.12 while leaving its only cited test at the MCP installer seam, and the route-suite prose I wrote covers exact file sets and forced publication failures with no case for the classification itself. An implementation could regress to the silent `imported: 0` behavior with every named test green. Repaired by adding a named parameterized route case across local, ZIP, and GitHub imports — a true duplicate is skipped and logged as one, while a malformed runtime block and an oversized path each produce a structured per-item validation error with the client-visible status — and citing it in 1.1.12 alongside the existing test.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR14-F002","note":"Verified as a contradiction my PR13-F002 repair introduced: the install flow writes the receipt into the complete staged tree before publication, matching the SRT precedent, while my rewritten crash contract listed a receipt write after the pointer swap. A generation-owned receipt cannot be independently missing post-activation without mutating an immutable generation, and an activation-owned one would need its own path, atomic replace, fsync, and recovery rules that nothing defines. Repaired by keeping the receipt inside the generation, written and fsynced before the swap, and reducing the post-swap surfaces to the launcher and the stamp in the body, the kill matrix, and 1.3.15.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR14-F003","note":"Accepted: my PR13-F010 repair called the candidate version a required argument and in the same sentence gave it a default, which are mutually exclusive — a leaf could not tell whether omission means reproduce or upgrade, and 3.1.3's invocation contract was undetermined. Repaired by making it a required no-default positional in exact MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH form, with 3.1.3 passing IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version explicitly, and by adding omitted, malformed, range-shaped, resolved-version-mismatch, and explicit-current-pin cases to the fixture and to 3.1.4. Defaulting would also have made the riskiest invocation the shortest one.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR14-F004","note":"Verified in code and it would have aborted the focused run before collection: `default_bootstrap_path()` resolves through `get_gobby_home()`, which honors GOBBY_HOME, so with the matrix's temporary home exported the pytest process's `_live_hub_identity()` reads the isolated daemon's bootstrap; `_dsn_identity` ignores the schema query parameter, so the base DSN I pass as DATABASE_URL normalizes to that same identity and `pytest_configure` raises \"points at the live Gobby hub database\". Repaired more cheaply than the finding proposed: rather than adding a new protected-test identity input to the shared fixture, the focused step simply runs without the matrix's HOME/GOBBY_HOME overrides, which is how the repository's suites already run. The operator bootstrap becomes resolvable again and the guard does what it was designed to do — prove the suites are not pointed at the operator's hub. The overrides remain where they are load-bearing, on the daemon and CLI rows.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR14-F005","note":"Verified: the repository supports native Windows — the gcode, ghook, gwiki, and gdaemon installers all carry `.exe` binary names, msvc target triples, and `sys.platform == \"win32\"` branches — while every primitive this plan's managed installer uses is POSIX: a shell launcher, a symlink pointer, extensionless `.bin` shims, and process-group signalling. The warn-only setup wiring would attempt that lifecycle on a platform where its core primitives do not exist. Repaired with a gate rather than a port, mirroring the Homebrew skip the plan already records: on native win32 setup warns and publishes nothing, diagnostics report the limitation without failing preflight or health, materialization degrades to scripts-only with no npm or browser execution, and uninstall finds every path absent. WSL keeps the POSIX path, and native support is a recorded follow-up rather than a silent gap. New acceptance 1.3.16.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR14-F006","note":"Verified: the exact step-7 command omitted `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_install_skill.py`, which is where 1.1.12's own cited test lives — the plan could have finished without ever running its own acceptance test — and `tests/utils/test_deps.py`, which owns the adjacent managed-version probes and was also absent from 1.3 Targets, leaving the new get_impeccable_version exercised only indirectly through the diagnostics report. Repaired by adding both files to the command, adding the deps suite to 1.3 Targets with a scope-reason, and requiring direct missing, valid, mismatched, configured-home, and corrupt-artifact cases for the probe.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR14-F007","note":"Verified in code: `TestEnsurePath` in tests/cli/test_install_setup.py calls `_ensure_gobby_bin_on_path()` with no arguments four times (lines 1056, 1066, 1083, 1103), covering normal insertion, repeat no-op, non-UTF-8 rc content, and unreadable rc content. My 1.3 text inventoried production callers of the retargeted helper and said nothing about its direct test callers, so the focused command would fail on a required-parameter TypeError before validating anything the deliverable adds. Repaired by requiring all four to pass the configured resolved bin directory with their existing rc-file assertions intact, in both the test prose and 1.3.12.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR14-F008","note":"Accepted: row 5 needs a fresh Gobby home and a PATH without npm, and both are read inside the process that materializes — the daemon — so no client-side change can establish them against the daemon rows 1 through 4 already launched. The row as written could not reach the cold, npm-less state it asserts. Repaired the only way the state is reachable: a second isolated daemon with its own temporary home, HOME, ports, and schema-scoped bootstrap, launched with a PATH exposing node and excluding npm, seeded with a distinct skill identity and content hash, with both daemon identities recorded and both torn down.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR14-F009","note":"Accepted and reachable: `start_new_session=True` is precisely what lets an npm or Puppeteer descendant outlive a SIGKILLed installer or daemon, and the kernel releases the dead process's file lock immediately — so the next lock holder can delete a stage another process is still writing into, or start a second Chrome fetch into the shared cache. The timeout and cancellation helpers cover only the paths where the parent survives to reap. Repaired with the least mechanism that answers the actual question \"has every writer stopped?\": each stage and each in-flight cache fetch records the writing child's process-group id, and a later lock holder collects or refetches only once that group has no live member, skipping and reporting it otherwise. One recorded id and one killpg liveness probe replace the proposed crash-owner watchdog with its supervisor process and control channel, and the rule is stated once in 1.3 and reused by 2.2 and 2.3. Acceptance 1.3.9 and 2.2.17 each gain a parent-death case.","vote":"accepted"}]},"session_id":"765cd308-4a93-411a-b7a5-8d8e824c604e"}

**Round 15** `kind: verification`

- reviewer_run: 448ba418-f029-4ff5-89b4-4bdde480d517
- reviewer_session: 8b2cb9dc-42b9-435e-885c-e431c8e0b810
- verdict: needs_review
- findings:
- PR15-F001 / blocking / 1.1 rest-import-validation-response-contract
- PR15-F002 / blocking / 1.3 generation-receipt-activation-semantics
- PR15-F003 / blocking / 1.3 subprocess-owner-record-start-barrier
- PR15-F004 / blocking / 2.3 uninstall-orphan-writer-liveness
- PR15-F005 / blocking / 2.3 win32-degraded-cache-uninstall-transition
- PR15-F006 / blocking / 4.1 protected-test-live-hub-identity-fail-closed
- PR15-F007 / blocking / 1.2 manifest-overflow-selector-identity
- PR15-F008 / blocking / 1.3 acceptance-leaf-ownership-parity
- PR15-F009 / blocking / 4.1 focused-validation-direct-suite-coverage
- resolution_notes: All 9 blocking findings verified before acceptance and repaired; the user delegated finding judgment to this session for unattended rounds. Coverage reconciled `matched` at 10 sections / 89 items before the repairs. Six were confirmed by direct repository or plan inspection. F001: the import handler returns HTTP 200 with an imported count and the stored rows and drops skipped items entirely, so my round-14 requirement for a "structured per-item validation error with a client-visible status" named a field that does not exist and left the cited route case nothing to assert. F006: `pytest_configure` returns silently whenever `_live_hub_identity()` is None or either DSN fails to parse, so the isolation proof 4.1.2 claims holds only when the operator bootstrap happens to load — removing the HOME/GOBBY_HOME overrides was necessary but not sufficient. F007: `get_skill` and `get_skill_file` both resolve `skill_id` first with a name fallback while `get_by_name` prefers a project row over a same-named global one, so my name-only `get_skill_files` continuation could paginate a different skill than the manifest it continues. F009: all four named suites exist and own directly affected seams — get_skill's serve-scan gate, the install-tool scan gate, skills-registry construction, and the uninstall command's selector cases — each able to fail while the prescribed command stayed green. F002 and F005 are contradictions my own round-14 repairs introduced: a fresh-install crash contract claiming "no receipt" one sentence before stating that death leaves the staged tree that now contains it, and a win32 uninstall claiming nothing is removed when 2.2 already states that materialization writes a scripts-only cache on that platform. F008 was accepted on scope: 1.3.16 asserted materialization and uninstall behavior whose production surfaces first appear in downstream 2.2 and 2.3. F003 and F004 are the invited challenge against my round-14 process-group decline, and both land: the parent cannot learn the group id before the fork, so a kill in the spawn-to-record window leaves a live unrecorded writer; and 2.3 synchronized deletion through locks and markers alone, never consulting the ownership records 1.3 and 2.2 write, which is exactly the case those records exist for. Repairs: the bulk-import response gains one additive `errors` array with stable `duplicate`/`validation_error` codes, their numeric per-item statuses, and a `detail` naming the offending key, asserted as a whole payload across all-success, duplicate-only, validation-only, and mixed batches; the fresh-install crash contract is restated by reachability with the unactivated generation collected by the existing recovery rule; the owner record gains a start barrier (one inherited synchronization descriptor the child blocks on until the record is fsynced, closed by parent death so the child exits unwritten) and the group leader's start identity so a reused group number is collected rather than skipped forever; uninstall reads those same records under its held locks and removes a root only once every recorded group beneath it is gone; the win32 gate is split along leaf ownership into new 2.2.23 (scripts-only materialization) and new 2.3.6 (removing what it leaves behind) with 1.3.16 reduced to setup and diagnostics; a matrix-local preflight makes the isolation proof fail closed on missing, malformed, unparseable, or equal identities without adding any input to the shared postgres fixture; `get_skill_files` gains `skill_id`/`name` resolved by the existing composite seam's CTE with the resolved id echoed in the overflow guidance; and the four missing suites join their owning sections' Targets and the exact focused command. Two smaller-mechanism choices were made against the findings' proposed fixes: F003's watchdog with a supervisor process and control channel was replaced by one barrier descriptor plus one recorded start identity, which the finding itself admits as an equivalently strong alternative; and F006's guard was made fail-closed matrix-locally rather than by enlarging the shared fixture's API. Two new acceptance items bring the count from 89 to 91 (2.2 to 23, 2.3 to 6). Contract validation passes (10 deliverables, 4 phases, 0 warnings) and the semantic lint — target coverage included — is clean; symbol validation remains unavailable for this plan's targets.

The spawn-to-record window is real: the parent cannot learn the group id before the fork, so a parent killed after spawn and before the record's fsync leaves a live unrecorded writer that a later collector deletes underneath. The reuse hazard is real but strictly narrower than the finding implies — POSIX withholds a process-group number while any member lives, so reuse implies the whole group already exited, and the failure mode is a stage leaked forever rather than a stage deleted under a writer. I repaired both without adopting the proposed watchdog, which the finding itself offers as an alternative to an equivalently strong start barrier plus non-reusable incarnation identity. The barrier is one inherited synchronization descriptor: the child blocks on it until the parent has fsynced the owner record, and parent death closes it, so the child exits at the barrier having written nothing — the window closes without a supervisor process or control channel. The incarnation identity is one extra recorded field, the group leader's process-start identity, so a probe that finds the leader alive under a different start identity treats the record as dead and collects. Both 1.3.9 and 2.2.17 gained the two cases.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR15-F004","note":"Verified against the section text: 2.3 synchronizes deletion with producers entirely through locks and owner markers, and never consults the recorded writer ownership my round-14 repair added to 1.3 and 2.2. That is exactly the case the record exists for — a killed installer or daemon has its file locks released by the kernel while its detached npm or browser descendants keep writing — so uninstall could acquire every lock uncontended and tombstone a root still being written into. Repaired by restating the rule at this leaf rather than duplicating the mechanism: under the held locks, uninstall reads the same records through the same shared module and under the same start-identity rule, skips and reports any root with a live recorded writer, and removes only once every recorded group beneath it is gone. 2.3.1 carries the normative clause and 2.3.4 gains the two immediate-uninstall-after-SIGKILL cases.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR15-F005","note":"Verified: 2.2's Gates paragraph already states that on native win32 materialization still writes the cache tree and returns it scripts-only, so my round-14 win32 repair's claim that uninstall 'finds every path absent and reports nothing removed' is false after any materialization on that platform — and 2.3's prose repeated the same false premise. Repaired by making the win32 home a partial case rather than an empty one: managed-tool paths absent, the impeccable-owned scripts-only materialization cache present and removed, with a never-materialized home reporting nothing removed. That behavior is pinned by the new 2.3.6 rather than by 1.3's setup item.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR15-F006","note":"Verified in the fixture and it is a genuine fail-open: `pytest_configure` returns silently when `_live_hub_identity()` is None (a BootstrapConfigError on load, or no configured DSN) and compares nothing when `_dsn_identity` cannot parse either side, so the isolation proof 4.1.2 claims holds only when the operator's bootstrap happens to load. My round-14 fix removed the HOME/GOBBY_HOME overrides to make that bootstrap resolvable, which is necessary but not sufficient. Repaired with the finding's own smaller option: a matrix-local preflight that resolves both DSNs, normalizes both to (host, port, dbname), and aborts unless both parse and differ — missing, malformed, unparseable, and equal identities all abort. No input is added to the shared fixture, which keeps behaving exactly as it does today.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR15-F007","note":"Verified in code: `get_skill` and `get_skill_file` both resolve `skill_id` first and fall back to `name`, and `get_by_name` resolves a project row ahead of a same-named global one. My new `get_skill_files` tool took `name` only, so an agent that hit the manifest cap on a skill it had resolved by explicit global id would paginate the project skill's inventory instead — the continuation surface silently changing which resource it continues. Repaired by giving the tool optional `skill_id` and optional `name` resolved by the same rule, reusing the resolving CTE 1.2.11 already defines rather than adding a second resolver, echoing the resolved id in the manifest's overflow guidance, and adding a project-shadows-global selector case plus a second test reference on 1.2.6.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR15-F008","note":"Accepted: my round-14 acceptance 1.3.16 asserted materialization and uninstall behavior, but both production surfaces first appear in 2.2 and 2.3, which depend on 1.3 — the 1.3 leaf could neither implement nor verify them, while the ledger mapped the whole item to that leaf. Repaired by splitting along ownership: 1.3.16 keeps setup, diagnostics, and the WSL statement; new 2.2.23 owns the scripts-only degraded materialization; new 2.3.6 owns removing what that materialization leaves behind. This also resolves F005's contradiction at its source, since the uninstall claim now lives with the leaf that can test it. Acceptance count 89 to 91 (2.2 to 23, 2.3 to 6) and the ledger regenerated.","vote":"accepted"},{"finding_id":"PR15-F009","note":"Verified by inspecting all four suites: `test_get_skill_serve_scan.py` owns get_skill's serve-scan gate (fail-closed scanner-absent, cache-hit, external-refusal cases), `test_install_skill_scanning.py` owns the install-tool scan gate whose per-item failure classification 1.1 changes, `test_registry.py` owns skills-registry construction that 2.2's new tool registration extends, and `test_install_coverage.py` owns the uninstall command's existing selector cases that 2.3's new flag default-resolution changes. Each could fail while the prescribed command stayed green. Repaired by adding each to its owning section's Targets with a scope-reason and all four to the exact focused command.","vote":"accepted"}]},"session_id":"765cd308-4a93-411a-b7a5-8d8e824c604e"}

```yaml
    \ complement filter, and with the preserved `exclude_license` flag, and supports\
    \ keyset pagination via `after_path`/`limit` applied as an ordered server-side\
    \ LIMIT. symbol: `SkillFilesMixin.get_skill_files`.\n1.2.2: The MCP get_skill\
    \ manifest drops `content_hash`, lists non-script files under a fixed sorted cap\
    \ with total and overflow counts, and collapses `scripts/**` to a capped per-top-level-dir\
    \ summary with `remaining_directory_count`/`remaining_file_count` overflow fields\
    \ and an execution note. symbol: `get_skill`. file: `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/get_skill.py`.\n\
    1.2.3: The skill files REST route accepts `path_prefix`. symbol: `list_skill_files`.\n\
    1.2.4: Filtering and manifest shape are pinned by tests. test: `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_files.py::test_get_skill_files_path_prefix`.\n\
    1.2.5: The whole manifest stays bounded against a synthetic 190-file tree, an\
    \ adversarial one-unique-directory-per-script shape, and a large long-named non-script\
    \ tree: capped listings plus overflow counts only, under the exact `MAX_MANIFEST_RESPONSE_BYTES\
    \ = 16384` serialized ceiling, with `MAX_SKILL_FILE_PATH_BYTES = 1024` enforced\
    \ at the write seam and oversized legacy paths omitted from listings and from\
    \ `total_files`/`remaining_file_count`, reported only through `omitted_oversized_path_count`,\
    \ so an exhaustive walk always terminates with a null cursor and zero remaining.\
    \ test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_manifest_bounds_scripts_summary`.\n\
    1.2.6: A bounded `get_skill_files` MCP tool is registered on gobby-skills with\
    \ `skill_id`/`name` resolved by the same ID-first / name-fallback rule as `get_skill`,\
    \ `path_prefix`/`file_type`/`after_path` arguments, and a capped server-side page\
    \ plus overflow counts and a `next_after_path` continuation, so manifest overflow\
    \ is discoverable and exhaustively walkable from the MCP surface itself and a\
    \ continuation never paginates a different skill than the manifest it continues.\
    \ test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_get_skill_files_tool_bounded_page`.\
    \ test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_get_skill_files_tool_resolves_same_skill_as_manifest`.\n\
    1.2.7: Same-prefix listings larger than the page cap are retrieved exactly once\
    \ per entry by walking `after_path`, with correct remaining counts at every step.\
    \ test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_get_skill_files_tool_paginates_same_prefix`.\n\
    1.2.8: One private filtered-set builder renders the shared predicate \u2014 `path_prefix`,\
    \ `file_type`, `exclude_file_type`, `exclude_license`, and `after_path` \u2014\
    \ once, and backs every composite statement, so a page's remaining count is counted\
    \ beyond the cursor rather than inferred and no unconsumed public count or summary\
    \ API is added; `get_skill_files`' list contract is unchanged for existing callers,\
    \ and the manifest summary and the listing tool's `total_files` come from the\
    \ same statement that produced their page, with no full-row materialization. file:\
    \ `src/gobby/storage/skills/_files.py`. test: `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_files.py::test_count_and_summary_aggregates`.\n\
    1.2.10: Every listing, count, summary, page, and composite seam applies the identical\
    \ complete predicate including `exclude_license`, preserving the legacy `True`\
    \ default and the updater's explicit `False`, so pages never disagree with their\

    \ test: `tests/storage/skills/test_skill_files.py::test_exclude_license_parity_across_seams`.\n\
    1.2.11: `get_skill` derives its skill row and its bounded manifest from one composite\

    \ rules on the name branch \u2014 so an atomic 1.1 revision publication yields\
    \ wholly-old or wholly-new metadata-plus-manifest, never a mixed pair, and no\
    \ call resolves a different skill than it does today. file: `src/gobby/storage/skills/_files.py`.\
    \ test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_get_skill.py::test_get_skill_reads_one_revision`.\n\
    1.2.9: Both MCP handlers run every storage call on the request path through `ctx.run_db`\
    \ off the event loop \u2014 the composite query and `get_skill`'s session resolution,\

- title: Install the pinned impeccable CLI as a Gobby-managed tool
  category: code
  task_type: feature
  depends_on: []
  validation_criteria: "1.3.1: A pinned `impeccable@3.5.0` install lands under `~/.gobby/tools/impeccable/3.5.0/`\
    \ with staging, locking, post-verify, receipt, and atomic promote. file: `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py`.\n\
    1.3.2: Daemon setup wires the installer warn-only in the non-Homebrew branch,\
    \ and repeat runs no-op only after verifying the complete installed state (receipt,\
    \ package identity, launcher, stamp). symbol: `run_daemon_setup`.\n1.3.3: Dependency\
    \ diagnostics report impeccable as an optional managed tool with repair guidance,\
    \ and a missing impeccable never produces an install-preflight error. symbol:\
    \ `collect_dependency_report`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_preflight_succeeds_without_impeccable`.\n\
    1.3.4: `gobby status` renders the managed impeccable dependency in the optional\
    \ section through the shared labels map and managed-name policy. symbol: `format_status_message`.\
    \ test: `tests/utils/test_status.py::TestStatusUtils::test_format_status_message_deps`.\n\
    1.3.5: Installer behavior is pinned by tests. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_second_install_is_noop`.\n\
    1.3.6: Existing daemon-setup caller tests mock and assert the impeccable installer\
    \ across the success, warn-only failure, and Homebrew-skip branches. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup.py::test_run_daemon_setup_homebrew_skips_impeccable`.\n\
    1.3.7: Partial corruption of any installed artifact \u2014 including an independently\
    \ missing, wrong-file, wrong-target, or installation-escaping `node_modules/.bin/impeccable`\
    \ exec target or `node_modules/.bin/puppeteer` locked fetch executable, and a\
    \ puppeteer package version diverging from the locked graph \u2014 and a previously\
    \ failed Chrome fetch are repaired or retried by a later `gobby install`; reuse\
    \ verifies executable identity and containment, never presence alone. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_repairs_partial_corruption`.\n\
    1.3.8: The install is lockfile-reproducible with lifecycle scripts suppressed:\
    \ the vendored install lockfile verifies against `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.lockfile_sha256`,\
    \ the invocation is `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, and the explicit locked fetch \u2014\
    \ the installation's own `node_modules/.bin/puppeteer`, with a missing executable\
    \ warn-skipping rather than falling back to `npx` or the registry \u2014 is the\
    \ only Chrome producer. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_install_uses_locked_ci_without_scripts`.\n\
    1.3.9: A timed-out installer subprocess is terminated and reaped as a whole process\
    \ group \u2014 including npm-spawned descendants \u2014 before staging rollback\
    \ or lock release; and when the installer itself is killed mid-`npm ci`, its orphaned\
    \ group keeps the stage's recorded owner alive, so a competing collector skips\
    \ that stage and reports it rather than deleting a tree still being written, collecting\
    \ it only after the group exits; the owner record is published before the child\
    \ can write \u2014 a parent killed in the spawn-to-record window leaves the child\
    \ blocked at its inherited barrier, exiting unwritten \u2014 and it carries the\
    \ group leader's start identity, so a reused group number is collected rather\
    \ than skipped forever. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_timeout_reaps_process_group`.\
    \ test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_orphaned_group_blocks_stage_collection`.\
    \ test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_owner_record_precedes_child_writes`.\n\
    1.3.10: The installer rejects Node below `IMPECCABLE_NODE_MIN_VERSION` (22.18.0)\
    \ before staging with zero artifact publication, accepts exactly 22.18.0, repairs\
    \ after a Node upgrade, and rejects reuse after a post-install downgrade below\
    \ the floor without mutating installed artifacts. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_node_floor_gate`.\n\
    1.3.11: Every managed-tool path this deliverable publishes \u2014 tool root, launcher,\
    \ stamp, puppeteer cache, PATH bin entry, and diagnostics \u2014 derives from\
    \ one `get_gobby_home()` value; with `GOBBY_HOME` overridden they all resolve\
    \ under the configured home and never touch the literal user-home dot-directory.\
    \ symbol: `_ensure_gobby_bin_on_path`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_gobby_home_override_consistency`.\n\
    1.3.12: The retargeted PATH helper takes the resolved bin directory, and its three\
    \ existing production callers pass their own \u2014 asserted by value in each\
    \ caller's own owning suite, including the direct ghook install path, so no caller\
    \ is left invoking the old zero-argument form or covered only by a permissive\
    \ mock; the helper's four direct `TestEnsurePath` invocations are migrated to\
    \ the new signature with their existing rc-file assertions intact. symbol: `install_ghook`.\
    \ test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup.py::test_binary_installers_pass_resolved_bin_dir`.\
    \ test: `tests/cli/test_install_ghook.py::test_install_ghook_passes_resolved_bin_dir`.\n\
    1.3.13: The vendored managed-CLI install lockfile is shippable and resolvable\
    \ from an installed distribution: its package-relative path matches a declared\
    \ `gobby` package-data pattern, and the installer opens and checksum-validates\
    \ it through that package-relative path against a layout containing only package-data-matched\
    \ files. file: `pyproject.toml`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_packaged_lockfile_is_shippable_and_resolvable`.\n\
    1.3.14: The shared browser-cache contract \u2014 the puppeteer-cache lock identity\
    \ and the compatibility-keyed readiness schema keyed by platform, producing Puppeteer\
    \ version, expected browser build, and channel \u2014 is created in this deliverable,\
    \ and the installer's Chrome fetch writes, reuses, and refetches against it under\
    \ that lock. file: `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_chrome_fetch_uses_compatibility_keyed_readiness`.\n\
    1.3.15: The managed install publishes as an immutable generation behind a stable\
    \ version pointer swapped by one atomic replace, with a crash contract stated\
    \ per state: injected process death before the fsync or between the fsync and\
    \ the swap leaves a repair's previous generation live and resolvable \u2014 a\
    \ concurrent launcher loop never observing a missing exec target \u2014 while\
    \ a fresh install is left with nothing resolvable through the stable version path\
    \ \u2014 no pointer, launcher, receipt, or stamp \u2014 the tool reported absent,\
    \ and the unactivated generation that remains on disk with its generation-scoped\
    \ receipt collected by the next locked run, so the next run installs cleanly;\
    \ recovery under the install lock preserves the current pointer target and every\
    \ generation named in the durable retained-generations record written before the\
    \ swap and removes every other owned generation, so repeated crashes accumulate\
    \ nothing and nothing still resolvable is collected; the receipt is written inside\
    \ the generation before the swap while the launcher and stamp are the only post-swap\
    \ surfaces, and a crash between those two writes leaves the install readable as\
    \ incomplete and repaired by the next run; and a superseded generation survives\
    \ until `gobby uninstall --tools` so an in-flight process can still complete a\
    \ deferred module load from it. file: `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py`.\
    \ test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_publication_survives_kill_at_every_point`.\n\
    1.3.16: The managed runtime is gated on native win32 rather than attempting POSIX\
    \ primitives that do not exist there: setup warns and publishes no tool root,\
    \ launcher, stamp, or receipt; diagnostics report the platform limitation without\
    \ producing an install-preflight error or an unhealthy state; WSL takes the ordinary\
    \ POSIX path. The downstream behavior this gate implies belongs to the leaves\
    \ that implement it \u2014 scripts-only materialization to 2.2.23 and cleanup\
    \ of what that materialization leaves behind to 2.3.6 \u2014 because neither production\
    \ surface exists in this deliverable. symbol: `impeccable_dependency_status`.\
    \ test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_native_win32_is_gated_and_reported`."
  labels:

- title: Add the materialize_skill_scripts MCP tool
  category: code
  task_type: feature
  depends_on:
  - '1.1'
  - '1.2'
  - '1.3'
  - '2.1'
  validation_criteria: "2.2.1: The tool is registered on gobby-skills and returns\
    \ the materialized scripts directory with independent `parser_deps`, `browser`,\
    \ and `node` prerequisite states. symbol: `create_skills_registry`.\n2.2.2: Scripts\
    \ materialize from `skill_files` bytes into the content-addressed cache with atomic\
    \ writes and self-invalidation; previously returned generations are never pruned\
    \ by materialization. file: `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/materialize_skill_scripts.py`.\n\
    2.2.3: Missing npm degrades to a warning with a usable cache dir. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_degrades_without_npm`.\n\
    2.2.4: Unsafe file paths are rejected before any write. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_rejects_unsafe_paths`.\n\
    2.2.5: Materialization succeeds from `skill_files` bytes alone and never falls\
    \ back to bundled-source files. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_materializes_from_db_bytes_only`.\n\
    2.2.6: Concurrent cold materialization performs one dependency install and publishes\
    \ one directory, and the event loop stays responsive while the file lock is held\
    \ across an awaited subprocess. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_concurrent_materialize_single_install`.\n\
    2.2.7: Subprocess timeout and caller cancellation terminate and reap the whole\
    \ process group \u2014 including npm-spawned descendants \u2014 before any filesystem\
    \ recovery, leaving no live process and no post-publication writes. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_timeout_reaps_subprocess`.\n\
    2.2.8: A materialization directory returned before a newer content hash publishes\
    \ remains readable afterwards. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_old_generation_survives_new_publish`.\n\
    2.2.9: A failed dependency install \u2014 or a crash at any point before the attach\
    \ rename \u2014 leaves a usable scripts-only generation with no reachable `node_modules`-present/marker-absent\
    \ state; the next call attaches dependencies via the always-absent rename target\
    \ without mutating published script files, and a concurrently held directory stays\
    \ byte-identical. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_failed_install_retry_attaches_deps`.\n\
    2.2.10: Execution prerequisites report and repair independently, and a missing\
    \ node executable is non-fatal with defined null states; a stored `runtime` block\
    \ written before write-side validation existed is defensively re-validated here\
    \ and, when invalid, treated as absent \u2014 no version gate, `satisfies_floor:\
    \ null`, and a warning \u2014 never trusted and never fatal. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_missing_node_reports_independently`.\
    \ test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_malformed_legacy_runtime_treated_as_absent`.\n\
    2.2.11: The `<skill_id>` cache root carries a validated versioned owner marker\
    \ after first materialization, published under the namespace/discovery lock so\
    \ no root is ever observable without its marker. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_owner_marker_written`.\n\
    2.2.12: External-source materialization scans SKILL.md and every DB-loaded `scripts/**`\
    \ payload fail-closed before any cache tree is created; a safe SKILL.md with one\
    \ unsafe script refuses with zero cache publication. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_scan_gate_covers_all_script_files`.\n\
    2.2.13: The tool returns `environment.PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR` as an absolute path\
    \ so consumer processes can resolve the fetched browser. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_returns_browser_cache_environment`.\n\
    2.2.14: First-root publication is crash-atomic: a crash before the staged-root\
    \ rename leaves no visible `<skill_id>/` root, only an abandoned stage cleaned\
    \ under the namespace lock, and the next materialization publishes cleanly. test:\
    \ `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_first_root_crash_leaves_no_unmarked_root`.\n\
    2.2.15: A materializer whose local puppeteer executable is absent never invokes\
    \ `npx` or any registry fallback: `browser.ready` is false with a warning, no\
    \ browser marker is published, and a later valid local binary repairs readiness.\
    \ test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_materializer_missing_local_puppeteer_never_invokes_npx`.\n\
    2.2.16: Cancelling a waiter on any cross-process cache lock strands nothing: acquisition\
    \ is nonblocking-with-async-retry through the shared cancellation-safe wrapper,\
    \ and after cancellation a subsequent caller acquires the lock. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_lock_waiter_cancellation_leaves_lock_free`.\n\
    2.2.17: Script and dependency staging directories carry the reserved ownership-recognizable\
    \ stage names and record the writing child's process group, and stale stages are\
    \ collected under the held locks before each retry only once that group has no\
    \ live member \u2014 repeated crashes before publication or attach never accumulate\
    \ abandoned stages, and a daemon killed mid-install leaves an orphaned group whose\
    \ stage a competing collector skips and reports rather than deleting under an\
    \ active writer; the same 1.3 record rules apply here, so a materializer killed\
    \ in the spawn-to-record window leaves its child blocked at the inherited barrier\
    \ with nothing written, and a stage whose recorded group number was taken over\
    \ by an unrelated live group is collected on the start-identity mismatch rather\
    \ than skipped forever. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_repeated_crashes_leave_no_stage_accumulation`.\
    \ test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_orphaned_group_blocks_stage_collection`.\n\
    2.2.18: The materializer consumes the 1.3-defined compatibility-keyed readiness\
    \ record: it reuses a record only when platform, Puppeteer version, expected browser\
    \ build, and channel match its own verified local Puppeteer, and otherwise refetches\
    \ under the shared lock and rewrites the record for its own key \u2014 never a\
    \ `browser.ready: true` for an unlaunchable build. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_browser_readiness_is_compatibility_keyed`.\n\
    2.2.19: The resolved skill row and the complete scripts inventory are read by\
    \ one composite statement in a single `ctx.run_db` call whose resolving CTE reproduces\
    \ `get_by_name`'s exact selector semantics \u2014 project-first, global fallback,\
    \ deleted filtering, and bundled-template shadow rejection \u2014 so a concurrent\
    \ atomic revision publication yields a wholly-old or wholly-new materialization\
    \ and the tool never resolves a different skill than `get_skill` does for the\
    \ same name. file: `src/gobby/storage/skills/_files.py`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_materialization_reads_one_revision`.\n\
    2.2.20: Cache generation identity covers every field that changes what the generation\
    \ may contain or execute, the normalized trust tier included: a metadata-only\
    \ revision (`skill_release` or `runtime.node` changed, scripts byte-identical)\
    \ publishes a new generation carrying its own provenance record, a trust-tier\
    \ flip in either direction with byte-identical scripts also publishes a fresh\
    \ generation rather than inheriting the other tier's dependencies, the write-once\
    \ root marker holds only immutable ownership facts, and previously returned generations\
    \ stay held and readable. file: `src/gobby/skills/script_cache.py`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_metadata_only_revision_publishes_new_generation`.\n\
    2.2.21: External sources never execute unscanned bytes: dependency installation\
    \ and browser-binary execution are restricted to non-external sources, and a scan-clean\
    \ external skill materializes scripts-only with no npm or `.bin` invocation, false\
    \ `parser_deps` and `browser` states with restriction warnings, and a usable degraded\
    \ cache. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_external_source_never_installs_dependencies`.\n\
    2.2.22: Every synchronous phase runs off the event loop \u2014 one whole-surface\
    \ `scan_skill_content` call, cold staging, stale collection, fsync, and publication\
    \ all in workers \u2014 and a cancellation during any phase awaits its worker\
    \ to a safe boundary before releasing the held lock. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_cold_materialization_keeps_event_loop_responsive`.\n\
    2.2.23: On native win32, where 1.3 gates the managed runtime off, materialization\
    \ still writes and returns the cache tree scripts-only: no npm and no browser\
    \ process is spawned, `parser_deps.installed` and `browser.ready` are false with\
    \ warnings naming the platform limitation, and the line-based detector stays usable\
    \ \u2014 so the platform gate leaves a real cache root behind rather than nothing.\
    \ symbol: `materialize_skill_scripts`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/skills/test_materialize_skill_scripts.py::test_native_win32_materializes_scripts_only`."
  labels:
  - covers:gobby-skills-scriptful-skills:2.2:2.2.1
  - covers:gobby-skills-scriptful-skills:2.2:2.2.2
  - covers:gobby-skills-scriptful-skills:2.2:2.2.3

- title: Add managed-tool uninstall behind gobby uninstall --tools
  category: code
  task_type: feature
  depends_on:
  - '1.3'
  - '2.2'
  validation_criteria: "2.3.1: `remove_impeccable_runtime()` clears the impeccable\
    \ tool dir, launcher, stamp, and impeccable-owned materialization entries only\
    \ \u2014 other skills' materializations and the shared puppeteer cache survive\
    \ \u2014 reporting what it removed, and removing a root only once every recorded\
    \ writer group beneath it has exited. file: `src/gobby/cli/install_setup_impeccable.py`.\n\
    2.3.2: `--tools` participates in the implicit-default resolution rather than inheriting\
    \ it: bare `gobby uninstall --tools` performs managed-tool cleanup only and leaves\
    \ every installed hook in place, no flags at all keeps today's implicit hook `--all`,\
    \ and `--tools` combined with `--all` or with a named CLI selector is additive\
    \ \u2014 each combination pinned by its own case, so a scoped destructive request\
    \ can never silently widen to hook removal; and `--tools` with `--project` raises\
    \ a `UsageError` with or without `--all` or a named CLI selector, because every\
    \ managed tool artifact is home-global and cannot be project-scoped. symbol: `uninstall`.\
    \ test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_uninstall_tools_selector_matrix`.\n\
    2.3.3: `src/gobby/cli/install.py` remains under 1,000 lines. file: `src/gobby/cli/install.py`.\n\
    2.3.4: Cleanup behavior is pinned by tests: preservation of unrelated skill materializations\
    \ and the shared puppeteer cache, producer-overlap synchronization (install-vs-uninstall,\
    \ materialize-vs-uninstall including a publisher for a previously unseen content\
    \ hash serialized by the shared `<skill_id>`-root lock, empty-namespace enumeration\
    \ serialized against a cold materializer's atomic first-root publication \u2014\
    \ including under the skill-creation UUID-collision fallback id \u2014 and a deterministic\
    \ warm-waiter-versus-delete case where the waiter finds the root gone after acquiring,\
    \ releases, and restarts at namespace-then-root), marker-proven ownership (a random-UUID\
    \ root with a valid marker is removed; unmarked or malformed-marker roots are\
    \ preserved and reported), live-writer quiescence (uninstall run immediately after\
    \ an installer parent and after a materializer parent are each SIGKILLed with\
    \ their detached groups still writing skips and reports the containing root, and\
    \ removes it only once those groups exit), crash-atomic deletion (killed after\
    \ the tombstone rename but before removal completes: no unmarked live root exists,\
    \ the tombstone alone remains, and a later uninstall or the opportunistic namespace-lock\
    \ cleanup finishes removing it while producers treat the root as absent and republish\
    \ atomically), and home-override resolution (with `GOBBY_HOME` pointing at a directory\
    \ distinct from the user home, removal resolves the tool root, launcher, stamp,\
    \ and materialization entries under the configured home and leaves the literal\
    \ user-home dot-directory untouched \u2014 the uninstall half of the 1.3 lifecycle\
    \ guarantee, testable here because this deliverable introduces the removal path).\
    \ test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_remove_impeccable_runtime`.\n\
    2.3.5: On a hook-free installation, `gobby uninstall --tools` still removes the\
    \ tool artifacts instead of early-exiting to success. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_uninstall_tools_without_hooks`.\n\
    2.3.6: On native win32, `gobby uninstall --tools` removes and reports the impeccable-owned\
    \ scripts-only materialization cache that degraded materialization (2.2.23) leaves\
    \ behind, while every managed-tool path is absent; a home that never materialized\
    \ reports nothing removed. symbol: `remove_impeccable_runtime`. test: `tests/cli/test_install_setup_impeccable.py::test_native_win32_removes_degraded_cache`."
  labels:
  - covers:gobby-skills-scriptful-skills:2.3:2.3.1
  - covers:gobby-skills-scriptful-skills:2.3:2.3.2
  - covers:gobby-skills-scriptful-skills:2.3:2.3.3
  - covers:gobby-skills-scriptful-skills:2.3:2.3.4
  - covers:gobby-skills-scriptful-skills:2.3:2.3.5
  - covers:gobby-skills-scriptful-skills:2.3:2.3.6
  tdd: true
  source_section: '2.3'
  implementation_domain: backend
- title: Rewrite the .upgrade transform tooling for the release channel
  category: code
  task_type: feature
  depends_on:
  - '1.3'
  - '2.1'
  - '3.3'
  validation_criteria: "3.1.1: The transform tool implements the 4-step release-channel\
    \ flow (stage, scripts, references, pins). file: `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/.upgrade/transform.py`.\n\
    3.1.2: The README documents the flow and the judgment-needed report. file: `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/.upgrade/README.md`.\n\
    3.1.3: Invoked with `IMPECCABLE_RELEASE.version` passed explicitly as the candidate\
    \ version, so the staged release is identical to the current pin, the tool reproduces\
    \ `scripts/**` byte-identical and reports no judgment diffs beyond the adaptations\
    \ catalogued in NOTICE.md. behavior: \"upgrade flow\" in `src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/.upgrade/README.md`.\n\
    3.1.4: The transform's mechanical steps are pinned by a deterministic fixture\
    \ test, including idempotent re-execution, the complete release-pin tuple after\
    \ a simulated CLI bump, and both node-floor fields normalized into their own grammars\
    \ after a simulated engine-floor bump from the real two-component source literal,\
    \ with the candidate version supplied as a required no-default positional argument\
    \ whose omitted, malformed, range-shaped, and resolved-version-mismatch cases\
    \ are each rejected before staging, and with unrepresentable constraints rejected\
    \ in preflight before any destination write, leaving the whole vendored tree \u2014\
    \ scripts, references, SKILL.md, NOTICE.md, both lockfiles, dependency constants,\
    \ and the bundled-content manifest \u2014 byte-for-byte unchanged. test: `tests/skills/test_upgrade_transform.py::test_transform_mini_release_idempotent`.\n\
    3.1.5: The bundled-content manifest enumerates exactly the files a built wheel\
    \ ships: the generator excludes every leading-dot path component the package-data\
    \ globs also exclude, the regenerated manifest carries no `.upgrade/` entry, and\
    \ a freshly installed wheel verifies clean so production sync is not blocked on\
    \ an untampered install. symbol: `should_include_bundled_file`. test: `tests/install/test_bundled_content_manifest.py::test_manifest_membership_matches_wheel`."
  labels:

  - covers:gobby-skills-scriptful-skills:3.1:3.1.3
```
